  • Personality: Earnest, curious, kind and talkative. She picks the starter weak to the PC's "because somebody should give the underdog a chance. Also it was looking at me." Her anxiety hides under a constant stream of plant facts. She is braver than she knows.
  • Voice:
  • (Ch1) "Did you know larches are the only conifers that drop their needles? Every winter they look dead. They're not! They're just... letting go so they don't snap. Professor says that's the most sensible thing a tree's ever done."
  • (J3, as Warden) "I didn't want this seat. I wanted him in it. But the city's drowning in flowers and nobody else is coming, so. Hi. I'm the Warden of Verdance. Please don't go easy on me — I'll cry either way."
  • (J4) "We started on the same dock. Let's see who's grown more."
  • Motivation: Make the people she loves proud, and later, become someone worth the seat she inherited.
  • Arc by slot:
  • J1 (Saltmere): a cheerful spar outside the theater.
  • J2 (Mirefen): she finds Rowan's name in Corvin's ledger and refuses to believe it.
  • Ironreach: watches the Mourner's mask break.
  • J3 (Gym 9): her Warden trial and gym battle, the most emotionally loaded gym in the game.
  • Acts III–IV: an increasingly confident Warden who leads the anti-Meridian "Greenward" bloc. She carries her guilt over visiting Rowan in prison.
  • J4 (Festival R16): a joyful, bittersweet battle between equals.
  • Finale: the first Warden to release her Plate. "Verdance has never had an autumn. I'd like to see one."
  • Team concept: the starter weak to the PC's; Bounsweet → Steenee → Tsareena (grown from a seed-pod in Act I, so it rises from a sweet fruit to a queen); Whimsicott; Lilligant; Comfey (a Ch18 gift from Celestine if met earlier); Shiinotic. Ace: Tsareena.
  • Epilogue: In Homecoming, Elin wakes under Juno's first autumn.

THE THREE POWERS

Aurelian Voss — CEO of Meridian Concord (MERIDIAN_VOSS; final battle ~lv 98 on :PRISM_ARENA)

  • Age 47. Tall and silver at the temples. Bespoke charcoal suit with a single gold pin of Meridian's sunburst. He never raises his voice. Warm handshake, perfect memory for names. He drinks tea from a chipped company mug he's had since he was a linesman.
  • Public story: Born in Halden's Reach, a Bleed-adjacent Nadir mining town, to a poor branch of a Zenith founding house that lost its Council seat generations ago. He started as a Lattice linesman and built Meridian into the company that "lit Velmora". He won his family's seat back and now chairs the Zenith Council, which makes him the city-state's head of government. He still visits job sites in person. Topside loves him, and Nadir remembers he was once one of them, which is the only reason they tolerate him at all.
  • True story: The last heir of House Aurel, the line of the Gilded King. ("Aurelian" is a family name that hides in plain sight.) His family survived 300 years under the name Voss and helped found Zenith in AF 118, deliberately on top of drowned Aurelia. They passed down fragments of the Gilders' science and one belief: the god owes us. In AF 290, as Meridian's head of research, he installed the "monitoring array" in the Crownhaven Basilica, which drew on the converged Plates and cracked the Crown. He watched the Glassfall and understood what he was looking at: the power of eighteen Plates in one place.
  • Philosophy: "Gods are absentee landlords. Arceus dropped its furniture on our heads three hundred years ago and never came back to collect it. Every generation since has paid the rent in blood. I'm not trying to become a god. I'm trying to make sure we never need one again."
  • Voice:
  • (Prologue, ferry) "First crossing? Take the badge case — Meridian makes them for the League anyway. Call it a welcome gift from a fellow traveler. No, I insist. Somebody should be kind to you on a day like this."
  • (Ch5, Lumen Gala) "They'll tell you light is a gift. Light is work. Every lamp in this city was lit by someone who was told to be grateful for the dark."
  • (Ch17, throne room) "Twelve people in the Lowlight. I've lost more to the Bleed in a quiet week. I made those twelve mean something."
  • (Festival, hijack) "You carried my key through eighteen doors. Let me show you what it opens."
  • Motivation: Sincere, terrible conviction, plus inheritance. He genuinely wants to end the Bleed, end Velmora's dependence on Wardens, and give power "to those who make it". The binding of Arceus is his family's unfinished sentence, and he means to finish it.
  • Arc: Patron (Act I) → protector (the Mandate, Act II) → embattled reformer (Act III) → revealed heir (Act IV) → hijacker (Act V).
  • Fate: Arceus's Judgment strikes him when he tries to command it. If PC_SPARED_VOSS is set (the PC chooses to shield him with the shard in the final moment), he survives, crippled and imprisoned. He has one epilogue scene: he asks to see the sunrise. If not, he is Fixed into light at the center of the arena.
  • Team concept: "Things men made." Silvally (ace; Meridian's "Project NULL", a replica of what the PC is. On the Prism Arena its type follows the active field via Meridian memory discs). Porygon-Z, Golurk (an Aurel family automaton dredged from Aurelia), Duraludon, Magnezone, Klinklang.

Valen Corrigan — Sovereign of the Accord (CHAMPION_VALEN; Festival final ~lv 96)

  • Age 58. Broad-shouldered, close-cropped white hair, a soldier's posture. He wears white Sovereign's robes and always one grey glove on his left hand. Under it, the arm is crystal to the shoulder.
  • History: Won the AF 270 Festival at 28 and defended it in AF 280 (against Brann) and AF 290. He was briefly married to Lucent scholar Edith Marr. Their daughter Ilsa took her mother's name after he chose the Seat over them.
  • What he knows: Everything. The Tuning. The Crown. The depleting shard. The Quiet Towns sealed off the maps. Meridian's hand in AF 290 he only suspects, because he never let himself investigate. He scapegoated Morwen Vane so the world wouldn't learn the ceremony could fail, and has spent ten years searching for "the child". Since the Fracture, every Tuning has cost him far more. He will not survive another.
  • Personality: Grave, gentle, exhausted, and capable of terrible decisions made calmly. He loves Velmora the way a man loves a house he's holding up with his back. He doesn't defend his lies; he explains them.
  • Voice:
  • (Ch5, after the Shatter) "You were in the Lowlight when it went? And you're... standing." (He looks at them a beat too long.)
  • (Ch9) "Come to Verdance. There's a seat to fill, and I think you should see what it costs."
  • (Ch20, audience) "Every ten years I bring the Plates home to Crownhaven, and every ten years Velmora endures. You noticed I never said 'thrives'. Nobody ever notices."
  • (Ch21, holding the ring) "Go. Free it. I'll hold the door — I've had thirty years of practice."
  • Arc: A remote authority (Acts I–II), then a judge (Act III), then exposed as a liar (Act IV), and finally a confessor and sacrifice (Act V). He dies holding the Concordance Ring during the Arceus battle. If ILSA_RECONCILED, Ilsa is at his side and he calls her "little owl", a name the player heard in her Mindscape.
  • Team concept: Zygarde (ace; the Order Pokémon. Lore: Zygarde cells threaded through Velmora's crust have been helping hold the land together, and Zygarde chose Valen in AF 270. "Order, not justice. I chose order."); Bronzong (the Crownhaven bells); Gallade; Lapras (the ferry motif: "she carried me out of a flood once"); Volcarona; Kingdra.

The Mourner / Rowan Ashby — leader of the Hollow (HOLLOW_MOURNER); former Grass Warden of Verdance

  • Age 52. Rangy and weathered, with soil permanently under his nails and a soft burr of a voice. As the Mourner he wears a grey cowl and a carved ash-bark mask with one white lily. A Chimecho-resonance plate inside the mask doubles his voice.
  • Background: Grass Warden for 24 years. His wife Elin (Juno's aunt) was Fixed in AF 288 while tending the Heartgarden. The Bleed turned her into a flowering tree that has never stopped blooming. Verdancers call it "the Bride Tree", and Rowan waters it every dawn. He attended three Tunings and asked Valen what they really did. Valen told him to tend his garden.
  • Why him (full justification in 02_FACTIONS):
  • He is the man who loves the land most, and so the one who sees the Imprint as a sickness.
  • He knows the Accord from the inside.
  • His unmasking is what makes the SPINE-locked Juno succession happen.
  • It turns the friendly rival's story into the emotional core of Act III.
  • Personality: Gentle, patient, funny with children, devastating when grieving. As the Mourner he is theatrical and liturgical, because grief needs ritual.
  • Voice:
  • (Rowan, Ch1) "Everything that grows must be allowed to die, Juno. That's not sad. That's what makes room."
  • (The Mourner, Ch3, Saltmere stage) "Three hundred years of pageantry. Ask your Warden why nothing in Verdance ever rots. Ask your Sovereign why the old capital is underwater. Let the land grieve."
  • (The Mourner, Ch5, holding the cracked Zap Plate, hand burning) "No — no — what did you do?" (He is speaking to Sable. The player will assume he means the PC or himself.)
  • (Unmasked, Ch9) "Juno. Don't look at me like that. Look at the flowers. Look at what they're standing on."
  • Arc: Gentle uncle (Act I) → masked zealot (Acts I–II, with fair clues) → unmasked and imprisoned (end of Act II) → tribunal (Ch13), where the PC's testimony matters → escapes in the Fault and leads the reformed "Lantern Hollow" as uneasy allies (Act IV) → in disguise at the Festival → in Homecoming, he is under the Bride Tree when Elin wakes.
  • Team concept (as the Mourner): Trevenant (ace; as Rowan in Ch1 he shows Juno and the PC a sickly Phantump he rescued from the Glasswood, which is a fair clue); Shiftry; Cacturne; Gourgeist; Abomasnow; Breloom. As Warden, his public team was gentler: Leavanny, Roserade, Sunflora.

MENTORS & ALLIES

Professor Imelda Larch — Tidewatch Station, Ferrow's Landing

  • Age 56. Wiry, grey braid, oilskin coat, reading glasses on a chain. She constantly drinks a bitter kelp tea nobody else can stand.
  • Secret: She was Meridian's Chief Engineer and invented the Lattice. She left in AF 292 when her models showed the drain was accelerating the Bleed, and Voss buried the report. She was Ines's friend and knew the child was "hurt at the Fracture" and "different", but not about the Legend Plate.
  • Why she has every starter: Tidewatch's Wayfarer Sanctuary studies how foreign Pokémon resist Imprinting. For twenty years professors abroad sent her starter-line Pokémon, which explains the Reborn-style any-starter choice.
  • Voice: "I lit up this whole region, you know. Eleven cities on my design. And I think I'm killing it. Drink your tea." / "Science is just refusing to be lied to by things that can't talk."
  • Arc: Mentor (Act I) → investigator (Act II) → publishes the Larch Papers and is framed (Ch12) → hides in Cragholm and explains the badge-case telemetry (Ch14) → at the Festival she reads the Engine's live output (Act V).

Captain Edda Morrow — the ferry Dovetail

  • Age 64. Squat and weathered, with a gold tooth and a Lapras tattoo. Gruff with a hidden tenderness.
  • Secret: In AF 290 she ran the night ferry that smuggled Ines and the five-year-old PC out of Velmora on Morwen Vane's coin. Bringing them back on the last ferry is a coincidence that she doesn't believe is one.
  • Voice: (Prologue) "Don't look down at the strait at night, sprog. The old city's lamps still burn under there." / (Ch1) "You stand at the rail like somebody I used to know." / (Ch15) "I took you out on the night ferry. Brought you back on the last one. Tide's got a sense of humour."
  • Role: After the quarantine impounds the Dovetail, she turns smuggler. She runs the PC to the outer islands and to Underveil, and evacuates Crownhaven in the finale.

Brother Wick — Hollow theologian (HOLLOW_WICK)

  • Age 70. A defrocked Prism Choir priest, blind in one eye, gentle, who quotes scripture he was excommunicated for reading. He co-founded the Hollow with Rowan and is the source of the "Return" doctrine.
  • Voice: "The Choir teaches that the Plates were a gift. Child, nobody bleeds for three hundred years from a gift."
  • Role: Hollow admin (Ch7). Later he becomes the bridge to Celestine's Hymn of Return.

Calla Wren — Voss's executive assistant

  • Age 26. Immaculate and polite. She runs the scanner at the dock in the Prologue.
  • Arc: She becomes increasingly uneasy. She leaks Voss's itinerary to Nyx (Ch17) and smuggles Larch the arena's power schematics in Act V. She is a quiet defector, never a fighter.

THE EIGHTEEN WARDENS

Format: look/age · personality · voice · motivation · secret · plot role · arc & reappearances · ace concept.

1. Tamsin Loom — Bug — Briarwick — LEADER_TAMSIN — :SILKWOOD

  • 19. Ink-stained fingers, a measuring tape around her neck, and a moth-wing shawl woven by her late grandmother. She became Warden last spring when Old Mother Loom died.
  • Personality: An anxious perfectionist who apologizes constantly. She thinks in thread and turns ferocious the moment her people are threatened.
  • Voice: "Sorry — sorry — the Silkwood's a bit tangled today. Like me." / "A thread holds because it's twisted with others. Alone it's just... string." / (battle start) "Granny Loom wove this room. Don't you dare tear it."
  • Motivation: Keep Briarwick's looms running and live up to a legend.
  • Secret: Briarwick is bankrupt. She secretly signed a Meridian Lattice contract alone, and the Lattice is pushing the Glasswood (a crystallizing Bleed) toward the silk-moth groves.
  • Plot role: The PC's first look at Meridian's double edge, and the first Hollow sabotage.
  • Arc: Confesses after the gym and either tears up the contract publicly (TAMSIN_PUBLIC_CONFESSION) or renegotiates it quietly. Reappears in Ch10 at the Verdance succession as the first small-town Warden to join Juno's Greenward bloc, and again in Act V weaving the Festival's eighteen banners. During the hijack, her Galvantula nets catch falling arena debris.
  • Ace: Leavanny line (the tailor, Swadloon at Gym 1); later Galvantula, Masquerain.

2. Dorian Pell — Normal — Saltmere — LEADER_DORIAN — :GRAND_STAGE

  • 56. Former matinee idol in a velvet coat with a silver-topped cane he doesn't need. His laugh fills rooms on purpose.
  • Personality: Flamboyant, vain, and generous. He speaks in stage directions ("Enter, stage left, our brave challenger!") and is a devoted father-figure to his troupe.
  • Voice: "Saltmere is the only city in Velmora where nothing is a miracle, darling. So we built our own." / "Every pageant is a lie. The trick is choosing which lie to tell." / (Act V) "Tonight, for one night only, the truth. Lights, please."
  • Motivation: Keep the stage alive in a city with nothing else.
  • Secret: As the Accord's Pageant-Master he stages the official Pageant of the Prismfall and knows it's propaganda. He owns the last copy of "The Gilded King", a banned play by Ansel Crewe, a playwright executed in AF 52, that tells the true story.
  • Plot role: The Mourner hijacks his pageant (Ch3). He slips the PC the play's prologue page, which is the first written clue. Saltmere is Elowen's free port, chartered to answer to no king, and Dorian guards that charter jealously against Queen Maelis's tax-collectors.
  • Arc: In Act III Elowen's crown shuts his Lantern Theatre at Meridian's request for a satire about Meridian. His troupe also gives the PC the gift Eevee "Understudy" in Ch3. On the Festival's Night of Masques (Act V) he stages "The Gilded King" in Crownhaven in front of Voss, which turns public opinion. He casts Celestine, who cannot lie, as the voice of Princess Maren's lament.
  • Ace: Kecleon (the quick-change artist; Color Change is a costume change); also Ambipom, Chatot (the voice), Furfrou, and later Exploud.

3. Marisol Reyes — Water — Ebbmouth — LEADER_MARISOL — :WATER_SURFACE

  • 34. Broad-shouldered dockworker in rolled sleeves and a union pin, with a laugh like a foghorn. Ebbmouth is Isenmar's southern port and the only city whose Warden is elected (by the Ebb Guild). The Guild has fought Thane Eirik's harbor taxes for a generation.
  • Personality: Brash, warm and profane ("tides take it"). She would die for her crews.
  • Voice: "The Ebb runs both ways. Always has. Lately it can't decide." / "Meridian pays my people. Meridian's killing my river. Pick one, kid, I dare you." / (Ch9 or Ch13, if Teo died) "He was nineteen. Nineteen. Tell me again how the Hollow are the monsters."
  • Motivation: Jobs versus the river: she needs both.
  • Secret: Her little brother Teo runs Ebbmouth's Hollow cell. She has been tipping him off about Meridian patrols.
  • Plot role: K2 happens on her causeway. Teo is a recurring Hollow admin.
  • Arc: At Ironreach the PC's choice decides whether Teo lives (TEO_SAVED). Either way, in Act III she leads the Ebbmouth strike and cuts the tidal Lattice. In Act V her fishing fleet evacuates Crownhaven's lower city during the hijack.
  • Ace: Gyarados (raised from a Magikarp on her first day on the docks: "Everyone laughed at it too."); also Floatzel, Pelipper, Lanturn.

4. Rook Calder — Electric — Lumen City — LEADER_ROOK — :NEON_GRID

  • 20. Bleached undercut, holo-jacket and a Rotom camera drone always recording. Lumen's celebrity Warden and the face of every Meridian billboard.
  • Personality: Hyper and charismatic, with catchphrases ("Stay charged, Lumen!") and deep insecurity. He has never been offline long enough to know who he is.
  • Voice: "Chat says you're cute. Chat also says you're gonna lose. Chat's wrong a lot." / (after the Shatter, off-camera) "...Turn it off. Rotom. Turn it off."
  • Motivation: Stay loved.
  • Secret: He doesn't hold the Zap Plate. It sits in Meridian Spire's Plate Vault. Rook is a contractual figurehead whose family's debts Meridian paid off.
  • Plot role: His city is where the Shatter happens. Dex, the soda-seller kid the PC met earlier, is one of the twelve Fixed.
  • Arc: The Shatter breaks him open. In Act III he quietly visits the glass victims every night. In Act IV he leaks raw Spire security footage of Sable entering with a Meridian keycard. In Act V he cuts Lumen's grid from the arena feed during the hijack, starving the Engine and blacking out his own city. "First time Lumen's been dark in ten years. Kinda pretty."
  • Ace: Rotom (his camera drone, in a battle form); later Toxtricity (punk idol), Luxray, Electivire.

5. Sahar Ostrand — Ground — Duneholt — LEADER_SAHAR — :DESERT

  • 58. Archaeologist-Warden in a sand-colored keffiyeh and wire spectacles. She has a dry cough from forty years in the dunes.
  • Personality: Measured and patient. A teacher at heart, with very dry humor.
  • Voice: "The desert keeps everything. It just doesn't keep it where you left it." / "I buried the truth once because I was told to. The sand dug it up. I'd rather not argue with sand twice."
  • Motivation: Duneholt's aquifer is collapsing under Meridian's deep drills, which Ossaria's Prince-Elector Idris Kassan licensed without asking her. She also runs the Dig Lab, the game's fossil-revival service.
  • Secret: In AF 281 she uncovered the Aurel murals beneath the Old Caravanserai, reported them to Valen, and sealed them on his order.
  • Plot role: First concrete Prismfall clue. She gives the PC the Duneholt Rubbing (key item).
  • Arc: Confesses after the gym. In Act IV she leads the expedition into the Drowned Crown (Ch17). In Act V she testifies before the Accord Council.
  • Ace: Claydol (an ancient effigy, the archaeologist's partner); also Hippowdon, Krookodile, Sandaconda.

6. Corvin Mott — Poison — Mirefen — LEADER_CORVIN — :CORROSIVE_MIRE

  • 41. Gaunt, in a long oilcloth coat and a plague-doctor half-mask he wears "for the smell, mostly". He runs the Mirefen Infirmary in Nadir's runoff marsh, where Zenith's Lattice slag drains into the Toxic Plate's mire. His apprentice Wenlow (17) is cheerful, clumsy and doomed: Wenlow is killed in the Sump Purge (Ch16), and the player can't prevent it.
  • Personality: Sardonic, morbid and wickedly funny, and he hasn't slept properly since AF 288.
  • Voice: "Everything's poison, dear. Dose makes the cure." / "I've a ward of forty patients slowly turning into garden furniture. Forgive me if I don't stand for the Sovereign's anthem." / (battle) "Don't worry. I'm a doctor. I know exactly how much this hurts."
  • Motivation: Keep his Fixing patients alive.
  • Secret: He treats Hollow wounded off the books, and he treated the Mourner's burned hand the night of the Lumen Shatter. He knows exactly who Rowan is.
  • Plot role: The Quiet Ward shows the human cost of the Bleed. His ledger is where Juno finds the clue (J2).
  • Arc: In Act III he is arrested under the Mandate for aiding terrorists, and an optional sidequest frees him (CORVIN_FREED). In Act IV he runs the field hospital in Cragholm. In Act V he privately treats Valen and is the one who tells the PC, "He has weeks. Maybe."
  • Ace: Toxapex (the patient defender); also Amoonguss, Drapion, Crobat.

7. Brann Holloway — Fire — Cindervault — LEADER_BRANN — :VOLCANIC

  • 63. A giant with a braided beard, forge apron and hands like anvils. Valen's oldest friend and his AF 280 Festival final opponent.
  • Personality: Booming, jovial and sentimental. He cries at weddings and tells terrible jokes.
  • Voice: "Some things you forge to last. Some things you forge to break right — that's the harder craft." / "Valen and I nearly killed each other in the Festival of '80. Best day of my life." / (Act IV) "Aye. I reset the Crown's stone myself after the Fracture. It was smaller than it should've been. I never asked where the rest went."
  • Motivation: Protect his friend and his forge.
  • Secret: He is "the Sovereign's Hand", the one Warden who knows about the Tuning and that Valen is dying.
  • Plot role: The Caldera Crisis (Ch8). He is a commoner in a feudal kingdom: Ashkaar's Dragon-Lord, Dominar Hrothgar Ashfall, treats Cindervault's forges as his armory and demands the Light Stone (Reshiram) that Brann's guild has kept for centuries. Brann refuses. He confirms the truth about the Tuning when confronted in Act IV.
  • Arc: He stands guard beside Valen through the Festival. After Valen dies, he forges the memorial from the Crown's broken setting.
  • Ace: Coalossal (a walking forge); also Arcanine (loyalty), Magmortar, Camerupt.

8. Kaito Ren — Fighting — Ironreach — LEADER_KAITO — :COLOSSEUM

  • 39. Ex-Wardguard captain, close-cropped hair, a burn scar along his jaw, training wraps always on. Ironreach is Nadir's pit-fighting quarter, carved into the old Aurel-era mine shafts. Its Warden is whoever wins the Iron Trial, and he has held the seat for nine years. He is the only ex-Accord soldier Nadir trusts.
  • Personality: Disciplined and laconic. He speaks in short declarative sentences and is fiercely honest about everything except one thing.
  • Voice: "Stand up. Again." / "A fist is a promise. Don't make one you won't keep." / (Act III, testimony) "Marrowgate. Fifteen years ago. My orders. My unit. I sealed the gate. They were still knocking."
  • Motivation: Atone.
  • Secret: In AF 285 he led the Wardguard detachment that sealed Marrowgate, a Bleed-struck town, with Fixing survivors still inside, on Valen's order. His Lucario could feel them.
  • Plot role: Gym 8, and his Colosseum hosts the Conclave attack.
  • Arc: In Act III he testifies publicly about Marrowgate if the PC encourages him (KAITO_TESTIFIED), which shatters the Accord's credibility and makes him the Wardens' conscience. In Act V he is a Festival entrant (opponents per the Festival draw, NARRATIVE_KEYS §6) and fights with the PC in the hijack.
  • Ace: Lucario ("It remembers for me."); also Conkeldurr, Hawlucha, Machamp.

9. Juno Hale — Grass — Verdance — LEADER_JUNO / RIVAL_JUNO — :FLOWER_GARDEN

See Rivals. She succeeds Rowan Ashby in Ch10. Her trial ends with bonding to the Meadow Plate at the foot of the Bride Tree. Gym 9 is J3. Her Warden-era ace is Tsareena, and the :FLOWER_GARDEN growth stages track her confidence.

10. Anja Sorrel — Ice — Rimeholt — LEADER_ANJA — :FROZEN_LAKE

  • 46. Ice-fisher matriarch with a Mamoswine-hide coat, rime in her eyebrows, and a pipe she never lights indoors.
  • Personality: Blunt and pragmatic, with a sense of humor so dry it squeaks. She is contemptuous of southerners who moralize from warm houses.
  • Voice: "You want me to choose between my principles and my grandchildren's toes? Easy choice. Toes." / "Ice remembers. Every crack. Every weight. You walk on my lake, it knows your name by spring." / (after the Thaw) "I read the numbers. I read them. And I signed anyway, because the heaters were warm."
  • Motivation: Rimeholt's people survive an endless winter. Meridian's heat keeps children alive. She is the younger sister of Thane Eirik Sorrel, Isenmar's ruler, who signed the kingdom-wide heat contract. She signed Rimeholt's Lattice out of loyalty to him as much as to her people.
  • Secret: She knew the Lattice readings showed the lake cracking and suppressed them.
  • Plot role: The leading pro-Meridian Warden in Act III's political split.
  • Arc: The Thaw (Ch11): the Lattice overdraws and the lake breaks, and part of the city sinks (:FROZEN_LAKE → :WATER_SURFACE). She breaks with Meridian publicly (or the PC exposes her, ANJA_EXPOSED). In Act IV she leads Rimeholt's refugees south. In Act V she casts the deciding Conclave vote to strip Meridian of Festival security. The vote fails anyway, because Hollis has bought enough Wardens.
  • Ace: Mamoswine (matriarch of the herd); also Avalugg, Walrein, Weavile.

11. Odessa Gale — Flying — Skyreach — LEADER_ODESSA — :STORMFRONT

  • 27. Aviator goggles, scarf, a scar through one eyebrow, and a laugh carried off by the wind. Captain of the Gale Post, the airship-and-wing courier guild based in Skyreach, Zenith's airship spire. Its mail runs through the permanent storm wall, the Stormfront, to every kingdom and the outer islands.
  • Personality: Irreverent, quick, flirtatious, reckless and brave. She hates rules she didn't write.
  • Voice: "Skyreach's got one law: the wind doesn't care. Everything else is negotiable." / "I've flown letters through three storms and a Bleed. You want me to hand them to Meridian because a clerk said 'please'?" / (battle) "Hold onto something. No, really."
  • Motivation: Keep the post flying, and keep people free.
  • Secret: She smuggles refugees past the quarantine through the Stormfront to the outer islands. She has carried Hollow messages. Her dead-letter archive holds undeliverable mail from ten years back, including letters from Morwen Vane to "I." (Ines) abroad.
  • Plot role: K5. Larch broadcasts the Larch Papers through her relays.
  • Arc: Meridian raids the Post in Act III. In Act IV she flies the PC through the blockade. In Act V she airlifts civilians during the hijack. She is a Festival entrant (R16 vs Valen, and loses with style).
  • Ace: Corviknight (the air-taxi); also Pelipper (the old mail carrier), Swanna, Noivern.

12. Ilsa Marr — Psychic — Lucent — LEADER_ILSA — :MINDSCAPE

  • 31. Provost of the Lucent Athenaeum. Silver-blonde hair pinned with a pencil, high-collared academic robes, noise-cancelling earmuffs she wears around anyone emotional.
  • Personality: Cool, precise and unnervingly perceptive. She can read surface thoughts involuntarily and finds crowds unbearable. She is wry, lonely and secretly kind.
  • Voice: "You're thinking very loudly. Please stop." / "The truth is a weapon. I've kept it sheathed for eleven years. Don't mistake that for mercy." / (Act V, to Valen) "You chose a country over a daughter. Fine. Then save the country, Father. Don't make a child pay for it."
  • Motivation: Know everything; risk nothing.
  • Secret: She is Valen's estranged daughter. She has read the Tuning, his dying and his plan for "the child" out of his mind and kept silent.
  • Plot role: Guides the PC through the Mindscape (Ch13), where they recover the AF 290 memory. She hosts Rowan's tribunal at the Athenaeum.
  • Arc: In Act IV she sends the PC the Concordance's Secret Articles. In Act V, if the player completes her sidequest ("Little Owl"), she reconciles with Valen before the final (ILSA_RECONCILED). In the finale she holds the panicking crowd's minds calm and gives Arceus's voice to everyone in the arena.
  • Ace: Hatterene (it attacks emotions that are too loud); also Reuniclus, Espeon, Bronzong.

13. Bastian Ore — Rock — Cragholm — LEADER_BASTIAN — :CRYSTAL_CAVERN

  • 74. The oldest Warden. Stooped, with a miner's lamp on his hat, a clay pipe and enormous hands. He is half-deaf and pretends to be fully deaf when it suits him.
  • Personality: Folksy, stubborn and kind. He tells long stories that turn out to be important.
  • Voice: "Rock's patient. Rock'll wait for you to make a mistake for a thousand years. I'm only seventy-four, so I'll wait for about ten minutes." / "I've seen three Festivals, two Sovereigns and one crown break. Sit down, I'm telling you about the crown."
  • Motivation: Protect the people under his mountain.
  • Secret: He shelters fugitives in Cragholm's deep seams (Larch, Hollow, Rimeholt refugees). At the AF 290 Fracture he saw Meridian technicians at the Crown's mount and kept silent for ten years after the purge.
  • Plot role: The first eyewitness account of the Fracture (Ch14).
  • Arc: Defends Cragholm during the Meridian raid. In Act V he is too old to compete but stands at the ceremony carrying the Stone Plate. In Homecoming he dies peacefully in the epilogue, "the first Warden in three hundred years to die with nothing in his keeping".
  • Ace: Gigalith (crystal); also Carbink, Rhyperior, Golem.

14. Ysolde Ashgrave — Ghost — Gravenmoor — LEADER_YSOLDE — :HOLLOW_CATHEDRAL

  • 37. Pale, in layered black lace and a veil, with a lantern always lit. She speaks very softly. She was Morwen Vane's apprentice and partner, and Kestrel's step-parent in all but law.
  • Personality: Melancholy, tender and quietly unbreakable. She talks to the dead as easily as to the living.
  • Voice: "The dead aren't gone here. They're just very bad at conversation." / "Kestrel thinks I stole her seat. I kept it — from the man Valen wanted in it. There's a difference, but you can't explain it to a grieving boy." / (K6 aftermath) "She made me swear. 'When he's ready.' I don't think anyone is ever ready. I'm sorry I waited."
  • Motivation: Keep Morwen's promise and win back Morwen's son.
  • Secret: She holds Morwen's sealed letter to Kestrel. She knows Morwen's echo still lingers in the Hollow Cathedral.
  • Plot role: Gym 14, K6, and the Fracture echo.
  • Arc: At the Festival she watches over Kestrel. If KESTREL_YSOLDE_RECONCILED, she offers him the Ghost seat in the epilogue.
  • Ace: Chandelure (the cathedral candles); also Gengar, Dusknoir, Sableye. Morwen's Mismagius lives with her and goes to Kestrel after K6.

15. Magnus Strade — Steel — Cogsworth — LEADER_MAGNUS — :FACTORY

  • 52. Immaculate grey waistcoat, pocket watch, prosthetic left hand he built himself. A Meridian board member and Cogsworth's Warden.
  • Personality: Calm, exact and unflappable. He holds a utilitarian code he will not break: engineering ethics as religion.
  • Voice: "Faith gave us three hundred years of the Bleed. Engineering will give us the next three hundred without it." / "I don't build weapons. I build tolerances." / (Act V, if MAGNUS_KEPT_FAITH) "No machine I have built will kill a child. Governor's off. Go."
  • Motivation: Replace miracles with machines.
  • Secret: He built the Arbiter Engine's core. He believed it was a "Plate stabilizer", but by Act IV he knows exactly what it does.
  • Plot role: Gym 15. The PC finds the Arbiter blueprints in his foundry (:FACTORY → :SCRAPYARD in the infiltration battle).
  • Arc: The PC chooses to expose him to the Conclave (MAGNUS_EXPOSED: he is arrested, and Meridian loses a board vote) or to appeal to his conscience (MAGNUS_KEPT_FAITH: he stays in place and sabotages the Engine's safety governor at the finale, which weakens Voss's binding).
  • Ace: Metagross (the supercomputer); also Klinklang (his first machine), Bisharp, Excadrill.

16. Nyx Calloway — Dark — Underveil — LEADER_NYX — :NEW_MOON

  • 33. Information broker and undercity queen. Velvet and smoke, a crescent-moon earring, eyes that are always amused.
  • Personality: Silky, theatrical and dangerous. She treats secrets as currency and mercy as an expensive luxury she occasionally buys.
  • Voice: "Underveil isn't on the Accord's maps, sweetheart. That's the point of Underveil." / "Everything has a price. Yours just went up." / (Ch17) "Oh. Oh, darling. You're the missing piece."
  • Motivation: Survive, profit, and keep a promise her family made 300 years ago that she has privately decided is stupid.
  • Secret: Her house descends from Princess Maren's guard. They are the Keepers of the Drowned Truth and have always known the real Prismfall. She sold Voss the location of the Gilded Chain's remains two years ago.
  • Plot role: Gym 16. Underveil is Nadir's heart, built in the upper towers of drowned Aurelia beneath Zenith. Zenith claims sovereignty, but Nadir has no recognized government, so Nyx is its de facto queen and the Sump Barons pay her tithes. She guides the PC into drowned Aurelia.
  • Arc: Offers the PC a bargain: their secret for the Engine schematics (NYX_BARGAIN_TAKEN). After the Sump Purge she declares Nadir independent of Zenith (Ch17). In Act V she cuts Meridian's comms during the hijack. She is a Festival entrant (opponents per the Festival draw).
  • Ace: Zoroark (illusion); also Honchkrow, Grimmsnarl, Weavile.

17. Celestine Aubade — Fairy — Gloaming — LEADER_CELESTINE — :FAIRY_TALE

  • Looks 20, is 61. Gloaming's dusk Imprint slows aging. Gown of moth-silk and twilight, flowers that open only at dusk woven in her hair, barefoot.
  • Personality: Whimsical and riddling, speaking in fairy-tale cadence. Gloaming's oath-binding means she cannot lie, so she has become an artist of the true-but-misleading.
  • Voice: "Once upon a time — which is the only time there ever is, here —" / "I've been waiting for you for forty years, little colorless thing. You took the long way. Everyone does." / "I cannot lie. I can, however, phrase."
  • Motivation: See the Hymn fulfilled before she dies.
  • Secret: She keeps the Hymn of Return, the Accord's original liturgy, suppressed since AF 140. It says the Plates must go home when "one of no color walks the eighteen roads".
  • Plot role: Gym 17 and K7. Gives the PC the Hymn.
  • Legendary tie: Gloaming's Great Tree holds the Pixie Plate in its roots. The Ogre's masks in Elowen's folklore (Ogerpon) were carved from its fallen bark. Celestine is the one person who has always told the true version of the Ogre story, in rhyme, to anyone who'd listen.
  • Arc: At the Festival she is an entrant (opponents per the Festival draw). She voices Princess Maren in Dorian's Night of Masques. In the finale she sings the Hymn as the chains break. In Homecoming, her dusk ends with her Imprint. She sees her first full night sky in sixty years and ages gracefully in the epilogue.
  • Ace: Sylveon (ribbons that read intent: a creature of bonds for a woman bound by oaths); also Mimikyu, Togekiss, Alcremie.

18. Sigrun Ashfall — Dragon — Wyrmrest — LEADER_SIGRUN — :DRAGONS_DEN

  • 29. Tall, with a burn-scarred left cheek and neck, a braided crown of red hair and dragonhide armor. She is the strongest Warden and Valen's protégé. She is also the Dominar's daughter. She renounced her claim to the Ashkaar throne to take the Warden's oath, and her father has never forgiven her or the Accord.
  • Personality: Fierce, proud, blunt and honorable. She has no patience for lies, which makes her guilt unbearable.
  • Voice: "Wyrmrest is the last gate. Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed." / "He trained me to be his successor. Then he told me I'd never survive the Crown, and I was relieved. Do you understand? Relieved. And then he went looking for you."
  • Motivation: Honor, and atonement for her relief.
  • Secret: She knows Valen's plan for the PC, and she let it happen because it meant the Crown wouldn't be hers.
  • Plot role: Gym 18 makes the PC prove they choose the Festival with full knowledge. She refuses to give the badge until the PC says aloud what they intend.
  • Arc: In Ch19 she defies her father, whose legions are poised to march on Zenith, by keeping Wyrmrest's pass open to the PC. Reshiram wakes for the PC's spoken truth during her trial. At the Festival she fights her way to the semifinal (opponents per the draw). She loses the semifinal to Valen after entering to win the Crown herself and spare the PC. In the hijack, her dragons hold the sky over Crownhaven against Meridian's airships, and if DOMINAR_SWAYED, her father's legions fly with her.
  • Ace: Salamence (it dreamed of wings until it grew them); also Haxorus, Flygon, Kommo-o.

IMPORTANT SIDE CHARACTERS

  • Hesper Vane (78): Kestrel's grandmother and keeper of the Ferrow's Landing lighthouse. Bitter, sharp-tongued, and secretly sends the PC care packages after she learns who they are (Act IV). Gives Kestrel the Honedge.
  • Teo Reyes (19): Marisol's brother and Ebbmouth Hollow cell leader (HOLLOW_TEO). Idealistic, angry and funny. His survival at Ironreach is a player choice. If he lives, he becomes the Lantern Hollow's youngest organizer.
  • Mara Thistlewood "Thistle" (40): Hollow Pallbearer (HOLLOW_THISTLE), a former Briarwick weaver whose village of Lowmarsh was Fixed. The PC's first admin battle. Fierce and grieving. She is captured at Lumen and returns in Act IV on the PC's side.
  • Sable / Sabine Kroll (35): the Hollow's "Hammer" (HOLLOW_SABLE, later MERIDIAN_KROLL), secretly a Meridian Special Accounts operative. Cold, cheerful and professional. She caused the Lumen Shatter and the Ironreach attack. Exposed in Ch13 and fought again in Act V.
  • Dr. Percival Quent (44): Meridian Head of R&D and lead on the Arbiter Project (MERIDIAN_QUENT). Larch's former protégé. Giggly, brilliant and morally weightless. Fought in Ch8, Ch16 and the Festival tag battle. Post-game villain hook.
  • Director Helene Marchetti (50): Meridian Security Directorate, the Mandate's enforcer (MERIDIAN_MARCHETTI). Ex-Wardguard. Cold, loyal and competent, with no illusions. Fought in Ch12, Ch14 and the Festival tag battle. If MARCHETTI_TURNED (the PC shows her Kroll's file), she stands down in the hijack.
  • Chancellor Ambrose Hollis (61): the Accord's chief administrator, a smooth bureaucrat who is quietly on Meridian's payroll. He passes the Mandate and gives Meridian Festival security. He is arrested in the epilogue.
  • Elin Ashby (Fixed, AF 288): Rowan's wife and Juno's aunt, the Bride Tree in Verdance's Heartgarden. She wakes in Homecoming.
  • Dex (11): street kid who sells Fizzbolt soda outside Meridian Spire in Lumen. Talkative and lovely. Fixed in the Lowlight Shatter mid-wave. His glass figure becomes a shrine. He wakes in Homecoming and finishes the sentence he was saying.
  • Morwen Vane (deceased AF 296): former Ghost Warden, Kestrel's mother. She appears only as an echo (Ch15) and in her letter (Festival). Her echo's line to the PC: "Little one. You came back. I told her not to let you."
  • Ines (deceased): the PC's mother. She appears in the Ch13 memory, singing in the Choir.
  • Hierophant Maud Ellery (68): head of the Prism Choir, which performs the Tuning. A true believer who learns in Act V that the hymn she sings is half of a song.
  • Pip Ore (14): Bastian's granddaughter and his ears. A fast-talking lamp-runner who guides the PC through the deep seams.

KINGDOM RULERS & POLITICAL FIGURES

The Wardens answer to the Accord, but their cities sit inside kingdoms whose rulers resent that. Each ruler wants something from the Plates in their land. - Queen Maelis Thorne of Elowen (82): an ancient, fairy-tale-cunning monarch who holds court in Briar Keep, the castle crowning Verdance. Her doctrine is "the Plates are the Crown's by right of soil." She claims the Meadow Plate when Rowan falls (Ch10) and later signs a "Forest Compact" with Meridian. Her heir Prince Caedmon (34) is a smiling modernizer and quietly Meridian's man. Maelis outlives her usefulness to him in Act V. - Archseer Imre Dask and the Veiled Synod of Antumbra: Antumbra's rulers are a theocratic council of seers (Lucent) and lantern-priests (Gravenmoor) who mediate with Giratina. Dask (60s, two different-colored eyes, speaks in mirrored sentences) fears the Bleed is thinning the Glass. He refuses to open the Veil Gate for this Festival ("I won't open a door in a wall that's falling down"). On the Ruin Route, Dask is the Synod's champion who claims the Renegade in the QF: "Behind, welcome. Welcome, behind." (Team: Psychic/Ghost on :INVERSE.) - Thane Eirik Sorrel of Isenmar (52): Anja's older brother. Practical and proud, he is drowning in debt to Meridian for heat. He declares martial law after the Thaw (Ch11) and sends Isenmar's refugees south into Elowen, which causes a border crisis. - Dominar Hrothgar Ashfall of Ashkaar (64): Dragon-Lord and Sigrun's father. A warlord-king who believes "dragons bow to no Accord." He threatens war on Zenith after the Cindervault caldera crisis (Ch8) and masses legions at Wyrmrest in Act V. He can be swayed (DOMINAR_SWAYED) to fly for Crownhaven instead of against Zenith. - Prince-Elector Idris Kassan of Ossaria (45): elected for life by the caravan houses. Charming and venal, he sells Meridian drilling and mining rights (Duneholt's aquifer, Cragholm's seams) and pays the Bone Sands' fossil-hunters to look the other way. - The Zenith Council: Zenith's governing council of founding houses, chaired by Voss. Notables include Councillor Renata Sallow (old-money airship house, Voss's rival, who becomes a reluctant ally in Act IV) and Magnus Strade (Cogsworth). - The Sump Barons of Nadir: the chem-lords and pit-bosses who actually run the City Below, chief among them Mother Gutter (an ancient sludge-baron with a Muk the size of a cart). They pay tithes to Nyx and hate Zenith. They are also the Hollow's most reliable source of recruits and money. - Envoy Delphine Okoro of the Concert of Regions: the neighboring realms across the strait. She attends the Festival to decide whether the quarantine lifts or becomes Severance, meaning permanent abandonment. She can be persuaded (ENVOY_PERSUADED), which affects the epilogue's international outcome. - Chancellor Ambrose Hollis: see above. He is the hinge between the kingdoms' grievances and Meridian's money. - Vesper Lindqvist (24): leader of the Eevee Octave, eight Glassfall orphans raised by the Prism Choir, each beside a different Imprint. She runs the Festival-week Eeveelution gauntlet. Gentle and devout, and a little in love with the idea of the Festival.

SISTER-GAME CAMEOS (optional sidequests, Revision 4)

Lin, Amaria, Shelly, Alexandra, Venam, Shiv, Garret, Melia and Titania (and Nova, on the Ruin Route only) appear in small optional sidequests across the kingdoms, as Rejuvenation handles Reborn's Aya and Adrienn. None appear in the Festival or the finale. Revision 5: every Festival opponent is a character the player has met at least twice by Act IV. Antumbra's Glass is leaking, and that is the in-universe reason they're here. The full roster is in design/CAMEOS.md.

HISTORICAL FIGURES

  • King Theodric Aurel, "the Gilded King" (d. AF 0): tried to bind Arceus to end a famine-plague in his kingdom.
  • Princess, later Sovereign, Maren Aurel, called "Maren Ilse" after renouncing her house (AF −19 to 61): founded the Accord and set the Legend shard in the Crown.
  • Ansel Crewe (executed AF 52): playwright of "The Gilded King".
  • Sovereign Adela Wyndham (d. AF 270): Valen's predecessor. She died months after her last Tuning.

Story Bible 04: Act I — The Last Ferry (Prologue + Ch1–5, Gyms 1–4)

Tone: Rejuvenation-warm. Cozy, funny and curious, with the Bleed eerie but beautiful. The first real wound comes at the very end (the Lowlight Shatter). Conventions: - CHOICE = a named decision. Keys and effects are owned by story/10_CHOICES_ENDINGS.md. Suggested keys are in code, and Bonds/Standing deltas are indicative. - GO = a proposed story game over (title / trigger / foreshadowing / epitaph) for the Ledger catalog. - Map names follow design/WORLD.md. Revision 6: this act carries beats of the Ashkaar–Isenmar War and the Blessing. See 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md (timeline table) for the inserts per chapter; that file wins on conflicts.

Revision 7 (comedy): each chapter here has a scripted funny scene in 15_COMEDY.md §4 (Ch0–5), plus running gags (§3) and optional kingdom bits (§5). Place each scene where its heading says, and never inside the beats §1 lists as joke-free.

PROLOGUE — "The Last Ferry" (the Hallow Strait → Ferrow's Landing)

Open on black. A ship's horn. Text: Spring, AF 300. The Concert of Regions has sealed the borders of Velmora. This is the final civilian crossing.

Scene 1: the deck of the Dovetail at dusk. - The PC stands at the rail with one bag and their mother's letter. The player can read the letter (Key Item). - Captain Edda Morrow stomps past: "Don't lean on my rail like that, sprog — you'll — huh." She stares a beat too long. "...Never mind. Mind the gap when we dock." - On the horizon, eighteen colored glows sit low over the sea. A deckhand: "When I was a lad you could only see the Imprints on a clear night. Now you see 'em through fog." - Aurelian Voss joins the PC at the rail in a plain coat, holding a chipped Meridian mug. He's warm and self-deprecating, a man coming home from a summit where he begged the Concert to keep Velmora's trade open. "First crossing? Everyone gets quiet when they see the lights." - He gives the PC a Meridian badge case: "We make them for the League anyway. Call it a welcome from a fellow traveler." This is the telemetry device, so the player must receive it. He also offers a Meridian Fellowship card (₽ stipend, Mart discount). - CHOICE :pro_fellowship: accept the Fellowship (+Meridian Standing, more money in Act I) / politely decline (+Voss Bond, because he respects it: "Independent. Good.") / say nothing (he leaves the card on the rail anyway).

Scene 2: Ferrow's Landing dock. - A brand-new Meridian customs gate stands beside the old bronze Accord plaque ("Eighteen in Concord, One in Keeping"). - Every passenger is scanned and gets a colored tint bar. The PC's reads blank: NULL. - The technician (Calla Wren) frowns and rescans. Same result. - Voss, smoothly: "Faulty unit. These things hate sea air. Let them through, Calla." He glances back once. (Players who look closely will see Calla photograph the screen.) - Game over GO_HOMEWARD "Homeward". Trigger: at the dock, the PC tells Edda they want to go back. Foreshadowing: Edda asks twice, "Last chance, sprog. Once I cast off, that's it." The ferry leaves. Epitaph: "You went home. The lights went out one by one, and you watched them from the other shore." (A Rejuvenation-style joke game over in the first five minutes. It teaches players that GOs exist and are recorded.)

CHAPTER 1 — "Tidewatch" (Ferrow's Landing, R1)

Beats: 1. Ferrow's Landing. Tarred stilt-houses, drying kelp, gulls. Juno's parents, Wenna and Coll Hale, run a bramble-tart stall and adopt the PC on sight ("You're the Larch kid? Eat this. You're too thin."). - Children skip rope to the counting rhyme (01_WORLD_LORE §10). This is the first time players hear it. - The PC can collect the Old Rod from a fisherman. 2. Tidewatch Station. Professor Imelda Larch reads Ines's letter, sets it down very carefully, and pours two cups of kelp tea. "Your mother was the bravest coward I ever knew. Drink. It's disgusting." She doesn't explain. She takes the PC to the Wayfarer Sanctuary. 3. Starter selection. Any Gen 1–9 starter (SPINE). Juno Hale (assistant, kerchief, seed packets, chatter) bounces in. She picks the starter weak to the PC's ("It was looking at me. Also, underdogs."). 4. Kestrel crashes the ceremony. He has been sleeping in the Sanctuary's loft because his grandmother kicked him out again. He grabs the counter-starter: "Fair fights are for people who can afford to lose." Larch lets him take it with a look that says she has history with this family. - K1 (lv 6), at the Sanctuary gate. Afterward, Kestrel: "You came in on the last ferry? Great. Velmora's sinking and they're still letting tourists on." 5. Rowan Ashby visits. Juno's Uncle Rowan, the Grass Warden, has come down from Verdance to collect seed stock. He's gentle and funny, with soil under his nails. He shows Juno and the PC a sickly Phantump he rescued "from the Glasswood up past Briarwick. It was sitting in a tree that had turned to glass, crying." (Clue: the Mourner's ace is Trevenant.) - He tells Juno about larches: "Everything that grows must be allowed to die, Juno. That's not sad. That's what makes room." - Of his wife: "She'd have liked you." He's talking to the PC, and it lands oddly. 6. R1 → the Glassed Orchard (map 522, :BLEED, mandatory tutorial). - The Hales' neighbor's apple orchard turned to glass last winter. Glass apples chime in the wind. - Juno stops at the Bleed Veil: "My skin's burning. I can't — how are you doing that?" - The PC walks through. Inside, a Bleed-maddened Pokémon (a glassed Wurmple swarm leader) calms when the PC approaches. This is the first demonstration of Harmony. - The PC retrieves the farm's lost Skwovet for a reward. - Larch, waiting at the edge with a notebook, says very quietly: "Well. That's that, then." She records the first Field Notes entry (:BLEED). - Game over GO_ORCHARD "Two in the Orchard". Trigger: Juno asks "Can you pull me through? Just for a second?" and the PC says yes. Foreshadowing: the burning line, plus Larch's earlier warning, "Never take a tinted person past a Veil. Never." Juno's hand glasses in yours. Epitaph: "She trusted you. That was the whole problem." 7. Evening. Hesper Vane's lighthouse. The old woman shouts at Kestrel from the gallery while the PC passes below. She sees the PC and drops her lamp-rag. Inside the lighthouse door, visible if the PC knocks, hangs a cracked mirror Hesper refuses to replace: "Leave it. It shows the truth crooked, same as everything." (A Ruin Route seed: it is the last image of END_REFLECTION.)

Cliffhanger: Larch, at night, writes in her journal (a short cutscene): "Subject reads NULL. Second such reading in my life. The first was in Crownhaven, AF 290. God help us, Ines." Fade.

CHAPTER 2 — "The Weaver's Debt" (R2 → Briarwick, Gym 1)

Location: R2 (farmland into forest), Gossamer Wood (:SILKWOOD), Briarwick, the Glasswood and its heart Cocoonfall Hollow (548, :BLEED, reached on the way to Saltmere).

Arrival. Briarwick is a weaving village lashed between trees, with looms clacking and silk drying like flags. There is a shrine to the Loyal Three ("who drove the Gossamer Ogre from the wood"), and children wear carved ogre masks. (Seed for Elowen's legendary arc, and the Hollow's masks are the same design.) There is also a Meridian survey line of orange flags marching east, toward a half-built Lattice pylon.

Conflict. - The silk-moths are dying. The Glasswood, a crystallizing Bleed, is creeping toward the groves, and it is creeping fastest along the Lattice line. - Warden Tamsin Loom (19, anxious, a measuring tape around her neck) is overwhelmed. She is polite, apologetic and lying about something. - Hollow saboteurs are cutting the survey flags at night. The PC catches them at the pylon base. This is the first admin battle: Pallbearer Thistle (HOLLOW_THISTLE), a grieving ex-weaver: "Lowmarsh had looms too. Ask your Warden what she signed."

Gym 1 (:SILKWOOD). Puzzle per GYM_PUZZLES.md. Thematic note: threads that hold only when twisted together. Tamsin battles with her nervous fury: "Granny Loom wove this room. Don't you dare tear it." Her ace is the Leavanny line (Swadloon). On victory she awards the badge, and the badge case hums faintly the first time a badge goes in. (Seed. Juno: "Ooh, is it supposed to do that?")

Aftermath and revelation. Thistle's accusation sticks. Tamsin confesses to the PC in the loom-loft: Briarwick is bankrupt, and she signed the Lattice contract alone. The Lattice is feeding the Glasswood. - CHOICE :ch2_tamsin: - Expose her publicly at the village meeting. Village trust is shaken and the contract is torn up. The Hollow and Accord gain Standing, and Meridian loses some. - Help her confess herself. The slow route: she renegotiates the contract, and Meridian keeps a smaller pylon. She never forgets who stood next to her. - Keep her secret. Meridian Standing goes up. The pylon keeps draining Briarwick, Thistle's cell comes back angrier in Ch10 ("You knew"), and in Act V Tamsin can't afford to release her Plate (her Consent condition). - Every branch leads to the same next beat. - Boss battle, "The Glass Matriarch" (multi-bar, Rejuvenation-style). In Cocoonfall Hollow, a giant glassed Galvantula has woven a nest of crystal silk at the Bleed's heart. Tamsin can't cross the Veil, so she gives the PC her grandmother's shuttle "for luck". Defeating the Matriarch slows the Glasswood's spread (map state change: the Hollow's edge recedes on return in Ch10). The PC's presence calms the nest's Pokémon afterward. - CHOICE :ch2_matriarch: the beaten Matriarch thrashes in the silk-moth grove, shedding crystal. - Soothe: stay with it all night. It calms at dawn, but half the grove crystallizes (Harmony). - Silence: press your hand to it, and the Imprint drains out of it like water down a drain. The grove is saved, and the Matriarch is left hollow and dull-eyed (Ruin −2). Tamsin, seeing it: "Oh. Oh, that's — thank you. I think." - This is the player's first lesson: the efficient choice is the Ruin one. - On the way out, a wandering old woman at the Loyal Three shrine (actually Celestine, in disguise, unnamed) sings: "Three loyal hounds and an ogre so bold — but who was the thief, when the story was told?" (A seed for Elowen's legendary arc and for Celestine's Act V reveal.)

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Lost in the Gossamer" (Shelly). See design/CAMEOS.md. After Gym 1, a pink-haired stranger is stuck in Gossamer Deepwood, apologizing to the Bug Pokémon. It's the first cameo, and it teaches players that the Glass leaks.

Cliffhanger: A Festival poster in Briarwick's square shows the Crown. Its stone is drawn with a tiny chip missing. An old mural in the chapel shows it whole. A child: "Mum says the Crown got hurt once."

CHAPTER 3 — "Curtain Call" (R3 → Saltmere, Gym 2; J1)

Location: R3, Thistledown Manor (546/547, the haunted house), R4 (:BEACH), Brinecut Mine, Saltmere (Elowen's free port, :GRAND_STAGE).

Optional set piece: Thistledown Manor (comic haunted house). The dead Thistledown Players, a ghost acting family, are still rehearsing Hamlet-but-with-Pokémon. The PC must "play a role" in three scenes (choose lines; wrong choices start ghost battles). Reward: Spell Tag and a Litwick. The Manor's matriarch-ghost wants the PC to stay for the second act. - Game over GO_CURTAIN_CALL "Curtain Call". Trigger: accept her offer to "join the company forever". Foreshadowing: the butler-ghost mouths don't behind her. Epitaph: "Your notices were excellent. The run has not closed. It never will."

Arrival. Saltmere is salt-caramel stalls, lamplit promenades and a breakwater where the Queen's tax-barges are turned back by tradition (show it: an Elowen barge, booed). The Blank Plate makes Saltmere ordinary, so it's the only city untouched by Bleed, and it makes its own miracles on stage. Everyone is preparing the Pageant of the Prismfall (the official myth, "a gift of love from the Original One"). This year's companion piece is "The Loyal Three".

J1. Juno arrives by cart with seed-sacks and challenges the PC to a cheerful spar on the promenade (J1). Afterward the two of them sit on the breakwater eating salt-caramel. This is a quiet friendship scene, and it should be a breather. - CHOICE :ch3_juno_talk: tell Juno about the NULL scan (Juno Bond up. She promises to keep it secret, and she does) / deflect / say nothing.

Gym 2 (:GRAND_STAGE), a double battle: "a duet". Thematic note: perform for the crowd; the audience's mood changes the stage. Dorian Pell in velvet: "Enter, stage left, our brave challenger!" His ace is Kecleon, which changes "costume" with each type. After the battle, his troupe gives the PC the gift Eevee "Understudy" ("forty auditions, never cast — you two are a matched set").

The Pageant. The PC attends opening night with Juno and Dorian. - Mid-performance, the stage lights go grey, and the Mourner steps out of the painted backdrop of the Plates falling. Ash-bark mask, single white lily, doubled voice:

"Three hundred years of pageantry. Ask your Warden why nothing in Verdance ever rots. Ask your Sovereign why the old capital is underwater. Ask why the Loyal Three wore poison on their collars. Let the land grieve." - Bereaved grunts drop Hollow Ring banners. The crowd panics, and a battle against two grunts follows. - The Mourner looks straight at the PC across the theater and goes still. "...You." Then he's gone in a burst of Cotton Spore. - Juno, shaken: "He sounded sad. Isn't that weird? I thought he'd sound evil." - First meeting with the Eevee Octave. The Prism Choir's touring chorus sings the Pageant's hymns. It includes the Eevee Octave, eight Glassfall orphans each partnered with an Eevee-line Pokémon. Their leader Vesper Lindqvist (24, gentle, devout) finds the PC backstage after the panic and kneels to Understudy: "It hasn't decided which Imprint it belongs to yet. Neither have you, I think." Optional battle vs Vesper's Eevee and one sister's Eeveelution (double). Encounter 1 of 3 for her Festival exhibition.

Aftermath and revelation. - Dorian, rattled and pale, pulls the PC into his dressing room and presses a yellowed page into their hands: the prologue of "The Gilded King" (Key Item). "Ansel Crewe wrote that in AF 52. They hanged him for it. I've never staged it. I'm a coward with excellent diction." The page includes the line: "If heaven will not kneel, we'll build a chain." - Kestrel is outside the theater, white-faced. He heard the Mourner's voice. "That's — the way she talks — no. No." He runs. This sets up the "Mourner = Morwen" rumor.

Cliffhanger: An Elowen herald posts a royal notice. Queen Maelis "notes with grief" the Hollow outrage in her free port, "where the Crown's protection was refused". Dorian rips it down.

CHAPTER 4 — "Both Ways" (R5 → R6 → Ebbmouth, Gym 3; K2)

Location: R5 (salt flats), R6 (K2), Gullcry Caves, The Rehearsal (568, :BLEED, an optional zone where a stretch of road replays the same five seconds of a wagon crash forever), Ebbmouth (Isenmar's southern port).

Borderland. The PC crosses from Elowen into Isenmar. Mixed wild types, the first fur-coated NPCs, bone toggles. A border marker has both kingdoms' arms and a Meridian sunburst bolted over both.

K2 (R6, before Gym 3). Kestrel is hunting a Hollow cell he tracked from Saltmere, and he wants the PC out of his way. He is more raw than in K1. He has his new Honedge from his grandmother ("It was your mother's blade's child. Don't you dare lose it."). After the battle: - CHOICE :ch4_kestrel_mourner: ask whether he thinks the Mourner is his mother. - Kind: Kestrel Bond up a little. He snaps, but later sends a curt note. - Blunt: Kestrel Bond down. He shoves past. - Silence: neutral. He fills the silence himself: "...She'd never wear a mask. She hated masks."

Arrival. Ebbmouth: fog, gulls, smoked-eel stalls and union halls. The Ebb runs both ways, but lately it stutters, stopping dead for minutes. Mill-wheels freeze mid-turn and dockworkers hold their breath. Meridian's tidal Lattice squats at the river mouth. A strike is brewing: the Ebb Guild is squeezed between Thane Eirik's harbor taxes and Meridian's layoffs. - Ice-fishers in the tavern sing about "the Sleeping Whale under the ice". This is a Kyogre seed. ("When the whale wakes, the sea remembers it's a sea.") - Get the Good Rod at the Anglers' Guild.

Conflict. Warden Marisol Reyes, elected and brash, is caught in the middle. A Hollow cell spikes the tidal Lattice at night, and the river stops entirely for an hour. A child's boat drifts toward the stalled mill-race, and the PC and Marisol rescue the kid in a battle-and-chase. The cell leader is Teo Reyes (HOLLOW_TEO admin battle), 19 and furious, and he is Marisol's brother. Marisol lets him run. The PC sees it. - CHOICE :ch4_teo: - Report Teo to the Meridian guard: Meridian Standing up, Marisol's Bond down hard, and Teo is hunted (his Act II arc gets harder). - Keep Marisol's secret: Marisol's Bond up, Hollow Standing up a little. - Confront Marisol privately: Marisol's Bond up. She admits it, crying and swearing.

Gym 3 (:WATER_SURFACE). Thematic note: a current that changes direction mid-fight. Marisol: "The Ebb runs both ways. Always has. Lately it can't decide." Her ace is Gyarados (from her first Magikarp on the docks). Afterward she buys the PC smoked eel and says the thing Act I needs said: "Meridian pays my people. Meridian's killing my river. Pick one, kid, I dare you."

Revelation. Larch arrives by Gale Post with tide data. The stutters line up exactly with Meridian's Lattice draw peaks. "It's not the Hollow stopping the river. It's us. It's my design." This is the first crack in Larch's guilt.

Cliffhanger: Hesper Vane's letter reaches the PC via the post: "Whoever you are — stay away from my grandson. Everyone who gets close to that boy's mother's ghost gets hurt." Unsigned, but on lighthouse paper.

CHAPTER 5 — "Ten Years of Light" (R7 → R8 → Lumen City, Gym 4; the Lowlight Shatter)

Location: R7, R8, Voltaic Sluiceworks (:SCRAPYARD, Rotom), Ebb Hollow (588, :BLEED), Lumen City (Zenith's capital district), the Lowlight district (181, replaced by 608 after the Shatter), Meridian Spire.

Arrival. The first big-city wow moment: airships, holograms, Rotom delivery drones and a skyline of brass and glass. Everything is free-lit and humming. "Light for Everyone" banners celebrate Lumen's Tenth Year of Light, ten years since the first Lattice switched on. - The PC meets Dex (11), a Fizzbolt soda seller outside the Spire. He's talkative and lovely, and he tells you which roof has the best airship view. He's halfway through a story about his sister when he waves the PC off: "Tell you the rest tomorrow!" - Topside versus below. Down every alley there are grilles and lift-shafts into Nadir, with grimy air coming up and Hollow Ring graffiti. A Zenith officer moves a Nadir kid along: "Back downstairs."

Rook's gym is closed because he's filming. The PC helps Rook Calder with a blackout in the Sluiceworks (wild Rotom swarm) and earns his attention. He's hyper, a little lonely, and constantly on camera: "Chat says you're cute. Chat also says you're gonna lose."

Gym 4 (:NEON_GRID). Thematic note: circuits you complete with your own body. Rook's ace is Rotom (his camera drone, in battle form). After the badge, he gives the PC Flash. He's also the first person to notice the badge case pulse brighter on the fourth badge. He films it: "Ooh, special edition?"

The Gala (main set piece). Voss invites the PC, Juno, Rook and Larch (Larch refuses at first, then comes to keep an eye on things) to Meridian Spire's Tenth Year Gala. - Champagne, airships outside the windows and the Dark Stone core glowing black in the atrium shaft. (Zekrom seed: guests swear it "breathes".) - Voss's speech: "They'll tell you light is a gift. Light is work. Every lamp in this city was lit by someone who was told to be grateful for the dark." It's genuinely stirring. - Valen Corrigan is present. White robes, grey glove, grave and courteous. He speaks briefly with Voss and with nobody else. - Larch, murmuring to the PC: "The Zap Plate's not in Rook's gym, you know. It's here. In their vault." Rook, overhearing, goes very quiet.

The raid. - Lights flicker. The Hollow breach the Spire through Nadir's service lifts. Partner battle: PC + Juno vs two Bereaved Pallbearers' squads, the first tag battle. - The PC pursues them down to the Plate Vault and arrives in time to see it go wrong. The Mourner reaches the Zap Plate's cradle, but Sable (masked, Hollow-grey, cheerful) slaps a Splitter charge on the Plate. The Mourner lunges for it. - The Shatter. A white-violet blast. The Plate cracks. The Mourner catches it barehanded and his glove smokes: "No — no — what did you do?" (To Sable. The player will read it as aimed at the PC or at himself.) Sable is gone. The Mourner sees the PC, drops the cracked Plate into its cradle, and flees. - Boss battle, "Overcharged" (multi-bar): a Bleed-maddened Electivire, swollen with the Shatter's discharge, blocks the vault door. The field flips mid-battle from :NEON_GRID to :BLEED, the first scripted any→BLEED transition. - Game over GO_LIVE_WIRE "Live Wire". Trigger: during the Electivire fight's interlude prompt, choose "Grab the cracked Plate and run". Foreshadowing: the Mourner's smoking glove a moment earlier, and Rook's shout, "Don't touch it with your hands!" Epitaph: "Even you weren't made to hold lightning bare."

Aftermath: the Lowlight. - The Spire's shadow-district has been hit by the Shatter wave. Twelve people are Fixed in glass mid-motion: a woman hanging laundry, a man mid-laugh, and Dex, mid-wave, soda crate on his hip. - The PC can walk through the Lowlight's Veil (map 608, :BLEED) when no one else can. They are the only one who can stand among the glass. NPCs watch them do it. - Rook, off-camera, shaking: "...Turn it off. Rotom. Turn it off." - Voss restores the light. Meridian crews reroute the grid in under an hour, and Lumen sees Voss on every screen, in shirtsleeves, carrying a glassed child's soda crate out of the Lowlight himself. He's a hero. (He means it, and he did it. Both are true.) - Valen walks into the Lowlight's edge, removes his glove for a moment to touch the Veil, and the player glimpses crystal. Then he sees the PC standing inside. "You were in the Lowlight when it went? And you're... standing." He looks a beat too long, then turns away. - Valen's broadcast that night grants Meridian the Mandate: security powers wherever Lattices stand. "Velmora endures."

CHOICE :ch5_lowlight_testimony: a Directorate officer asks what the PC saw. - "The Mourner broke the Plate" (what it looked like): Meridian Standing up, Hollow Standing down, and the Mandate passes with a bigger majority (flavor). - "Someone else was there" (the masked second figure): Hollow Standing up, Meridian Standing down a little. Marchetti makes a note of the PC's name. Larch quietly says "good". - "I don't know": neutral. - If the PC said "The Mourner broke the Plate", Dorian reads it in the papers in Saltmere: "So you told them the pageant version. I know that one by heart." This seeds his Act V refusal: he keeps the lie he knows, and won't release the Blank Plate.

Revelation. Larch, in the ruined vault at 3 a.m. with a borrowed scanner: "That wasn't the Hollow's hammer. Look at the stress lines. The Plate was already cracking. Something's been drinking from it for ten years." She looks up at the Lattice conduits. "Something I built."

INTERLUDE I — "Lamplight" (playable as Kestrel). - Ferrow's Landing lighthouse, the same night. The player controls Kestrel on the gallery while Hesper sleeps. - He finds his mother's old Ghost Warden's veil in a trunk. - A short memory plays: Kestrel, age 6, in a crowd, his mother in a veil running toward "a kid made of light". He wakes before he sees the rest. - Mini-battle: Kestrel vs a wild Bleed-touched Haunter on the lighthouse stairs. He talks to his Honedge like a person. - It ends with him packing a bag. "If you're alive, I'll find you."

END OF ACT I. Title card: II — Imprints.

Story Bible 05: Act II — Imprints (Ch6–9, Gyms 5–8)

Tone: Reborn-grey. The cost of Velmora's miracles becomes visible: company towns, Fixing wards, a street drug, an act of terror, and an unmasking that hurts someone you like. Humor stays, but it's gallows humor now. Conventions as in 04 (CHOICE / GO / map names per WORLD.md). Revision 6: this act carries beats of the Ashkaar–Isenmar War and the Blessing. See 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md (timeline table) for the inserts per chapter; that file wins on conflicts.

Act-opening beat (start of segment 4): the PC leaves Lumen City through the Lowlight (Shattered) (608, :BLEED). A shrine of soda cans and candles has grown around Dex's glass figure. If the PC stands with him, the shard hums and the glass warms slightly under their hand. (Harmony seed for the Good ending's waking.) Mandate checkpoints with Directorate officers in white-and-gold now stand at every lift down to Nadir.

Revision 7 (comedy): each chapter here has a scripted funny scene in 15_COMEDY.md §4 (Ch6–9), plus running gags (§3) and optional kingdom bits (§5). Place each scene where its heading says, and never inside the beats §1 lists as joke-free.

CHAPTER 6 — "What the Sand Keeps" (R9 → Static Tunnels → R10 → Duneholt, Gym 5; Ossuary of Dunes; K3)

Location: R9, Static Tunnels (Flash), R10 (:DESERT), Duneholt (Ossaria), R11, Ossuary of Dunes (Unown ruins, Strength), Sinkfield (628).

Borderland: Zenith's plateau gives way to Ossaria, the Bone Sands. Fossil ribs as big as houses poke from the dunes. Caravan-houses pitch tents in the lee of them. NPCs quote prices in "bones".

Arrival. Duneholt is sandstone terraces around a great sinking well. The aquifer the Earth Plate holds in the stone is collapsing under Meridian's deep drills, and every week the waterline drops another rung. The Prince-Elector's men post notices: "By license of His Excellency Idris Kassan, the Concord deep-works are a protected enterprise." Caravan-mothers curse at them. - Sahar's Dig Lab: fossil revival opens here. Ossaria is fossil country, so a wide selection is available, and Sahar is delighted by anyone who brings her bones. - Under the town, the ground is warm. Children say "the sleeping red giant" is turning over in its bed. (A Groudon seed.)

Conflict. - A drill-site explodes. The Hollow freed Pokémon that Meridian had been using as living drill-cores (Excadrill and Drilbur fitted with Lattice collars). The Directorate arrives with the Mandate's new powers and starts arresting caravan kids. - CHOICE :ch6_drillsite: - Help the Hollow get the freed Pokémon into the dunes: Hollow Standing up, Meridian Standing down. Directorate ambushes follow in Ch7. - Help the Directorate secure the site: Meridian Standing up, and you earn a "commendation" (a lightly sinister Key Item: a Mandate Pass that later opens checkpoints). - Get Sahar to invoke the Warden's right of sanctuary over the children: Accord Standing goes up and the Directorate backs off, furious. (Choosing the Directorate here is Sahar's Consent refusal in Act V.)

Gym 5 (:DESERT). Thematic note: dig down, because what you need is buried. Sahar is patient, dry and coughing: "The desert keeps everything. It just doesn't keep it where you left it." Her ace is Claydol. She gives Strength via her excavators.

Aftermath and revelation: the Ossuary of Dunes (Unown ruins set piece). - The latest dune-shift has uncovered a doorway in a giant fossil skull on R11. - Sahar goes pale. She has seen this before. She confesses: in AF 281 she excavated these ruins, reported them to Valen, and resealed them on his order. - Inside, Unown drift in the walls. The PC solves Unown-script puzzles (World/puzzle team) and reveals murals: 1. A golden king, the Gilded King. 2. Two titans, one red and one blue, tearing the land ("the Year of Two Weathers"). 3. A chain of eighteen golden locks around a radiant beast of eighteen lights. 4. The beast breaking apart, with lights falling like rain and a city crumbling into the sea. - Sahar takes a rubbing: the Duneholt Rubbing (Key Item). "I buried the truth once because I was told to. The sand dug it up. I'd rather not argue with sand twice." - A boss-grade guardian (a giant Unown-swarm "glyph" formation) defends the deepest chamber. It's an optional multi-bar fight that rewards an Unown and the Unown Report.

K3 (after Gym 5, at the Ossuary exit). Kestrel has been following the Hollow's trail from Ebbmouth and arrived in time to see the murals over the PC's shoulder. - After the battle he finally says it aloud: "Everyone keeps telling me to let it go. My mother had a seat, and a Plate, and a city that loved her. Then one night she had nothing. Let what go?" He lays out the rumor running all over Velmora: the Mourner is Morwen Vane, alive. "She wasn't killed by the Bleed. She was made to disappear." - CHOICE :ch6_kestrel_rumor: - Encourage him to find out (Kestrel Bond up, and his obsession grows). - Warn him it might not be her (Bond down a little now, but it pays off at Ironreach, where he remembers you warned him). - Silence.

Game over GO_SINKFIELD "Sinkfield". Sinkfield (628) is a Bleed zone where the collapsing aquifer and the Earth Imprint have made the sand behave like water. Trigger: ignore Sahar's three warnings ("walk the bones, never the sand") and step off the fossil path to grab a glinting item. Foreshadowing: the Field Notes entry and a caravan-boy's lost sandal sinking slowly. Epitaph: "The desert kept you. Somewhere else."

Cliffhanger: A Gale Post courier finds the PC with a letter from Juno: "Uncle Rowan's been 'away on Warden business' every time something happens. I'm being silly. Come to Mirefen? I'm helping Dr. Mott with his seed-medicine. Bring snacks."

CHAPTER 7 — "The Quiet Ward" (R12 → Mirefen, Gym 6; J2)

Location: R12, Mirefen (Nadir, :CORROSIVE_MIRE), the Quiet Ward, the Sump Barons' Sweet Market, R13, Miasma Caverns, Weeping Fen (648), R14 Lake Sallow (Surf after the badge).

Arrival: first descent into Nadir. - The PC descends a rattling lift-cage from the plateau's edge. The sky disappears, and Zenith's underside is a ceiling of pipes dripping slag. The air tastes of pennies. Rebreathers are for sale on every corner. - Hollow Ring graffiti is everywhere, often beside a painted Ogre in the Mask (Nadir's folk-saint). - Mirefen is stilt-houses over a marsh that glows blue at night: Zenith's Lattice runoff mixing with the Toxic Plate's mire, the "poison-blue moon" of the rhyme. - This is the best street food in Velmora (bog coffee, slag-fried noodles), and people are kinder here than topside.

Conflict: Corvin and the Ward. - Warden Corvin Mott is gaunt, in a plague-doctor half-mask, and wickedly funny. He runs the Infirmary and its Quiet Ward: forty patients at Fixing stages 2–3, some humming, some half-glass, one a child whose hair has turned to salt. - His apprentice Wenlow (17) is cheerful and clumsy, and introduces the PC to every patient by name. (Players should love Wenlow. Wenlow dies in Ch16.) - Sweetrot. Pastel mochi-like sweets sold in the Sweet Market by smiling vendors of the Sump Barons (Mother Gutter's people). Users go docile, loyal and blissful, and they keep coming back. Corvin's other ward is full of Sweetrot addicts. "It's not a drug. It's a leash." This is the Pecharunt seed: the sweets have a faint peach-and-poison smell, and a child's rhyme mentions "the peach that keeps you". - Game over GO_SWEET_TOOTH "Sweet Tooth". Trigger: accept Sweetrot from a vendor three times across the chapter (each offer gets more insistent; Wenlow's warning comes after the first). Epitaph: "You were so happy. You never wondered why again."

J2. - Juno is in the Infirmary helping Corvin brew seed-medicine from Verdance stock. She is luminous with purpose. - Alphabetizing his ledgers, she finds an entry from the night of the Lumen Shatter: "R.A. — 2nd-degree Bleed-burn, right palm and fingers. Lightning pattern. Discretion." Corvin snatches it back too quickly. - Juno laughs it off ("R.A., there's loads of R.A.s") and challenges the PC to a spar on the stilt-walk to shake off the feeling (J2). Mid-battle line: "He wouldn't. He wouldn't." - CHOICE :ch7_ledger: - Say "Rowan Ashby" out loud: Juno's Bond drops sharply and she storms off. At Ironreach she is less shattered, because she already knew. This becomes a branch flavor in J3. - Reassure her: Juno's Bond goes up. The unmasking hits her full-force. - Ask Corvin directly, privately. He says only, "I'm a doctor. I treat hands. I don't ask whose."

Hollow admin: Brother Wick (HOLLOW_WICK). - The PC tracks a Hollow medicine-runner to a drowned chapel on the edge of the Weeping Fen, a Bleed zone where rain falls upward and every puddle weeps. - Brother Wick is old, half-blind and gentle, and he's here to bless dying Bereaved. He fights only because the PC forces it, or the PC can talk him down if Hollow Standing is high enough or they chose kindly in Ch5 (no battle). - After the battle (or the talk) he explains the Wound Doctrine: "The Choir teaches that the Plates were a gift. Child, nobody bleeds for three hundred years from a gift." Then, more quietly, something new: "The Mourner doesn't want to break them anymore. He wants to carry them home. But you can't carry what you can't hold." He looks at the PC's chest and doesn't say why.

Gym 6 (:CORROSIVE_MIRE), a double battle. Thematic note: poison is a dose; the right amount heals. Corvin: "Don't worry. I'm a doctor. I know exactly how much this hurts." His ace is Toxapex. The Mirefen ferrymen give the PC Surf.

Revelation. That night Corvin drinks bog coffee with the PC on the Infirmary roof. "Do you know why nobody in my Ward is dead? They're not dying. They're dreaming. I can see their eyes move under the glass." A beat. "Somebody should find out what they're dreaming about." (This seeds the Good ending's waking and Venam's Act V testimony.)

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Sewer Royalty" (Venam). See design/CAMEOS.md. A loud punk girl with a Seviper has fallen through a sewer mirror into Mirefen's runoff tunnels, and she is helping Wenlow drag Sweetrot addicts to the Infirmary. Completing it earns Venam's Testimony, a recording of her account of being turned to stone and coming back ("I could hear everything"). It counts as one of the Envoy's proofs in Ch20 and can be played for Juno in Ch10.

Cliffhanger: A Hollow broadcast hijacks every screen in Nadir. It isn't the Mourner. It's Sable, unmasked mouth, cheerful: "The Mourner's gone soft. The Hammer hasn't. Cindervault's next. Light for everyone, right?"

CHAPTER 8 — "The Light Stone" (R13 → R14 → Cindervault, Gym 7; the Caldera Crisis)

Location: R14 Lake Sallow (Surf), Lantern Isle (optional, :BEACH), the road up into Ashkaar, Cindervault (:VOLCANIC), Mount Cinder, Ashkaarfall (668), R15 (:ASHEN_WASTE after the crisis).

Borderland: the land climbs out of Nadir's runoff into Ashkaar, the Dragonfire Dominion: black basalt, red lacquer banners and dragon-riders overhead. Oaths are sworn over braziers at every gate. Lying to a Dominion guard is a crime, and an early NPC teaches this ("Say true or say nothing, stranger").

Arrival. Cindervault is a forge-city inside Mount Cinder's caldera. Lava-channels for quenching, smithies stacked up the crater walls, the Hearthguild's bells. - Warden Brann Holloway is a giant, booming and sentimental. He drops his hammer to bear-hug Larch, an old friend, and then the PC for good measure. - Two crises: 1. Meridian's geothermal tap at the caldera floor is overdrawing the Flame Plate's Imprint. The magma is rising, and the forges run too hot to work. 2.
