The Dominar's envoys are in town demanding the Light Stone, the Hearthguild's ancestral relic (Reshiram), "for the throne of Ashkaar, as is right." Brann refuses on Concordance grounds. - First appearance of Sigrun Ashfall, the Dragon Warden. She arrives on a Salamence to mediate between Brann and her father's envoys. She is fierce and curt, and she treats the PC with frank appraisal: "So you're the ferry child everyone's whispering about. Hm. Smaller than I expected." She calls the Dominar "my father" exactly once, in a tone that ends conversations.

Gym 7 (:VOLCANIC). Thematic note: a forge you can stoke or quench, changing the field's heat level as you go. Brann: "Some things you forge to last. Some things you forge to break right — that's the harder craft." His ace is Coalossal.

The Caldera Crisis (set piece). The tap's pressure spikes, and the caldera will erupt within the hour. Three groups converge on the caldera floor: - Wick's Hollow faithful, with a plan to vent the pressure by quenching the Imprint with Lake Sallow water piped through the old lava-channels. It's safe but drastic: it turns the caldera to ash for a season. - Dr. Percival Quent's Meridian team, who want to install "dampers" and keep the tap running. Meridian admin battle: Quent (MERIDIAN_QUENT), giggling: "Oh, it's you. The null reading! Hold still, I've only got one probe." - The Hammer: a Bereaved squad planting a Splitter on the tap to cause an eruption "Meridian can't cover up". - CHOICE :ch8_caldera: - Help Wick vent. The battle field transitions :VOLCANIC → :ASHEN_WASTE. The eruption is averted and the forges go cold for months, and Brann grumbles about it for a season. Hollow Standing goes up, Meridian Standing down. R15 becomes ash. - Help Quent's dampers. The eruption is averted, Meridian Standing goes up and the tap keeps running. The Bleed at Ashkaarfall grows (map state), and Brann's Bond goes up ("You kept my forges lit"). - Shut the tap down by force (a harder fight vs Quent plus Directorate). The eruption is averted, Accord Standing goes up and the Dominar is impressed (DOMINAR respect +1). Meridian Standing drops hard. - Game over GO_LET_THEM_LISTEN "Let Them Listen". Trigger: a Hammer bomber hands the PC the detonator and says "Do it. They'll finally listen." The PC accepts. Foreshadowing: the Field Notes entry on Shatters, and the Lowlight's glass. Epitaph: "They listened. There was no one left to tell."

Aftermath: the Light Stone. - After the crisis, the Dominar's envoys try to seize the Light Stone in the confusion, and Sigrun stops them herself. - CHOICE :ch8_lightstone: - Back Brann in keeping it under the Hearthguild. The Dominar's grudge grows. - Suggest it go to Sigrun at Wyrmrest, neutral ground under a Warden: Sigrun's Bond up, Accord Standing up. This seeds DOMINAR_SWAYED, because the Dominar respects his daughter's claim more than Brann's. - Say it should go to the Dominar ("it's his people's"): Ashkaar's favor goes up and Accord Standing drops. Brann is hurt, and in Act V he won't release the Flame Plate "for a man who gave the Light Stone away" (his Consent refusal). Late payoff: Reshiram's trial at Wyrmrest is harder.

Revelation. Brann, drunk and maudlin over chestnuts, lets slip one sentence: "I reset the Crown's stone myself, y'know. After the accident. Ten years back. It was smaller than it should've been." Then he catches himself: "Ach, I'm an old fool. Forget it." The PC's shard throbs once. (Larch notices the PC wince.)

Cliffhanger: The Dominar's herald reads a proclamation in the caldera square: Ashkaar holds Zenith responsible for the "assault on the Flame" and closes the eastern passes to Meridian. War-drums. Sigrun, watching her father's banner, says nothing at all.

CHAPTER 9 — "The Iron Conclave" (R15 → R16 Ironway → Ironreach, Gym 8; K4; the Conclave; the Mourner unmasked)

Location: R15, R16 Ironway (:MOUNTAIN), Ironreach (Nadir, :COLOSSEUM), the Iron Pit, the Conclave galleries, the old Aurel mine shafts.

Arrival. Ironreach is Nadir's pit-fighting quarter, carved into Aurel-era mine shafts. The Fist Plate makes everyone strong, and arguments are settled in chalk circles. It's loud, sweaty and honest. Warden Kaito Ren (ex-Wardguard captain, laconic, wrapped hands) is the only former Accord soldier Nadir trusts. - The Conclave. Sovereign Valen has called a rare Accord Conclave here, the first held in Nadir in a century, to vote on extending the Mandate realm-wide. Every available Warden is present. - Introductions/reunions: Tamsin, Dorian, Marisol, Rook, Sahar, Corvin, Brann, Sigrun, Rowan (who embraces Juno), and Odessa Gale herself, who delivers Fly in person from a skiff she lands on the Pit's rim ("Courier's privilege"). - First sightings (Revision 5: these Wardens must be met before the Festival): - Ysolde Ashgrave, veiled, watches Kestrel across the Pit and leaves before he sees her. - Nyx Calloway is "slumming it" in the observers' gallery and buys the PC a drink they're too young for. - Celestine Aubade, barefoot and openly herself this time, smiles at the PC: "We've met. You didn't know it yet." - Anja Sorrel arrives with her brother's envoy and complains about the heat. - Bastian Ore pretends to be deaf to Hollis for the whole session. - Each Warden gets one line. Their full chapters come later. - Chancellor Hollis presides. - Kings' envoys sit in the "observers' gallery", and the Elowen and Ashkaar envoys refuse to sit next to each other.

The Ironreach Open, K4. The Iron Pit's open tournament runs during the Conclave as entertainment. Nadir loves it. The PC fights through brackets of pit-fighters (trainer gauntlet) to the final vs Kestrel (K4). - Kestrel is here because rumor says the Mourner will strike the Conclave. He wants to be closest when it happens. - The crowd chants for "the null kid".

Gym 8 (:COLOSSEUM). Thematic note: the crowd's roar is a stat; earn it. Kaito: "Stand up. Again." His ace is Lucario. After the battle, quietly: "A fist is a promise. Don't make one you won't keep."

The attack. 1. During the Mandate vote, the galleries go dark. The Hammer has rigged the Fist Plate's reliquary, which was brought up for the Conclave's oath, with Splitters. Sable's voice on the pit speakers: "Vote yes on the Mandate. Go on. Give them the leash. We'll give you a reason." 2. Partner battle: PC + Kestrel vs Sable + Hammer Captain Brisk. Sable escapes into the shafts. 3. The PC pushes up to the Pit's upper ring and finds the Mourner standing over the reliquary. He's not planting the charge; he's trying to disarm it. But the player doesn't know that yet. 4. The Mourner battle (HOLLOW_MOURNER). Boss: Trevenant (multi-bar), with a Shiftry and Cacturne escort. The Mourner fights defensively. He keeps saying "Not now — you don't understand — get BACK." 5. The partial Shatter. The charge goes off anyway, and half the Fist Plate's Imprint bursts. The field in the reliquary chamber flips to :BLEED. Glass races across the stone toward the gallery, where Juno is shielding a child. 6. The Mourner throws himself in the way. His Trevenant's roots tear up through the floor to wall off the glass, and his mask takes the burst and cracks in half. 7. Juno, three meters away: "...Uncle Rowan?" 8. Rowan, bleeding and half-glassed at the shoulder: "Juno. Don't look at me like that. Look at the flowers. Look at what they're standing on." 9. The PC is the only one who can walk into the Bleed to pull Rowan out. Everyone, Valen included, watches them do it.

CHOICE :ch9_teo_sable (the act's big choice). Through the smoke the PC sees two things at once: - Teo Reyes is pinned under fallen scaffolding at the Bleed's edge. He came with Rowan to stop the Hammer, and he is Fixing fast. - Sable is running for a Meridian service lift with a comm-device in her hand.

The PC has time for one: - Save Teo (TEO_SAVED). Teo lives, glassed fingers and all, and Marisol's Bond goes way up. Sable escapes clean, and her Meridian link has to be proven later and at higher cost (Ch13 via Ilsa/Nyx). - Chase Sable (IRONREACH_EVIDENCE). A short chase battle through the shafts. The PC tears the comm-device from her belt: Meridian Special Accounts encryption, and a voice on it saying "Kroll, report." Teo dies in the Bleed, half-glass, reaching for his sister. Marisol's line in Ch13: "He was nineteen. Nineteen. Tell me again how the Hollow are the monsters."

Aftermath. - Meridian Directorate officers take Rowan into Meridian custody, not the Accord's, under the Mandate, which just passed in the chaos. Marchetti: "Mandate jurisdiction, Sovereign." Valen allows it. - Kestrel stares at Rowan's face for a long time. "...It's not her." He doesn't know whether he's relieved. He walks away into the shafts. - If the PC warned him in Ch6: "You said it might not be. I hate that you were right." - Juno sits on the Pit's floor in the glass dust. She won't speak to anyone except, if :ch3_juno_talk was kind, the PC. - CHOICE :ch9_rowan_words: as Rowan is led away, he looks to the PC. Answer: - "Why?": he says, "Ask the Drowned Crown." - "I'll look after her": he says, "I know. That's why I'm not afraid." - Silence: he says, "You saw what I saw. In the Lowlight. Didn't you?"

Each sets a small Rowan Bond and colors his tribunal in Ch13.

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Two Lucarios" (Garret). See design/CAMEOS.md. In the Ironreach Open's undercard, a gruff stranger with a Lucario asks every pit-fighter about a missing girl named Eden. It's available after the Conclave, in the shafts below the Pit.

Revelation. Valen crosses the ruined Pit to the PC, whose shoulder is dusted with glass that isn't hurting them. He looks at the glass, then at the PC.

"Come to Verdance. There's a seat to fill — and I think you should see what it costs."

He leaves. Brann, at his elbow, looks back at the PC with an expression of pure grief.

Game over GO_PULL_THE_NAIL "Pull the Nail". Available only if Hollow Standing is high. Before the attack, Sable approaches the PC in the shafts: "You can walk in the Bleed. You could plant it where no one else can reach." Accepting leads to the Shatter in full, and Juno is Fixed. Foreshadowing: Wick's Ch7 line ("he wants to carry them home") and Sable's broadcast. Epitaph: "You pulled the nail. The whole house came down."

INTERLUDE II — "The Bride Tree" (playable as Rowan, as a flashback that plays after the unmasking). - The dawn before the Conclave. Verdance's Heartgarden. The player controls Rowan as he waters the Bride Tree. - Gentle walk-and-talk with the tree, which is Elin: "The roses are coming in again. They don't stop, love. They never stop." - Mini-scene: he shows the healed Phantump from Ch1, now a Trevenant, the bark mask, and the lily he picks from her branches every time. - It ends with him putting on the mask: "One more. Then I'll find the other way. I promise."

END OF ACT II. Title card: III — Fault Lines.

Story Bible 06: Act III — Fault Lines (Ch10–13, Gyms 9–12)

Tone: Desolation begins. Political fracture, kingdoms arming, a city sinking, a mentor framed, a rival curdling. The PC is now known, and everyone wants something. Each chapter ends worse than it began. Conventions as in 04. Map names per WORLD.md (segments 8–11). Revision 6: this act carries beats of the Ashkaar–Isenmar War and the Blessing. See 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md (timeline table) for the inserts per chapter; that file wins on conflicts.

Act-opening state: the Mandate is realm-wide. Directorate checkpoints stand at every kingdom border, and Meridian scanners at every Pokémon Center door now flag the PC's NULL reading with a polite "Fellowship Priority Guest" banner. Even being watched is courteous.

Revision 7 (comedy): each chapter here has a scripted funny scene in 15_COMEDY.md §4 (Ch10–13), plus running gags (§3) and optional kingdom bits (§5). Place each scene where its heading says, and never inside the beats §1 lists as joke-free.

CHAPTER 10 — "The Seat That Blooms" (R17 Lift Road → R18 Prism Falls → Verdance, Gym 9; J3)

Location: - R17 Lift Road (the great freight-lift from Nadir up to the plateau) and R18 Prism Falls (:RAINBOW). - Grudgestone Quarry, Echo Coliseum (688, :BLEED: Ironreach's attack replaying as ghost-sound). - Verdance (Elowen's royal seat, :FLOWER_GARDEN), Briar Keep (the Queen's castle on the highest terrace), the Heartgarden, the Bride Grove (708), the Royal Chase (Safari-style reserve). - Branch maps by Standing: 698 Hollow Sanctuary (high Hollow) or 699 Meridian Black Site (high Meridian).

Branch scene on the Lift Road: - High Hollow Standing: Brother Wick's runner leads the PC to the Hollow Sanctuary in Grudgestone Quarry. The leaderless faithful are in hiding. Wick teaches the missing verse of the Hymn early (Key Item fragment), and Thistle asks the PC to carry a letter to Rowan's jailers. - High Meridian Standing: Calla Wren invites the PC to tour the Meridian Black Site, "a Fellowship privilege". It holds Project NULL: a Type: Null in a containment cradle that reacts to the PC exactly the way the badge case does. Quent: "It's modeled on something. We're not allowed to say what." The PC can leave with the Type: Null (Bond event). It's an unsettling gift. - Neither: a short scene of refugees climbing the Lift Road from Nadir with everything they own.

Arrival: Elowen, second visit (escalation). - Verdance is drowning in flowers. The Meadow Plate has no Warden, and its Imprint has burst its banks. Roses grow through cobbles, vines pull down gutters, and pollen hangs so thick the Pokémon Center hands out masks. It is beautiful and horrifying. - Under the bloom, the soil is grey. Nothing rots. - Three claimants for the Meadow Plate: 1. Queen Maelis has sent her Thornguard to occupy the Heartgarden: "the Plate lies on Crown soil." 2. Meridian offers "temporary custody for public safety". Prince Caedmon smiles beside the Meridian delegate. 3. Rowan's sealed Warden's testament, opened by Valen, names Juno Hale heir. - Valen presides from the Heartgarden steps. Juno is terrified. "I didn't want this seat. I wanted him in it." - Tamsin has come up from Briarwick to stand with Juno, the first small-town Warden to join what the press is calling the Greenward. - If :ch2_tamsin was "keep secret", Thistle's cell appears to heckle Tamsin ("You knew"), and the PC must defuse it.

The Bride Tree. - In the Heartgarden stands the Bride Tree, a flowering tree in a woman's shape, arms raised, in permanent blossom. Juno tells the PC the truth: it's her Aunt Elin, Fixed in AF 288. Rowan watered her every dawn. - Juno: "Sometimes I think she's listening. Isn't that stupid?" - If the PC carries Venam's Testimony (cameo sidequest, Ch7), they can play it for her under the tree: "I could hear everything. Everyone who sat with me." Juno sits down in the petals and talks to her aunt for the first time in years. (This sets up the Good ending's waking. Without it, Corvin's Ch7 line carries the same idea, more quietly.)

Juno's Warden trial (set piece). A Warden is confirmed when the Plate accepts them. Juno must walk to the Meadow Plate at the Bride Tree's roots, in the Bride Grove (708). The grove is choked with bloom and Bleed-adjacent, so the PC must clear the way inside the Veil, then guide Juno along the one safe path. - Ogerpon: in the Grove the PC meets the "Gossamer Ogre", Ogerpon in its mask. It has been guarding the Bride Tree since AF 288, which is why nothing ever got close enough to harm Elin. - Boss battle, "The Loyal Three" (multi-bar, a triple-boss sequence). Okidogi, Munkidori and Fezandipiti come for the Plate. Their collars glow with poison (Sweetrot-pink), and they're thralls of something in the Lightless Deep. (Pecharunt seed. Their eyes are the same blissful blank as Corvin's addicts.) - CHOICE :ch10_ogre: the Three present themselves as the heroes of the shrine story, and the Ogre roars at the PC. - Stand with the Ogre (OGERPON_TRUSTED): Ogerpon fights alongside the PC in the boss sequence as a partner-battle ally. It later joins Juno, and it appears at the Festival. - Stand with the Three: the "official story" choice. See GO_LOYAL. - Hesitate: the Ogre fights alone, the battle is harder, and Ogerpon leaves the Grove afterward, distrustful. - Game over GO_LOYAL "Loyal". Trigger: side with the Loyal Three. Foreshadowing: Celestine's Ch2 rhyme ("who was the thief"), the Mourner's Ch3 line ("ask why the Loyal Three wore poison on their collars"), and the poison collars themselves. The Three seize the Meadow Plate and their poison runs into the Bride Tree's roots. Epitaph: "You believed the story everyone told. So did the people who wrote it."

Gym 9: J3 (:FLOWER_GARDEN, 5 growth stages). Thematic note: a garden you grow during the fight; the stages rise with Juno's confidence. Juno, newly confirmed, with pollen in her hair:

"I didn't want this seat. I wanted him in it. But the city's drowning in flowers and nobody else is coming, so. Hi. I'm the Warden of Verdance. Please don't go easy on me — I'll cry either way."

Her ace is Tsareena. If :ch7_ledger was "said his name", her mid-battle line is "You knew before me. You knew." Otherwise it's "He lied to me. Every summer."

Aftermath and revelation. - Juno's first act as Warden: she refuses Meridian's Lattice and orders the Thornguard out of the Heartgarden in front of the Queen. The Queen is coldly amused: "Little gardener. We'll see how long you bloom." - Queen Maelis throws open the Royal Chase (Safari-style reserve) to the public "as a gift to the people in troubled times" (PR, but it's a fun area). Here, in her private garden, she speaks to the PC and is shrewder than she looks: "Every Sovereign since Maren has died tired. Did you know? I've buried four. Ask yourself why the Accord needs a new champion so badly this year." - CHOICE :ch10_succession_stance (asked by the press on the Heartgarden steps): "Should the Plates belong to the kingdoms, the Accord or Meridian?" Answer Wardens/Accord, the kingdoms or nobody. This is the first explicit "nobody" option, and it sets a key the Return path checks.

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "What Are You For?" (Melia). See design/CAMEOS.md. In the newly opened Royal Chase, a blonde girl with a Togekiss is looking for a friend who "fell through a sewer". The quest pays off extra if the Venam quest is done.

Cliffhanger: Juno, alone that night, writes a letter: "Dear Uncle Rowan." She can't finish it. The pages she throws away bloom where they land.

CHAPTER 11 — "The Thaw" (R19 → R20 → Frostfang Pass → Rimeholt, Gym 10; Kyogre)

Location: R19, R20 (:SNOWY_MOUNTAIN), Gossamer Heartwood (Silkwood revisit), Frostfang Pass, Rimeholt (Isenmar, :FROZEN_LAKE), Lake Rime.

Travel: Juno travels with the PC. She is lobbying the northern Wardens for the Greenward and is nervous about it. She practices speeches on the PC's Pokémon.

Arrival: Isenmar, second visit (escalation). Rimeholt is timber and whalebone on the ice of Lake Rime, part of the frozen sea. Sled-roads, fishing huts and breath-smoke. Meridian heat-towers keep the city alive, and the ice under them is grey and thin. Fishers' holes near the towers don't refreeze. Old men sing the Sleeping Whale song more often than usual. - Warden Anja Sorrel is blunt, pragmatic and loyal to her brother, the Thane, who signed the kingdom's heat contract. She tells Juno to her face that southern idealists can keep their principles: "You want me to choose between my principles and my grandchildren's toes? Easy choice. Toes."

Gym 10 (:FROZEN_LAKE). Thematic note: ice that remembers every weight; crack the wrong tile and the field changes. Her ace is Mamoswine. After the battle, the Rimeholt ice-climbers give Rock Climb and the ice-fisher gives the Super Rod.

The Thaw (set piece). 1. At night, the heat-towers overdraw. The PC and Juno see the readings on a technician's console: red and climbing. 2. The ice breaks. A quarter of Rimeholt's lower town starts to sink. The field transitions :FROZEN_LAKE → :WATER_SURFACE mid-battle. 3. Partner battle: PC + Juno vs Directorate officers trying to "secure the Lattice" while people drown. 4. Then the lake breathes. Under the broken ice, something vast and blue opens an eye. - Prompt: Fight it now / Get to the towers. Anja has told the PC all chapter: "When the lake breathes, you run." - Fight it now triggers Game over GO_WEATHER "Weather": the sea doesn't notice you. - Get to the towers is a sprint across breaking ice to the heat-tower platform, where the fight becomes winnable. 5. Boss battle: Kyogre (multi-bar). It wakes in Primal rain that floods the lower town further. - The PC fights it on breaking ice. Mid-fight, the shard resonates, and on a Harmony lean the boss's final bar "calms" instead of being knocked out. - Kyogre recognizes the PC: the Field Notes entry later says "it looked at me as if I were very small and very familiar". - It sinks back to sleep, but the sea is no longer fully frozen. 6. Game over GO_THIN_ICE "Thin Ice". Trigger: mid-Thaw, a Meridian technician begs the PC to retrieve the Lattice's data core ("It's proof! It'll prove who's to blame!"). The PC goes back for it. Foreshadowing: Anja's "Ice remembers every weight", and the cracking-tile tutorial in her gym. Epitaph: "You went back for the proof. The lake kept it, and you."

Aftermath and revelation. - Anja stands on a fishing hut's roof in the rain, watching her city sink: "I read the numbers. I read them. And I signed anyway, because the heaters were warm." - CHOICE :ch11_anja: - Expose her to the Thane's court in the Holdfast's great hall with the console readings (ANJA_EXPOSED). Anja is stripped of her honors, though Wardens can't be removed by kings. Accord Standing goes up and Meridian is blamed loudly. In Act V she refuses to release the Icicle Plate: "Rimeholt drowns without it." - Let her confess herself before the court. She becomes a key Act V swing vote, and at the end she lets go. - Either way, Anja breaks with Meridian publicly. - Thane Eirik declares martial law and evacuates the lower town south into Elowen, which causes a border crisis with Queen Maelis's Thornguard. - Juno, wet through and shaking: "That's what Uncle Rowan meant. Isn't it. The flowers. The heaters. It's all the same thing."

Transit (segment 10): Ebbmouth, Isenmar second visit. R21/R22 and the Ebbmouth Sea Grotto (revisit) pass back through Ebbmouth: - The Ebbmouth Strike. Marisol leads the Ebb Guild in cutting Meridian's tidal Lattice with dockworkers' saws. The Ebb runs both ways again for the first time in a year, and the whole city cheers on the quay. - If TEO_SAVED: Teo is there with glassed fingers, organizing. - If not: Teo's photograph is nailed to the strike's banner pole, and Marisol delivers the "He was nineteen" line here instead. - CHOICE :ch11_strike: when the strikebreakers arrive at the picket: - Stand: a tag battle with Marisol. Her Bond rises a lot, and more if Teo died. This is the only Act III way back to her after Teo's death. - Mediate: talk the Directorate foreman down. It's peaceful and polite, and Marisol notes that you didn't pick a side.

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Crystal Sleep" (Alexandra). See design/CAMEOS.md. In the Thaw's aftermath, a tall woman with a Latias is sitting among Rimeholt's half-Fixed fishers, asking whether they can hear her. Her mother sleeps in crystal in another world.

Cliffhanger: Cloudstair (Rock Climb) and Stillwater (728, a Bleed zone where a waterfall hangs frozen in mid-fall without being ice). On the climb, a Gale Post airship passes overhead, trailing a banner from Skyreach: "LARCH SPEAKS TONIGHT."

CHAPTER 12 — "Dead Letters" (Cloudstair → Skyreach, Gym 11; K5; the Larch Papers)

Location: Skyreach (Zenith's airship spire, :STORMFRONT), the Gale Post sorting halls, the dead-letter archive, Stormwatch Spire (the relay crown).

Arrival: Zenith, second visit (escalation). - Skyreach is a vertical city of docking masts and skybridges climbing into the Stormfront, the Sky Plate's permanent storm wall. It now has a Mandate curfew: searchlights from 9pm, and Directorate airships patrolling the masts. - Larch is here, hiding in plain sight in the Gale Post sorting halls as a guest of Warden Odessa Gale (goggles, scarf, irreverence). Odessa: "Skyreach's got one law: the wind doesn't care. Everything else is negotiable." - A black dragon is glimpsed in the Stormfront's lightning by airship crews: "the Stormfront Wyrm." (Zekrom seed. Larch, softly: "That's impossible. That's the shape of the Spire's core.")

Gym 11 (:STORMFRONT), a double battle. Thematic note: wind lanes that redirect moves and Pokémon between rounds. Odessa: "Hold onto something. No, really." Her ace is Corviknight. The sky-river guides give Waterfall.

The Larch Papers. - At midnight, Larch broadcasts from Stormwatch Spire through the Gale Post's relays to every screen in Velmora. Her original AF 292 report shows Lattice extraction accelerating the Bleed: tide data, Rimeholt's ice, Cindervault's caldera, Lumen's cracked Plate. - "I designed the Lattice. I was wrong. I'm telling you now because nobody else will." - It's electric, and the realm hears it.

The raid. 1. Within the hour, Director Marchetti's Directorate storms the Gale Post. 2. A bomb tears through Stormwatch's relay mast, and the broadcast dies mid-sentence. Meridian's news cycle has the story by dawn: "Hollow sympathizer Larch bombs Skyreach relay." 3. The bomb was Kroll's (Special Accounts). The PC finds a Splitter casing in the wreckage identical to Lumen's (Key Item Splitter Casing, useful in Ch13). 4. Meridian admin battle: Marchetti (MERIDIAN_MARCHETTI) on the sorting-hall catwalks. She's cold and professional: "You're not under arrest. You're under protection. Learn the difference before it's taught to you." 5. Odessa gets Larch out on a Gale Post skiff through the Stormfront, to Cragholm ("I've got friends who live underground"). 6. The PC's Meridian Fellowship is cancelled that night, which they learn from a Mart clerk's apologetic terminal ping: "Fellowship status: SUSPENDED."

K5: the dead letters. - In the chaos, Kestrel breaks into Odessa's dead-letter archive, the undeliverable mail of a decade. He now wears Meridian's sponsor jacket and a Resonance Gauntlet on his left hand. Voss has promised him "the full AF 290 file" in exchange for one bundle of letters. - The PC catches him among the shelves (K5). The Gauntlet makes his Pokémon hit unnaturally hard: field effects surge whenever he attacks (visual tell only). - After the battle he flees with the bundle. One letter falls. The PC reads it:

"I. — You must not let the child come back. Not for my sake, not for anything. If Valen learns what lodged in that little chest, he will put a crown on it. Keep wilting, love. Keep them small and safe and far. I'll carry the name. — M." - CHOICE :ch12_letter: - Keep the letter: the PC holds it, and it becomes leverage or a gift in Ch15. - Send it to Kestrel via the Gale Post: Kestrel Bond up, and K6's opening line changes ("You sent me a letter. Hers."). - Burn it: Resonance shifts toward Ruin (−2). Ysolde later says, "So you chose not to know. That's a choice too." Odessa finds the ashes in her archive: "Dead letters are the last thing some people ever said." In Act V she won't release the Sky Plate.

INTERLUDE III — "Dead Letters" (playable as Kestrel). - Meridian Spire, Voss's office in the small hours. Kestrel hands over the bundle. - Voss is kind, attentive and paternal. He makes Kestrel tea in a chipped mug. He gives Kestrel a folder marked AF 290, and it's real, mostly: it proves Valen scapegoated Morwen. It omits Meridian's array entirely. - The player can walk the office: a photo of young linesman Voss, a model of the new Prism Arena, and a gold sunburst pin on the desk shaped exactly like a lock. - Voss's line: "Your mother was destroyed by a man who thinks he's holding up the sky. I'd like to help you take the sky off him." Kestrel signs the Festival sponsorship contract. - Game over GO_FINE_PRINT "Read the Fine Print". Interlude-only, Kestrel's game over. Trigger: Kestrel opens Voss's private desk drawer when Voss steps out (he's warned once by Calla's glance). Inside is a file on the PC titled UNIMPRINTED-01: KEYSTONE. Voss returns: "Ah." Epitaph: "Kestrel Vane was reported missing the next morning. Meridian offered a reward."

Cliffhanger: On R23, leaving Skyreach, the PC sees their reflection in a puddle move a half-second late.

CHAPTER 13 — "Behind the Glass" (R23 → R24 → Mirror Mere → Antumbra Threshold → Lucent, Gym 12; the tribunal; the Fault)

Location: - R23 (:STORMFRONT), R24, Mirror Mere (742). - Antumbra Threshold (743, :INVERSE), Lucent (Antumbra, :MINDSCAPE), the Lucent Athenaeum. - Stormwatch Spire (optional revisit), Shattered Sky (748).

Crossing the Glass: Antumbra, first visit. - At Mirror Mere, Archseer Imre Dask of the Veiled Synod meets the PC. He has two different-colored eyes and speaks in mirrored sentences: "Welcome, behind. Behind, welcome." He grants passage because Ilsa sent for the PC. - His one rule: never look back through the Glass once you've crossed. - Game over GO_LOOKING_BACK "Looking Back". Trigger: on the Threshold, the Mere shows Juno calling the PC's name from the other side, and the PC turns. (A prompt: "Look back? Yes/No.") Foreshadowing: Dask's rule, stated twice and written on a Threshold signpost in mirror-writing. Epitaph: "You looked back. Now you're the reflection, and something else is wearing your face to the Festival." - The Antumbra Threshold is :INVERSE. Rivers run uphill, shadows fall toward lamps, type matchups flip, and wild Ghost, Psychic and Dark Pokémon are everywhere. - Every reflection the PC passes contains a shadow with six black wings, watching (Giratina seed).

Arrival. Lucent exists twice: the living city of white colonnades, and its upside-down reflection in the sky where the Mind Plate lives. The Athenaeum is the Accord's archive and court. - Provost Ilsa Marr wears earmuffs against other people's thoughts. "You're thinking very loudly. Please stop." - She hosts Rowan's tribunal here. The Accord insisted on its own court, and the transfer from Meridian custody is a political fight Valen won narrowly.

Gym 12 (:MINDSCAPE). Thematic note: the arena reshapes itself to the challenger's memories. Her ace is Hatterene.

The Mindscape (set piece; the PC speaks). After the badge, Ilsa offers what she's been dreading: "Someone sealed a memory in you. Not you. A Ghost-type did, a long time ago. I can open it. I won't enjoy it." - The PC enters the Mindscape: a dream-version of Lower Crownhaven, AF 290, all bells and Festival banners. - The PC's five-year-old self stands holding a Choir ribbon. This is the first authored PC dialogue.

Child: "Mum's singing. Do you want to see the light? It's going to hurt." PC (CHOICE :ch13_remember): "Show me." / "I'm scared too." / "...Not yet." - "Show me" or "I'm scared too": the fragmented memory plays. - Ines singing in the Choir. - The Crown blazing on a white-robed man (Valen). - Strange brass machines behind the Choir loft, with Meridian sunburst plates. - A crack. Light. Pain in the child's chest. - A woman in a black Ghost Warden's veil (Morwen) throwing herself over the child. - A man's voice: "Find the child." - Then darkness, and the shape of a Mismagius closing the memory like a curtain. - "Not yet": Ilsa respects it. The memory plays in Ch15 instead, in the Cathedral echo, harder. - Ilsa, afterward, white-faced: "The man in white was my father." She has known for eleven years. "The truth is a weapon. I've kept it sheathed. Don't mistake that for mercy." (This seeds her "Little Owl" sidequest.)

The tribunal (set piece). In the Athenaeum's Glass Court, Valen presides. Wardens attend, and the kings' observers sit in their gallery. Rowan is gaunt from Meridian custody, with his right hand still Bleed-burned in a lightning pattern.

  1. In attendance: Hierophant Ellery, with the Eevee Octave as her honor guard. Vesper's second encounter is an optional sparring double battle in the Athenaeum cloister. She is shaken by the Fault later in the chapter: "The Choir says we were born lucky. I don't feel lucky." Celestine refuses to swear the witness oath ("I can't lie anyway; it'd be rude to pretend").
  2. Rowan's statement: "I wanted to pull the nails. I was wrong about how. I was not wrong about why."
  3. Kaito's testimony. - CHOICE :ch13_kaito: before the session, Kaito asks the PC whether he should speak about Marrowgate. Encourage him (KAITO_TESTIFIED) or tell him it isn't his burden. - If he testifies: > "Marrowgate. Fifteen years ago. My orders. My unit. I sealed the gate. They were still knocking." - The Glass Court goes silent. Valen doesn't deny it. The Accord's moral authority cracks, and the kings' gallery erupts.
  4. The PC's testimony. - CHOICE :ch13_rowan_testimony: Condemn (Rowan is sentenced to the Pale Gaol, Juno's Bond drops, Accord Standing rises) / Plead for mercy (Rowan's Bond and Hollow Standing rise; he gets a lesser sentence, house arrest in Verdance under Juno, which he never serves because of the Fault) / Refuse to testify (neutral; Juno never asks why).
  5. Sable exposed. - With IRONREACH_EVIDENCE (the comm-device is required; the Skyreach Splitter Casing alone is only circumstantial, though it still lets the PC turn Marchetti in Ch14): the PC presents the comm-device. Ilsa reads the court's minds, confirms it, and speaks it aloud: "Sable is Sabine Kroll. Meridian Special Accounts." Uproar. Kroll, in disguise in the gallery, attacks. Admin battle: Kroll (MERIDIAN_KROLL), now fighting openly as Meridian. - Without the evidence: Ilsa reads it privately and tells only the PC. Kroll stays hidden. The public learns in Act IV, through Rook's leak, and believes it less.
  6. Voss's response on every screen within the hour: "If any Meridian contractor acted outside the law, they acted alone. I've ordered a full inquiry." His approval ratings rise.

The Fault. 1. That night, across the realm, the ground moves. 2. Under Cogsworth, Meridian test-fires the Arbiter Engine's first core. It is built around the Dark Stone and draws on all eighteen Plates at once. 3. A tremor runs through every Imprint. The Bleed leaps forward everywhere, and from Act IV on all early maps use encounter version 1 (Bleed-spread). 4. On the north coast, the sea pulls back from Zenith's cliffs overnight. The Sump drains, and the spires of drowned Aurelia stand naked under Underveil for the first time in three hundred years. 5. In the Athenaeum, the Glass Court's windows shatter. 6. The undercroft floods. The Glass Court's floor gives way into the flooded undercroft. Rowan is chained in the dock and the water is rising around him. The orrery's great brass ring has come down on Director Marchetti, pinning her. Wick's people are on the far side of the water. The PC can reach one. - CHOICE :ch13_fault (MAJOR, who lives): - Rowan: the PC breaks his chain. He escapes with Wick, and the Accord guard look the other way. Marchetti is dragged out by her own troops, maimed and unturned. Before he goes, Rowan finds the PC in the broken court: "Ask the Drowned Crown. It's awake now. So are you." - Marchetti: the PC heaves the ring off her. Rowan drowns in the dark under the court, still chained. Juno arrives in time to see Wick's people bring the body up, and her Bond drops sharply. Marchetti, bleeding, says only: "I owe you a life." She stands the Directorate down at the hijack. - Knock-ons if Rowan drowns (all later files): Teo leads the Lantern Hollow if he was saved, otherwise Thistle and Wick, and Rowan's Act IV–V lines go to whoever leads. "The Gardener" can't be drawn in the Festival R16. In Homecoming, Juno holds Elin alone under the first autumn. In the Hollow Spring, Thistle plants the first seed.

Shattered Sky (748, :BLEED, optional): the reflected Lucent in the sky has cracked. Pieces of the upside-down city hang between earth and sky. The PC can walk among them and find the first Zygarde Core (Zygarde Cell quest hook).

Cliffhanger: Ilsa finds the PC at dawn on the Athenaeum steps, among the shattered glass. She hands over a sealed envelope. "Father's Secret Articles. The parts the Accord doesn't teach. Don't read them yet. Go and find Bastian Ore first. He was there." Behind her, in every broken pane of glass, six black wings fold and unfold.

END OF ACT III. Title card: IV — The Drowned Crown.

Story Bible 07: Act IV — The Drowned Crown (Ch14–17, Gyms 13–16)

Tone: Desolation. Truth arrives in layers, and every layer costs something. A massacre the player can't stop, a rival's world shattering, a beloved mentor's complicity, a villain who is reasonable. Keep one warm scene per chapter (a meal, a joke, a Pokémon moment) so the dark has contrast. Conventions as in 04. Map names per WORLD.md (segments 12–15). From the start of Act IV, revisited early maps use encounter version 1 (Bleed-spread). If Rowan drowned at the Fault (:ch13_fault = marchetti): every Rowan line in Acts IV–V goes to the Lantern Hollow's leader instead: Teo if TEO_SAVED, otherwise Thistle with Wick. Juno's scenes reference her uncle's death. Revision 6: this act carries beats of the Ashkaar–Isenmar War and the Blessing. See 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md (timeline table) for the inserts per chapter; that file wins on conflicts.

Act-opening state (after the Fault): - The Bleed has leapt forward everywhere. Briarwick's Glasswood has reached the looms. The Lowlight's glass has spread a block. Saltmere, never once touched by Bleed, has a glass puddle on the promenade, and Dorian has roped it off as "our first miracle, darlings". - News screens show the north coast: the sea pulled back from Zenith's cliffs, and drowned Aurelia's golden spires standing in the mud below Underveil. - The Concert of Regions announces it will decide on Severance at the Festival.

Revision 7 (comedy): each chapter here has a scripted funny scene in 15_COMEDY.md §4 (Ch14–17), plus running gags (§3) and optional kingdom bits (§5). Place each scene where its heading says, and never inside the beats §1 lists as joke-free.

CHAPTER 14 — "The Deep Seams" (R25 → R26 → Geode Hollow → Cragholm, Gym 13)

Location: R25 (:INVERSE, Antumbra exit), R26 (Waterfall, a mirror exit into Ossaria), Geode Hollow (:CRYSTAL_CAVERN), Dreaming Quarter (768, :BLEED: a mining street where the sleeping Fixed's dreams play as mirages), Cragholm (Ossaria), the deep seams.

Arrival: Ossaria, second visit (escalation). - Cragholm is a canyon wall of lamplit galleries and lift-cages. Prince-Elector Kassan has sold its seams to Meridian. Linesmen and Directorate guards stand at the main lifts, and Meridian's new Lattice-drill chews into the Stone Plate's crystal halls. - The miners are sullen and terrified. Many of them are refugees: Isenmar families from the Thaw, and Nadir families fleeing Mandate raids. - Warden Bastian Ore (74, a miner's lamp on his hat, pretending to be deaf to Directorate questions) greets the PC like a grandson: "Ilsa sent you? Sit down, I'm telling you about the crown." - Pip Ore (14), his granddaughter, runs lamp-errands and is the fastest talker in Ossaria. She's the PC's guide through the deep seams.

The deep seams (hidden-refuge sequence). Below Meridian's level, Bastian shelters everyone the Accord and Meridian would rather didn't exist: - Professor Larch, smuggled in by Odessa. - Rimeholt refugees. - Lantern Hollow faithful: Rowan, gaunt (or their new leader, if he drowned), with Wick and Thistle. - Corvin's field hospital, if CORVIN_FREED. That flag comes from the Act III side quest "The Doctor's Cell", available Ch11–13: Corvin was arrested under the Mandate for treating Hollow wounded, and the PC can free him from a Directorate holding barge on Lake Sallow. If he wasn't freed, the hospital is run by a frightened Wenlow alone, and Corvin's absence is felt.

Anja arrives on the second day with a column of Rimeholt refugees, frostbitten and furious at herself. If she was exposed in Ch11 she won't meet the PC's eyes, and if she confessed she shakes their hand. This is her second encounter before the Festival.

Warm scene: a communal meal of date-bread and miners' stew. If Rowan lives, he and Juno (if she's visited) don't speak, but they sit at the same table. If he drowned, Juno sits with Thistle, who was with him at the end.

Bastian's account.

"I've seen three Festivals, two Sovereigns and one crown break. AF 290. I was carrying the Stone Plate in the procession. I was nearest the Choir loft. There was brass behind the loft that had no business in a church, and men in white coats with little gold suns on their pockets, watching dials. When the Crown cracked, the dials jumped. They weren't surprised. They were pleased. Then Valen said 'find the child', and Morwen Vane threw herself over a little thing in a Choir ribbon, and the next week Morwen was ruined and nobody ever asked me what I saw. I never told. I was afraid. I'm still afraid. I'm telling you anyway."

Gym 13 (:CRYSTAL_CAVERN). Thematic note: light through crystal; darken it and the whole field turns. (:CRYSTAL_CAVERN → :DARK_CRYSTAL.) Bastian: "Rock's patient. Rock'll wait a thousand years. I'm only seventy-four, so I'll wait about ten minutes." His ace is Gigalith.

Larch and the badge case (revelation). Larch takes the PC's Meridian badge case apart on a miner's bench. Inside the lining is a sliver of Lattice-glass and a transmitter. - "Every badge you've won, it's recorded your — whatever it is in you — resonating with that Plate. And it's been sending it to Meridian. Voss isn't watching you. He's tuning you." - CHOICE :ch14_telemetry (major; it feeds the endings): - Destroy it. Voss loses the stream. In the finale the Engine is under-calibrated, but Voss knows immediately and moves faster (Directorate pressure in Ch16–17). - Feed it false data (Larch's plan, TELEMETRY_SABOTAGED). The PC keeps the case and Larch rigs it to send corrupted resonance. Voss's Engine is miscalibrated in the finale, which is the strongest single weakening. It's risky: every remaining badge triggers a tense "did he notice?" beat. - Leave it. It keeps working. This is the road to the Bad ending if other weak points also fail. - Game over GO_WILLING "Willing". Trigger: Dr. Quent arrives with the raid and offers to "remove the faulty device painlessly, and give you a full Fellowship medical". The PC agrees. Foreshadowing: Larch's flat instruction a scene earlier, "Never, ever let them examine you." Epitaph: "The exam was painless, as promised. You've been asleep since. Meridian sends flowers."

The raid. 1. Marchetti's Directorate raids the seams, hunting Larch and Rowan. 2. Admin battle: Marchetti in the Darkglass gallery (:DARK_CRYSTAL after the lamps are shot out). 3. Thistle defects openly to the PC's side, earlier and warmer at high Hollow Standing. Her Pokémon hold the lift-shaft while refugees escape. 4. CHOICE :ch14_marchetti: after her defeat, the PC can show Marchetti the Splitter Casing or the Ironreach comm-device: "Your own Special Accounts bombed Skyreach." With either item, MARCHETTI_TURNED becomes possible. She doesn't turn now. She just goes very still, and in the Festival hijack she stands down. Without the evidence, she remains loyal.

Transit (segment 13), Ossaria: Duneholt Undersands and Groudon. - On the road to Gravenmoor, the PC passes back through Duneholt. The aquifer has gone, the great well is a dry shaft, and Meridian's deep drill has punched through into something hot. - Duneholt Undersands (783, :DESERT) opens. - Boss battle: Groudon (multi-bar), woken by the drills, in a chamber of Unown glyphs that record Theodric's "Year of Two Weathers". - Sahar is with the PC, and she reads the glyphs mid-fight (story text between bars): "The King woke the red one and the blue one to remake the land. When they would not obey, he turned his eyes to heaven." - After the battle, Groudon sinks back into the earth. The aquifer starts, very slowly, to refill. This is the first good news in an act. - Stonesleep (788): the fossil beds exposed by the collapse. Rare fossils go to the Dig Lab.

Cliffhanger: At the Antumbra border on R28, Kestrel's Meridian airship passes overhead toward Gravenmoor. Pip: "That's a sponsor ship. Who'd sponsor a graveyard?"

CHAPTER 15 — "The Hollow Cathedral" (R27 → R28 → Gravenmoor, Gym 14; K6; Vane Hall; Giratina)

Location: R27 (:MOUNTAIN), R28 (Antumbra moors, fog), Gravenmoor (Antumbra, :HOLLOW_CATHEDRAL), Vane Hall (haunted house), the Lantern Hospice, the barrows, then on the way out Barrow of the Wardens and Giratina's Reverse Depths (806, :INVERSE), The Hush (808).

Arrival: Antumbra, second visit. Gravenmoor is fog, barrows and lanterns. The Spooky Plate makes the dead linger as echoes: a woman sweeping a step that isn't there, two children playing a game that ended in AF 211. - Archseer Dask has come to Gravenmoor himself (his second encounter, needed for the Ruin Route): "The echoes are louder. Louder, the echoes. Something behind the Glass is listening back." - The Veiled Synod's lantern-priests are frightened. Since the Fault, the echoes have been getting louder, and some have started to notice the living. - Warden Ysolde Ashgrave, lace and veil, lantern always lit, greets the PC softly: "The dead aren't gone here. They're just very bad at conversation." She knows who the PC is. She has been waiting for them, and for Kestrel.

Vane Hall (haunted house set piece, the sad one). - Kestrel has arrived on Meridian's ship and gone straight up the hill to his childhood home. The PC follows. - Vane Hall is dust-sheeted and haunted. The echoes are of his family: his grandmother younger and laughing, Morwen reading to a six-year-old Kestrel, Morwen at a desk writing a letter and crying. - A puzzle path (World/puzzle team) through rooms that replay the night of AF 290 from the household's side: a servant's echo saying "the Warden's not back yet", a clock stopped at 11:40. - Kestrel walks through it like a ghost himself. Short lines only. "That's my room. That's — don't touch that."

Gym 14 (:HOLLOW_CATHEDRAL). Thematic note: echoes of earlier turns replay; the dead fight beside the living. Ysolde: "Mind the candles. Some of them are people." Her ace is Chandelure.

The Cathedral echo (the central revelation of the game). 1. Kestrel's Resonance Gauntlet (Meridian-built) can amplify the Spooky Plate's echoes. Voss told him the full truth of AF 290 is "recorded in the Cathedral". He means to force the Plate to replay it. 2. Ysolde begs him not to: "Not like this. She made me swear — when you were ready—" 3. He does it anyway. The Hollow Cathedral replays AF 290 in full, through Morwen's eyes. The PC sees it whether or not they chose "Not yet" in Ch13. If they did, this is their first time, and it's harder. - The Crownhaven arena, bells, the Choir. Ines singing. - Behind the loft: brass machines, sunburst plates, and a young, eager Aurelian Voss in a white coat, watching the dials. - The Tuning begins. The Crown flares on Valen. A sound like a bell breaking. - A splinter of colorless light flies from the Crown, straight into the chest of a five-year-old in a Choir ribbon. - Morwen runs. She throws herself over the child as the Glassfall sweeps the lower stands, and forty people turn to glass around them. - Valen, through the light: "Find the child. Whatever it costs. Find the child." - Morwen, hiding the child in a vestry, to Ines: "Take them. Edda's boat is at the Landing at midnight. I'll carry the name. Go." - Morwen's Mismagius gently closes the child's memory like a curtain. - The echo ends with Morwen alone in the vestry, kneeling, saying to no one: "Kestrel. I'm sorry." 4. Silence. Then Morwen's echo turns, impossibly, and looks at the PC across ten years:

"Little one. You came back. I told her not to let you." It fades.

K6 (raw, furious). - Kestrel turns on the PC:

"My whole life for you. Do you know what they called me in school? The Glassfall boy. My grandmother spat on her grave. And she was saving YOU." - If :ch12_letter was "sent to Kestrel": he opens with "You sent me her letter, and you still came here." - The battle is K6. His Gauntlet surges with the Cathedral's grief. Mid-battle the field flickers to :BLEED for a turn, the Gauntlet overloading. - After K6: the PC speaks (authored line, CHOICE :ch15_kestrel). 1. "I'm sorry. I didn't know. I'd give it back if I could." Kind: Kestrel's Bond goes up a lot, delayed. He can't accept it now. 2. "I was five. She chose. Don't you dare make her choice my crime." Blunt: his Bond drops now, but it unlocks his Redeemed path in K8, because he respects being fought back. 3. "...Your mother saved my life. I'm going to make it worth it." The Resolve option: his Bond goes up, and Resonance leans Harmony. - Kestrel leaves without a word. Ysolde follows him to the moor's edge and gives him Morwen's Mismagius and Morwen's sealed letter. He takes the Mismagius and refuses the letter. Ysolde keeps it for him (it returns in the Festival SF).

Edda Morrow at Morwen's grave. In the barrows below the Lantern Hospice, the PC finds Captain Edda Morrow laying kelp-flowers on a plain stone marked M.V.

"I took you out on the night ferry. Brought you back on the last one. Tide's got a sense of humour." A beat. "She paid me in her Warden's ring. I never sold it."

She gives the PC the ring (Key Item Morwen's Ring; plot-relevant in Act V for Kestrel).

Game over GO_ECHO "Echo". In the Cathedral, after the replay, an echo of Ines keeps singing in the Choir loft. A prompt: "Stay and listen?" Choosing to stay three times triggers it. Ysolde warns that "echoes are a door that only opens one way", and the PC's own hand turns translucent on the second "stay". Epitaph: "You stayed for the song. Gravenmoor has one more echo now. It sings harmony."

Transit (segment 14), Giratina's Reverse Depths. - Leaving Gravenmoor through the Barrow of the Wardens, where generations of Wardens are buried with replica Plates, the PC finds a barrow-door that opens downward into the sky: the Reverse Depths (806, :INVERSE). - Giratina waits there. The Fault and the Gauntlet's abuse of the Spooky Plate have torn the Glass, and Giratina is furious and wounded. - Boss battle: Giratina (multi-bar; Altered, then Origin on the last bar). - After the fight it doesn't flee. It looks at the PC the way Kyogre did. Story text: it knows what you carry. It has been guarding the hole your god left for three hundred years. It lets the PC pass and closes the tear behind them. - The Glass has been leaking since the Fault, and Giratina's wound is why visitors from other worlds keep slipping into Velmora (the cameo sidequests, design/CAMEOS.md). Post-game: the Distortion Core. - Ruin Route trigger (only with a full Ledger, ruin_unlocked). A pane of black glass in the Depths shows Juno on the other side, calling the PC's name: the exact image of GO_LOOKING_BACK. The prompt appears again: Look back? (:ch15_look_back). - Yes: it no longer kills you. The reflection steps aside, and the PC walks through into story/12_RUIN_ROUTE.md (R1). - No: the main story continues, and the door never reopens on that save. - The Hush (808): a Bleed zone where sound doesn't exist. The PC can see their own footsteps as ripples. Atmospheric breather.

CHAPTER 16 — "Clear Air" (R29 → R30 → Cogsworth, Gym 15; the Arbiter blueprints; Zekrom; the Sump Purge begins)

Location: R29, R30 (:WASTELAND, Zenith's eastern waste), Cogsworth (Zenith's foundry district, :FACTORY), Strade Works, the Engine core chamber.

Arrival: Zenith, third visit (escalation). - Cogsworth runs three shifts. Smokestacks never stop, and clock-towers chime every quarter-hour. - Topside "Unity Rallies" fill the plazas. Voss on giant screens: "Order. Light. Unity." Directorate banners hang from every gantry. - Workers are tired and proud. Many are from Nadir, commuting up the lifts. They know something huge is being built and don't know what. - Rook Calder is here, off-camera and unshaven. Since the Lowlight he has been quietly collecting Meridian Spire security footage. He finds the PC in a workers' canteen: "I've got Sable on tape. With a Meridian keycard. I've had it for months. I was scared." - CHOICE :ch16_rook: - Leak it now: it's public, and Kroll's identity is confirmed realm-wide even without Ch13 evidence. Meridian fires Rook within the hour and blacklists him from the Spire, so he can't cut the grid in Ch21 and can't reach the Zap Plate to release it (unless Marchetti turns). - Hold it for the Festival: Rook stays inside, smiling for cameras. He leaks it at the worst possible moment for Voss (Ch20 opening ceremony), and he's still in position to cut Lumen's grid in the hijack (ROOK_CUTS_GRID).

Gym 15 (:FACTORY). Thematic note: conveyor-belt arena; machines you can jam or overdrive. Magnus Strade is immaculate, prosthetic hand, pocket watch. "Faith gave us three hundred years of the Bleed. Engineering will give us the next three hundred without it." His ace is Metagross. He gives Dive suits via the bathysphere works.

Infiltration: Strade Works (set piece). 1. With Rook's keycards, the PC and Rook infiltrate the deep foundry after the gym. 2. Partner battle: PC + Rook vs Marchetti + Quent on the casting floor. The field goes :FACTORY → :SCRAPYARD as the floor is wrecked mid-battle. 3. In the drafting vault the PC finds the Arbiter Engine blueprints (Key Item Arbiter Blueprints): - A ring of eighteen locks to be built into the new Festival arena. - A keystone cradle "to be recovered from site AURELIA". - The notation: "Calibration subject: UNIMPRINTED-01. Retention: indefinite." 4. The core chamber: the Dark Stone at the center of a cage of Lattice-glass. It has cracked open, and Zekrom is half-emerged and bound in golden cable. This was the Fault's test-fire. 5. Boss battle: Zekrom (multi-bar). Each bar breaks a containment cable, and the PC is freeing it, not beating it. On victory, Zekrom tears free through the foundry roof into the Stormfront. Story consequence: the Engine's original core is lost (ZEKROM_FREED), and Voss must reroute through Lumen's grid. 6. Game over GO_OVERTIME "Overtime". Trigger: during the core-chamber sequence, a prompt to "finish copying the blueprints" while the containment klaxon counts down from 3 (warned twice by Rook: "We go when the whistle goes, no heroics"). Epitaph: "You stayed past the whistle. Strade Works issued a statement about an industrial accident."

Magnus (choice). Magnus finds the PC in the ruined vault with the blueprints. He doesn't call security.

"I don't build weapons. I build tolerances. I was told it was a Plate stabilizer. I learned the truth two years ago and kept building, because the math was beautiful and the alternative was the Bleed."

  • CHOICE :ch16_magnus:
  • Expose him (MAGNUS_EXPOSED): the PC takes the blueprints to the Zenith Council via Councillor Sallow. Magnus is arrested, Meridian loses a board vote and some public trust, and the Engine's design leaks publicly. But nobody is inside to sabotage it later.
  • Appeal to his conscience (MAGNUS_KEPT_FAITH): "Will you let it take a child?" He says nothing. He winds his watch. In the finale, he disables the Engine's safety governor, which weakens Voss's binding: "No machine I have built will kill a child."

The Sump Purge begins. That night, every screen in Zenith shows "Operation Clear Air." - Since the Fault drained the Sump and exposed Aurelia, Meridian claims Nadir's lower districts are "lawless": Sweetrot gangs and Hollow terror cells. The Directorate descends into Nadir. - Topside watches it like weather. A Cogsworth worker beside the PC, quietly: "My mum lives down there."

INTERLUDE IV — "Clear Air" (playable as Wenlow, Corvin's apprentice). - Mirefen, the same night. The player controls Wenlow in the Infirmary as the Directorate comes up the stilt-walks. - Wenlow moves patients, hides Sweetrot addicts in the Quiet Ward ("They won't search the glass ones. Nobody ever looks at the glass ones."), and fights two officers with Corvin's spare Pokémon. - The last scene: Wenlow standing in the Ward's doorway between a Directorate squad and forty dreaming patients. "This is a hospital. You have to—" - Cut to black. A single gunshot sound-effect, and nothing else. - If CORVIN_FREED, Corvin is in Cragholm and learns by radio. If not, he's in a Directorate cell and learns from a guard. - This death cannot be prevented. It is the act's moral floor.

Cliffhanger: Dawn. The PC, Rook and the blueprints on a supply-lift heading down into Nadir. Through the grille below, smoke rises from Underveil.

CHAPTER 17 — "The Drowned Crown" (R31 → R32 → Seabed → Underveil, Gym 16; Aurelia; Voss)

Location: - R31 (:SCRAPYARD), R32 (:WATER_SURFACE, the drained north coast), R32 Seabed (:UNDERWATER, Dive). - Underveil (Nadir's heart, :NEW_MOON), the Moon Market. - Aurelia (824 :UNDERWATER / 825–826 :CAVE), the Sun Court. - Ironbloom (828, :BLEED: rust that flowers). - Branch map 838 Accord Archive (high Accord Standing).

Arrival: Nadir, third visit (aftermath of the Purge). - Underveil is a city of lanterns in the upper towers of drowned Aurelia, in permanent night under the Dread Plate. This morning it is smoke, broken lanterns and Directorate barricades. - Dozens are dead. Nadir's fighters have pushed the Directorate back to the lifts. Mother Gutter's Muk holds one barricade, and Lantern Hollow volunteers hold another. - Game over GO_INTO_THE_LINE "Into the Line". Trigger: at a barricade, a Directorate line advances on unarmed Nadir protestors. The PC is offered "Step between them?" Nyx's courier warns the PC twice, "Not you. You're the one thing they want alive. Don't make them choose." Epitaph: "They didn't choose. The bullet did. Meridian called it a tragic accident, and meant it."

Nyx Calloway. - Silk, smoke, a crescent-moon earring. She receives the PC in the Moon Market among sellers of secrets. - Today she isn't amused. She has been carrying bodies. "Underveil isn't on the Accord's maps, sweetheart. That's the point of Underveil. Apparently it's also why nobody comes when they shoot us." - Nadir declares independence from Zenith that afternoon, by Nyx's proclamation read from the Moon Market steps with every Sump Baron present. It's symbolic, defiant and legally meaningless, and everyone cheers.

Gym 16 (:NEW_MOON), an illusion double battle. Thematic note: nothing on the field is what it looks like; illusions mask both sides. Nyx: "Everything has a price. Yours just went up." Her ace is Zoroark.

Nyx's bargain. - CHOICE :ch17_nyx: Nyx has the Engine's arena schematics, Meridian's install plans for the Prism Arena. - Trade the PC's own secret (NYX_BARGAIN_TAKEN). Nyx gets to know exactly what the PC is, and she will use it. The PC gets the schematics, and in Ch21 Nyx cuts Meridian's comms precisely. - Refuse: Nyx shrugs and helps anyway in Ch21, sloppily, at a cost to herself (she's injured). - Nyx confesses, unprompted, after the gym: "Two years ago I sold Voss the way into the Sun Court. I told myself it was just a door. My family's kept that door shut for three hundred years. I'm the one who opened it."

Accord Archive branch (838, high Accord Standing only). Brann meets the PC in a sealed Coastwatch vault on R32. It holds the Concordance's Secret Articles in Maren's handwriting: the Tuning, and the Hymn of Return. Brann confesses everything here, gently and early (see below), and gives the PC Maren's original Hymn page.

Aurelia (dive set piece). 1. With Dive suits from Cogsworth, the PC descends from R32's drained shallows to the Seabed and into drowned Aurelia. - Sahar Ostrand leads the expedition. She's the expert, terrified and thrilled: "Three hundred years. I've waited my whole life." - Edda Morrow pilots the support boat. 2. Golden terraces lie under green water. Gilder machines are rusted into coral. The Gilded King's statues have had their faces chiseled off by his daughter's people. 3. The murals of the Sun Court tell the real Prismfall in full: the Blight, the Two Weathers, the Chain, the Calling, the god breaking itself, the city falling, and Princess Maren pulling a colorless shard from the wreck of the keystone cradle. 4. The keystone cradle itself sits in the throne room's center: a golden hand, open, the size of a door. The PC's shard resonates violently. The PC's chest glows through their shirt, and the drowned room lights up with eighteen faint colors. 5. Nyx, who came down after all: "Oh. Oh, darling. You're the missing piece." 6. The truth, named. Sahar reads the cradle's inscription: "Here rests the One that is every color and none." The PC carries a splinter of the Legend Plate, Arceus's heart. 7. Game over GO_THE_THRONE "The Throne". Trigger: an exploration prompt, "Sit on the Gilded King's throne?" Foreshadowing: Sahar ("Don't touch anything gold. Anything."), and the throne's armrests shaped like open hands. The cradle closes. Epitaph: "You fit perfectly. That was the problem the Gilders were trying to solve."

Aurelian Voss in the Sun Court. 1. A Meridian salvage team cuts in through the throne room's far wall, and Voss walks in among them in a pressure suit with his helmet off in an air-pocket. He's here to collect the cradle. He is delighted to see the PC. 2. The reveal, his way:

"House Aurel. My family. We changed the name and kept the grudge. I was eight when my grandfather showed me Theodric's notebooks. I was twenty-two when I realized he was right about everything except the chain. Metal was the wrong material." 3. He admits AF 290 plainly: "My array. My dials. I was trying to measure the convergence. I didn't know it would break the Crown. I was very sorry about the forty people. I was even sorrier when I understood what I'd learned." 4. On the Lowlight: "Twelve people. I've lost more to the Bleed in a quiet week. I made those twelve mean something." 5. His philosophy: "Gods are absentee landlords. Arceus dropped its furniture on our heads three hundred years ago and never came back. I'm not trying to become a god. I'm trying to make sure we never need one again." 6. His offer (CHOICE :ch17_voss_offer): "Stand with me at the Festival. When it's done, no child in Velmora will ever be Fixed again. Your mother wilted in exile because of a broken god. I can fix the god." - Refuse (always available). - Feign agreement (VOSS_FEIGNED): Voss lets the PC close in the finale, and the hijack has a different opening beat. It's risky, because if Meridian Standing is high and telemetry is intact, it tips toward Concord Dawn or the Bad ending. - Accept (VOSS_ACCEPTED): this opens the Concord Dawn ending path. Narrative Systems owns the consequences. 7. Meridian admin battle: Kroll (MERIDIAN_KROLL), or Quent if Kroll was beaten publicly in Ch13. They hold the PC off while Voss's team lifts the keystone cradle out through the wall. Voss leaves on a Meridian submarine. His last words: "Win your badges. I'll see you at the coronation."

Ysolde in Underveil. The Ghost Warden has come down the lifts with a cart of Gravenmoor lanterns, one for each of the Purge's dead, and she lights them along the Moon Market stairs with Nyx's people. She asks the PC one thing: "Is he eating? Kestrel. You'd know better than I would." (Her third scene before the Festival.)

Brann's confession (if not already given in the Archive branch). Above water, at Underveil's docks, Brann Holloway is waiting. He has come from Crownhaven on Valen's behalf. Confronted with everything, he tells the truth at last: - The Tuning is real. - The Crown kills Sovereigns. - He reset the Crown's stone after the Fracture and knew it was short, and never asked where the rest went. - Valen's arm is crystal to the shoulder. - "He has weeks. Maybe the Festival."

He delivers Valen's message:

"He says: 'Win your last two badges. Then come to me, and I'll tell you everything.'"

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Deliverer" (Amaria and Titania). See design/CAMEOS.md. Edda's divers report a woman swimming without a suit in drowned Aurelia's lower galleries, singing to a Lapras, and an armored knight pacing the drained shallows above, refusing to leave. After Gym 16, before the PC leaves Underveil.

Cliffhanger: Night on the Underveil docks. The PC, Nyx, Sahar, Edda and Rook (and Rowan, if he came down) sit on crates in the lantern-light. Nyx counts on her fingers:

"Meridian wants you in that arena. The Accord wants you in that arena. The Hollow wants you in that arena. Two kings want you as their champion in that arena. Thirty days, sweetheart. Every power in Velmora is waiting for you to walk into one room."

Edda: "Then we'd best make sure you walk in on your own feet."

END OF ACT IV. Title card: V — Sovereign.

Story Bible 08: Act V — Sovereign (Ch18–21, Gyms 17–18, the Festival of Champions)

Tone: Desolation-dark, then a hard-won dawn. Everything planted pays off. The Festival week should feel like the whole game is in one city: every Warden, every faction, every Bond. Mechanics ownership: the Festival draw, game-over IDs and ending triggers belong to story/10_CHOICES_ENDINGS.md and design/NARRATIVE_KEYS.md. This file gives the story content of every branch they select from. "Default" means the SPINE Rev. 2 path, the fallback. Map names per WORLD.md (segments 16–18, Crownhaven 480–519). Caps: R16 90 · QF 92 · SF 94 · Final 96 · Hijack 100 · True Finale 100. Revision 6: this act carries beats of the Ashkaar–Isenmar War and the Blessing. See 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md (timeline table) for the inserts per chapter; that file wins on conflicts.

Revision 7 (comedy): each chapter here has a scripted funny scene in 15_COMEDY.md §4 (Ch18–21), plus running gags (§3) and optional kingdom bits (§5). Place each scene where its heading says, and never inside the beats §1 lists as joke-free.

CHAPTER 18 — "Once Upon a Time" (R33 → Lightless Deep → R34 Gloamwood Verge → Gloaming, Gym 17; Pecharunt; K7; the Hymn)

Location: - R33 (:CAVE). - Lightless Deep (844 :DARK_CRYSTAL / 845 :NEW_MOON). - Inverted Ward (848, :INVERSE: an abandoned Choir hospital turned upside-down by the thinning Glass). - R34 Gloamwood Verge, Gloaming (Elowen's heart, :FAIRY_TALE), the Great Tree.

The Lightless Deep: Pecharunt (Nadir's legendary arc)

The road out of Underveil runs down, not up: below the drained Sump into the Lightless Deep, the oldest dark under the City Below. 1. Corvin (or Wenlow's notes, if Corvin was never freed) has traced Sweetrot to its source. 2. The Deep is a sweet-smelling cavern of pink crystal and docile, smiling thralls tending peach-shaped growths. Mother Gutter, the oldest Sump Baron, is here, blissed and centuries-old. She isn't the master. She's the first thrall. 3. Boss battle: Pecharunt (multi-bar). Its "mochi" poison spreads Sweetrot status-flavor across the field. 4. Defeating it frees the thralls across Nadir, and the Sweet Market's vendors blink awake in the street. 5. Lore payoff: Pecharunt enthralled the Loyal Three centuries ago and sent them to steal Elowen's Plates. The "Gossamer Ogre" drove them off. The pageants have had it backwards for 250 years. 6. If OGERPON_TRUSTED, Ogerpon appears at the Deep's mouth, waits for the PC and walks with them to Gloaming.

Gloaming: Elowen, third visit (escalation)

  • Thornguard checkpoints. Prince Caedmon (now effectively ruling Elowen, since his mother is "resting") has signed the Forest Compact. Meridian survey-flags line the Gloamwood. The Thornguard turns the PC back at the Verge "by royal order".
  • The dusk is spreading: Gloaming's twilight has crept south across half the forest, and Briarwick's looms now work by lamplight at noon.
  • Optional scene, Queen Maelis: in a dusk-garden she's "resting" in under polite guard. Shrewd and finally honest: "My son thinks the forest is a resource. I've been queen for sixty years. The forest is the sovereign. I'm just the gardener it tolerates." She gives the PC a Royal Writ (key item). It opens the Wardguard escort to Crownhaven even at low Accord Standing, and it seeds Elowen's epilogue: Maelis disowns the Compact.
  • Celestine (in her true form; the PC met her disguised in Ch2) dissolves the checkpoint with one sentence the Thornguard captain can't argue with, because she can't lie and everyone knows it: "If you stop this child, the forest will remember your name, and it will say it wrong forever."

Gym 17 (:FAIRY_TALE), a double battle: "the knight and the dragon"

Thematic note: the arena is a storybook. Choose your role (knight, dragon or thief), and the story's rules change who's strong. Celestine: "Once upon a time — which is the only time there ever is, here —" Her ace is Sylveon.

The Hymn of Return

In the dusk-chapel at the Great Tree's roots, Celestine gives the PC the complete Hymn of Return, sung for them by the Gloaming choir. If the PC received Wick's verse (Ch10) or Maren's page (Archive branch), the last verse visibly completes in the Key Item.

Eighteen we borrowed, eighteen we keep till the wounded land has learned to sleep; then one of no color, the eighteen roads walked, shall carry them home, as the old ones talked — not chained, not broken, not bought, not crowned: given back, as a gift is found.

Celestine: "I've been waiting for you for forty years, little colorless thing. Valen reads 'one of no color' as vessel. The Hollow read it as hammer. Meridian reads it as key. It says walker. I cannot lie. I can, however, sing. These are the verses I sing." (Her phrasing is exact and deliberate. On the Ruin Route she admits there's a verse she stopped singing.)

K7 (the Great Tree's roots)

  1. Kestrel, Meridian's sponsored Festival entrant with 18 badges, arrives on a Directorate skiff. He's here to stop the PC going to Crownhaven, not for Meridian.

    "They all want to use you. Valen wants a lock. Voss wants a key. Your Hollow friends want a crowbar. Stay out of Crownhaven and let me end it. I'll beat Valen in front of the whole world and make him say her name."

  2. K7 on the Fairy Tale field. Kestrel insists he's the knight and the PC is the dragon. If the PC's Bond with him is high, he flinches mid-battle.
  3. After the battle: - CHOICE :ch18_kestrel_ring: if the PC has Morwen's Ring (Ch15), give it to him now or keep it for later.
    • Give it now: Kestrel's Bond goes up, and his Redeemed path becomes more likely. He doesn't say thank you. He puts it on.
    • Keep it: the ring can be given at the SF instead, for a bigger moment if he's Redeemed.
  4. Prompt: Stay out of Crownhaven? (Yes / No.) Yes triggers Game over GO_STAY_OUT "Somebody Else's Story": the PC does what everyone has told them since the ferry, and the Engine takes Kestrel instead.
  5. On No, Kestrel leaves for Crownhaven: "Don't come. I mean it. ...You're going to come. Fine. Then don't lose before you reach me."

Optional thread: cameo sidequest "Dreamwalker" (Shiv). See design/CAMEOS.md. In Gloaming's dusk-chapels, a grinning stranger keeps appearing in the townsfolk's dreams. He says he's met the PC before, on a ferry.

Game over GO_FAIR_BARGAIN "Fair Bargain"

Gloaming's lantern-moth market offers bargains. One stall promises "the answer to your mother's last question". Accepting without asking the price triggers it. Celestine has warned the PC on arrival: "In Gloaming, always ask what it costs before you say yes." Epitaph: "The price was your name. You are very happy now, as a lantern-moth, and you will glow at every dusk."

CHAPTER 19 — "The Last Gate" (R35 Gloamwood → R36 → Wyrmscar Rift → Wyrmrest, Gym 18; Reshiram; Sigrun's trial)

Location: R35 Gloamwood (:FAIRY_TALE), R36 (:MOUNTAIN), Wyrmscar Rift (864 :CAVE / 865 :DRAGONS_DEN), Wyrmrest (Ashkaar), Rainbow Scar (868, :RAINBOW).

Arrival: Ashkaar, second visit (escalation)

  • Dominar Hrothgar is at the front with his legions (the Ashkaar Siege Camp, 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING). Wyrmrest is held by Sigrun's own riders. The Dominar means to march on Zenith "the hour the Festival ends", and he has ordered his daughter to close the pass to everyone Zenith-tainted. That includes the PC: Meridian's Fellowship, Meridian's badge case.
  • Sigrun Ashfall keeps the pass open anyway, in open defiance, standing on the gate in dragonhide with her Salamence. "Wyrmrest is the last gate. Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed. I do the weighing, Father. Not you."

The Dominar (optional political scene, DOMINAR_SWAYED)

  • The PC petitions the Dominar in his command pavilion at the Siege Camp, after the battle. Lies are a capital crime in an Ashkaar court, and the oath-brazier is lit.
  • Persuading him to fly for Crownhaven instead of against Zenith requires:
  • the Light Stone sent to Wyrmrest (:ch8_lightstone = wyrmrest) plus proof (the Arbiter Blueprints or the Larch Papers), or
  • Sigrun's Bond ≥ 4, so that she speaks for the PC (:ch19_dominar).
  • Success: "If the god is in chains, then dragons will break them. Not for the Accord. For the god." His legions are present in the hijack.
  • Failure: his riders go home after the truce and take no part in defending Crownhaven. (DOMINAR_SWAYED can't be set if Isenmar won the war without mercy terms.)

Sigrun's trial (Wyrmscar Rift)

  1. Sigrun won't let the PC challenge her until they walk the Wyrmscar Rift to the Draco Plate with her and speak aloud what they intend to do at the Festival.
  2. Sigrun's confession on the walk:

    "He trained me to be his successor. Then he told me I'd never survive the Crown, and I was relieved. Do you understand? Relieved. And then he went looking for you. I knew. For two years I knew, and I let it happen, because it meant it wasn't me."

  3. At the Rift's heart, at the brazier over the Draco Plate, the PC speaks. This is an authored line and also a soft commitment that the ending checks (Narrative Systems owns how much weight it carries). CHOICE :ch19_oath: - "I'm going to give them back." (Return) - "I'm going to keep them safe. Whatever it costs me." (Keep) - "I don't know yet. I'm going to find out with my eyes open." (Open) - (Only if Resonance ≤ −2:) "I'm going to end it. All of it." Sigrun draws her blade but lets them pass, horrified.
  4. Game over GO_LIARS_PYRE "Liar's Pyre". A fifth option is always offered: "Whatever you want to hear." Foreshadowing: the Ashkaar gate-guard's "say true or say nothing", and the brazier oath. The fire answers. Epitaph: "Ashkaar burns liars. It is the oldest law, and the dragons remember it even when men forget."

Reshiram (Ashkaar's legendary arc)

  1. Any true answer wakes the Light Stone, which has hung in Wyrmrest's Rift-shrine since Ch8 if it was sent there, or which arrives carried by a Hearthguild runner if it wasn't.
  2. Boss battle: Reshiram (multi-bar) on :DRAGONS_DEN. Its fire is the truth of the PC's oath: Return and Open lean Harmony, Keep is neutral, and Ruin makes the fight savage.
  3. After the battle, Reshiram bows to Sigrun, not to the PC: "the truth is hers to carry now." It flies to Crownhaven ahead of them.
  4. Sigrun, stunned: "...It chose the one who finally said it out loud."

Gym 18 (:DRAGONS_DEN)

Thematic note: the den's heat rises each turn, and the dragons grow stronger the longer you hesitate. Sigrun fights at her absolute best. "If you can't beat me, you can't survive him." Her ace is Salamence. On victory she gives the 18th badge, and the badge case sings: all eighteen tones together. - If the telemetry was left intact, a Meridian airship on the horizon turns toward Crownhaven within the minute. - If it was sabotaged, Larch's voice on the radio: "He bought it. God help us, he bought it."

Rainbow Scar and the road to Crownhaven

Rainbow Scar (868) is a Bleed zone where all eighteen Imprints bleed together into light. It foreshadows the Prism Arena. Then R37 and the Sovereign's Ascent: - At high Accord Standing or with the Royal Writ, the Wardguard escort takes the PC up the processional stair. - Otherwise, Edda's smugglers take them through the Glassfall Scar (888), the forty Fixed of AF 290, still in Lower Crownhaven where they fell. The PC walks among them. One of them is a Choir cantor with her mouth open mid-note who is not Ines; Ines got out. The PC finds the place where they stood at five.

CHAPTER 20 — "The Festival of Champions" (Crownhaven, Festival week)

Revision 4: no sister-game cameos appear anywhere in Festival week. They are optional sidequests elsewhere (design/CAMEOS.md). The bracket, draw pools and exhibitions use original characters only, following story/10_CHOICES_ENDINGS.md §3 and NARRATIVE_KEYS §6. Revision 5: every possible opponent is someone the PC has met at least twice by Act IV. Revision 6: the Festival is the ceremony of Arceus's Blessing. Entrants are kingdom delegations (two per kingdom, Valen, and the PC), chosen by player choices: see 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING.md Part A, which replaces the seed list here. The PC picks their colors on Day 0 (:ch20_colors). Companion-vs-companion matches can be watched as highlight cutscenes (13_CUTSCENES.md). The Coronation in Ch21 is "the Blessing", and Ch19's Dominar scenes play inside the Battle of the Shattered Ice.

Day 0: Arrival

  1. Crownhaven is white stone and bells in the caldera. Upper Crownhaven is festooned with Tamsin's eighteen banners, woven since Ch10. The Meridian Prism Arena gleams where the old grounds were, too new.
  2. The Consent display. By Day 1 each Warden's banner hangs white (they would let go) or grey (they won't). It is Tamsin's private reading of her colleagues, and the player can read it. These are the Consent conditions made visible (NS §6.2).
  3. Every faction is here: - Meridian runs security under a Council vote that Anja's swing vote couldn't stop. - The Lantern Hollow are disguised as Choir porters. - The kings are in the observers' gallery: Caedmon for Elowen, Eirik, the Dominar's herald, Kassan, Dask. - Envoy Delphine Okoro of the Concert, deciding on Severance.
  4. The Veil Gate stays shut. The Festival used to host champions of other worlds, until the Synod sealed the Gate in AF 230 (01_WORLD_LORE). Archseer Dask refuses Hollis's request to reopen it "for prestige": "The Glass is leaking already. I won't open a door in a wall that's falling down." No foreign champions come this year. Every entrant is someone the PC has met on the road (Revision 5).
  5. The Draw. The Hierophant draws lots from a crystal bowl at the Basilica steps. - Sixteen stand in the arena: the PC and fifteen kingdom delegates (10_CHOICES_ENDINGS §3.1; staging in 14_WAR_AND_BLESSING Part A). The war decides how many seats Isenmar and Ashkaar hold. - The PC's colors (:ch20_colors). Before the bowl, Queen Maelis, the Zenith Council and Nyx each offer the PC their colors, and Larch offers none: the old clause for "the Unsworn". Values elowen / zenith / nadir / unsworn (NARRATIVE_KEYS §4.3). - The lots are honest; the bowl isn't. The Accord's old customs (the Right of Grievance, yielded invitations, a Warden asking for a challenger's quarter, and Dorian, who has never once let the bowl ruin a good story) bend it on screen (NS §3.1).

Day 1: The Opening Ceremony (the 18 Plates)

  1. The procession.
