At dusk, eighteen Wardens descend the Sovereign's Stair into the Prism Arena, each carrying their Plate in a reliquary, in gym order to the counting rhyme sung by the Choir: - Tamsin (Insect) - Dorian (Blank) - Marisol (Splash) - Rook (Zap, still cracked, in a cradle; if Rook was fired in Ch16, a Meridian officer carries it, and Rook watches from the crowd) - Sahar (Earth) - Corvin (Toxic; carried by a Choir porter in his mask unless CORVIN_FREED) - Brann (Flame) - Kaito (Fist) - Juno (Meadow) - Anja (Icicle) - Odessa (Sky) - Ilsa (Mind) - Bastian (Stone, leaning on Pip) - Ysolde (Spooky) - Magnus (Iron; under guard if MAGNUS_EXPOSED) - Nyx (Dread) - Celestine (Pixie) - Sigrun (Draco)

Each sets their Plate into a pylon of the Concordance Ring. 2. The Tuning. Valen in white, the Crown on his brow, the grey glove off. The arena sees his crystal arm for the first time, and a murmur runs through forty thousand people. The Choir sings the half-Hymn. The Ring lights in eighteen colors, and the sky over Crownhaven ripples. 3. The PC's vision. The shard resonates violently. The world goes white. In the white, a vast shape of light with eighteen empty places in its wheel opens one eye:

"...Little light. You have carried me far."

The PC collapses in the stands. Valen sees. Ilsa, in the Warden ranks, takes her earmuffs off and flinches at her father's pain. 4. Rook's leak (if :ch16_rook = hold): the Spire footage of Kroll with a Meridian keycard plays on the arena's own screens, hijacked by Rook's Rotom. The crowd turns. Voss, in his box, doesn't blink: "Rogue contractor. The inquiry continues." Meridian's polling drops, and the Envoy takes notes.

Day 2: Round of 16 ("someone from your past")

Story staging for each R16 candidate (draw rules: NS §3.2): - Marisol, by Right of Grievance (she holds an Isenmar seat, Teo died, and her Bond ≤ 2). The old Accord custom lets a Warden claim a first-round match against a challenger who wronged their city, and the Hierophant reads it out. Marisol and Juno swap seeds: "Let's see you run past me." - Anja (she holds an Isenmar seat, and was exposed in Ch11). She invokes the same right, and she and Juno swap seeds: "You threw me to the court. Fine. Ice remembers." - Rowan, "the Gardener" (Rowan alive, mercy pleaded at the tribunal, Hollow Standing ≥ 4). Rowan enters masked under a false name. Juno gave him her own invitation: "One last garden, child. Then I'm done with masks." If Rowan drowned at the Fault, this candidate doesn't exist. - Juno (J4, fallback). Joyful and nervous under the arena's lights: "We started on the same dock. Let's see who's grown more." The fields cycle on :PRISM_ARENA. Her ace is Tsareena, with Ogerpon in her party if OGERPON_TRUSTED. If she's displaced by Marisol or Anja, she plays on in their seed (Isenmar's: against Nadir's second delegate, or against Ashkaar's), and her Garden Match exhibition opens when she's out. If she gave her invitation to Rowan, she watches from the Wardens' box.

After the match: the garden court. - If Rowan lives, the PC finds Juno and Rowan in the arena's garden court, sitting apart on the same bench. If Teo lived, he says it himself, holding up his glassed fingers: "I could hear everything while it had me. Everyone who sat with me." (If the PC carries Venam's Testimony from her optional sidequest, it can be played here instead.) Rowan sits down on the grass and cries, and Juno takes his good hand. It's the first time they've touched since Ironreach. - Without the testimony, Juno does it herself, more slowly: "Corvin says they dream. I'm going to believe him." - If Rowan drowned, Juno sits alone with Elin's pressed lily from the Bride Tree.

Other R16 results follow the companion match rules (NARRATIVE_KEYS §6), and each can be watched as a highlight (13_CUTSCENES.md). Story requirements: - Kestrel advances with the Gauntlet, over Zenith's first delegate (Rook or Odessa). - Valen and Sigrun advance.

Day 3: Exhibitions and the Festival Grounds

  • The Eevee Octave (Eeveelution gauntlet). Eight Glassfall orphans raised by the Choir, each beside a different Imprint, fight a back-to-back gauntlet in the Choir gardens, each fielding a different Eeveelution. It ends with leader Vesper Lindqvist's full Eeveelution team on a field that shifts to match whichever Eeveelution is active.
  • Vesper, gentle: "We were all born the year the Crown broke. The Choir says we're lucky. I think we're just... what was left."
  • Reward: a Prism Ribbon for Understudy. If Understudy has evolved, Vesper notes which Imprint "claimed" it.
  • Other exhibitions (NS §3.6, original characters only):
  • The Pageant Bout: Dorian and Rook in a double battle staged as "a duet for the cameras", with Rook's Rotom filming it.
  • Juno's Garden Match, if she wasn't the PC's R16.
  • The Ebb Guild match with Marisol while Teo heckles from the stands, if Teo was saved and Marisol is close.
  • Festival grounds side content: the Concordance Spire (Battle Tower) under construction, a teaser; street food from every kingdom; Dorian rehearsing in secret with Celestine.
  • Diplomacy with Envoy Okoro (:ch20_envoy).
  • Proof needs two of three: the Larch Papers, the Arbiter Blueprints, and testimony that the Fixed still dream (any one of: Teo's, if he lived; Corvin's Quiet Ward records, if he's free; Venam's Testimony from her optional sidequest). Together they show the Bleed is caused and so curable, not contagious. Okoro recommends against Severance (ENVOY_PERSUADED).
  • Plea: she listens and drafts Severance anyway, "with regret".

Day 4: Quarterfinal ("the honest view")

The story job is an honest view of the PC's choice from someone who isn't trying to use them, delivered in a hard fight at cap 92. Conditional candidates swap seeds with the fallback (NS §3.5). - Celestine (fallback, the headline). She confirms the Return, and it counts as an oath because she cannot lie: "I cannot lie, so listen while we fight." After the match:

"Give them back. I've waited forty years to say that to someone who could." - Sigrun (Resonance ≤ −3, or the PC helped Isenmar break her father's legions): "Dragons weigh things. You've gotten heavier." - Kaito (the oath was Keep): "You'd keep them? Marrowgate's still knocking." - Ysolde (Kestrel has fallen): "If I can't reach him, I'll find out what reached you." - Odessa (she holds a seat; five close Bonds; Harmony-leaning): "Everyone keeps telling you what you're for. The wind doesn't care. What do you want?" - (Ruin Route: Archseer Dask. See 12_RUIN_ROUTE.)

Other QF (default): Kestrel beats Kaito, whose Lucario felt it: "That isn't him fighting." Valen advances. Sigrun advances, unless she was drawn into the PC's quarter (then Celestine plays on in Ashkaar's seed).

Day 5: The Night of Masques, the audience, Little Owl

  • Corvin, privately, after treating Valen in the Sanctum: "He has weeks. Maybe. He's holding the Plates quiet right now, with his body, every hour since the ceremony. He'll fight you tomorrow on what's left."

The Night of Masques: "The Gilded King." 1. In the Basilica square, Dorian stages Ansel Crewe's banned play for the first time in 248 years. Celestine, who cannot lie, voices Princess Maren's lament, and the Saltmere Players wear the Hollow's ogre masks as the chorus. 2. The square watches the true Prismfall: the Blight, the chain, the god breaking itself, the city drowning, the daughter's penance. 3. Chancellor Hollis sends the Wardguard to stop it. Kaito (and, if alive, Teo's strikers) stand in the way, and the guard doesn't push. 4. When the actor playing the Gilded King says "If heaven will not kneel, we'll build a chain", every head in the square turns to the Meridian box. 5. Voss stands and applauds. Alone. Warmly.

"Bravo. They finally cast the king correctly."

Calla's defection (:ch20_calla, who lives). Only offered if someone inside is already moving (:ch16_rook = hold, or MAGNUS_KEPT_FAITH). - In the crowd after the play, Calla Wren finds the PC, shaking, still in her Meridian lanyard. She can get the arena's power schematics to Larch.

"I scanned you on the dock. I photographed the screen. I've been sending him your life for six months. Let me do one thing that isn't that." - Shelter her with Edda's boat. She brings the partial schematics she already has: one crack in the Engine. She lives. - Send her back for the full set: two cracks. Kroll finds her in the Spire stairwell, and she doesn't come back. The schematics arrive by Gale Post at dawn, with a smear of blood on the envelope. - Turn her away: "Then I'll just — go home, I suppose." She leaves, and nobody sees her again.

Valen's audience (the PC speaks). Valen summons the PC to the Sovereign's Sanctum: candlelight, a camp bed, his crystal arm resting on a cushion like something borrowed. He doesn't defend himself. He explains.

"Every ten years I bring the Plates home to Crownhaven, and every ten years Velmora endures. You noticed I never said 'thrives'. Nobody ever notices."

"Morwen didn't cause the Glassfall. I let them say she did, because if the world knew the ceremony could fail, the kings would tear the Plates out of the ground and we'd all drown. I chose a country over a woman's name. I'd do it again. I hate that I'd do it again."

"When you beat me tomorrow — and you will — I'll put the Crown on you. The splinter in your heart will make it whole. You'll hold the Plates quiet for a hundred years, and you won't age as fast as I did. You'll be... very lonely. I'm sorry. I have no other door."

The PC answers (authored line, CHOICE :ch20_audience): 1. "There's another door. The Hymn. We give them back." (Return) - Valen: "And let eighteen cities go dark? Rimeholt drowns, Cindervault freezes, Verdance's fields fail in a year..." - The PC can answer with the Hymn ("given back, as a gift is found") if they hold the complete version. - Valen, very quietly: "...Maren wrote that. I thought it was a poem." 2. "Then I'll wear it. Somebody has to." (Keep) Valen weeps, once. 3. "You don't get to decide that for me. Nobody does." (Refuse) Valen: "Good. Then decide it yourself, tomorrow. That's all I ever wanted, and I never had the courage to want it out loud." - If the PC refuses with a cold Bond, Ilsa never came, and the telemetry still runs, this sets VALEN_COLLAPSED. Voss's pre-ignition drains him overnight, and he can't stand for the Final.

Game over GO_EARLY_CROWN "Early Crown". Valen offers, desperate, to crown the PC tonight and skip the Final, "so I don't have to hurt you first." Accepting triggers it. Foreshadowing: Ilsa's earlier warning, "Don't let him hand you anything tonight. He's not thinking, he's drowning." The Crown is incomplete without the Engine's cradle, which Voss has. The half-Tuning Fixes the PC in the Sanctum, and Voss's men collect the keystone from the Sanctum floor. Epitaph: "The Crown was whole for one second. Then it was Meridian's."

"Little Owl" (Ilsa's side quest culmination, :sq_little_owl). If the PC completed her quest steps, carrying her messages to her father through Acts III–V, Ilsa comes to the Sanctum after the audience.

"You chose a country over a daughter. Fine. Then save the country, Father. Don't make a child pay for it."

Valen: "...Little owl." It's the name from her childhood, which the player heard in the Mindscape in Ch13. She stays the night. If not completed, she watches the Sanctum door from the colonnade and doesn't go in.

Morwen's Echo (conditional exhibition, NS §3.6: Kestrel and Ysolde reconciled, and the K6 line was Resolve). After midnight, Ysolde lights the Hollow Cathedral's lantern in a Crownhaven chapel. Morwen's echo fights the PC once, gently, "to see what I bought."

INTERLUDE V — "The Last Night" (playable as Valen). 1. Short and quiet. The player controls Valen in the Sanctum before dawn. 2. He writes three letters: - To Ilsa: if reconciled, he tears it up, because he doesn't need it. - To Morwen's son: "I'm not asking you to forgive me." - To "whoever wears it next". 3. He walks to the window and talks to Zygarde (a single Cell glowing on the sill): "Order, not justice. I chose order. Was I wrong?" The Cell pulses once. The player decides what that means.

Day 6: Semifinal — Kestrel (K8)

Kestrel's state (KESTREL_FALLEN or not; NS rules): - Kestrel (Redeemed) (fallback). He fights with the Gauntlet at first. Mid-battle, at the first field-cycle, he looks at the Gauntlet's light and tears it off, then throws it across the arena floor:

"No. Not like this. If I beat you, I beat you."

He finishes with Morwen's Mismagius and his Aegislash, honest and ferocious. - Kestrel (Fallen). He keeps the Gauntlet on. His Pokémon glow with stolen Imprint, the field cycles under his command, and he fights like someone drowning. After the loss, the Gauntlet overloads and burns his hand, and Voss's men carry him off "for medical attention". He reappears in the hijack harnessed into the Engine (Ch21).

After the match, Morwen's letter. Ysolde walks onto the arena floor and gives Kestrel the sealed letter at last. He reads it there, in front of forty thousand people, and the arena screens tactfully cut away. The text (Key Item, readable later):

"Kestrel. If you're reading this, you're ready, or Ysolde's given up waiting. Both are fine. I didn't fail. I chose. There was a child in the light and I was nearest, and I'd have wanted someone to choose you. They'll call me a disgrace. Let them. Be a Vane anyway — be the kind that's nearest when it matters. I love you more than my seat, more than my name, more than my Plate. That's the whole letter. — Mum."

  • Redeemed: Kestrel sits down on the arena floor. If the PC still has Morwen's Ring, they can give it now. Then:

    "...Then you take him. Take him for both of us."

If both Ysolde's and Kestrel's Bonds are high (KESTREL_YSOLDE_RECONCILED), he lets her sit beside him. - Fallen: he doesn't read it. Ysolde keeps it. If the PC kept :ch12_letter, they can quote Morwen to him in the Engine instead.

Other SF (default; if Sigrun was the PC's QF, Valen's semifinal is a one-line result instead): Valen beats Sigrun. Sigrun entered to win the Crown herself and spare the PC, and Valen beat her to protect her. She leaves the arena without a word. The PC finds her on the Basilica roof: "He beat me with an arm made of glass. I've never been so angry in my life."

Day 7: The Final, the Coronation, the Hijack

See Chapter 21.

CHAPTER 21 — "Sovereign" (the Final, the Hijack, Arceus, the endings)

The Final: Valen Corrigan (CHAMPION_VALEN, cap 96)

  1. Dawn. The whole cast fills the arena.
  2. Valen, with no glove at all now: "Come on, then. Show me there's another door."
  3. The battle on :PRISM_ARENA, with fields cycling. His ace is Zygarde (Complete Forme on its last bar: "Order, not justice. I chose order.").
  4. Mid-battle, every time the field cycles, his crystal visibly spreads, from shoulder to throat.
  5. Draw variant, "Valen or his successor": if Valen has collapsed before the Final, the PC fights the Sovereign-designate instead. - This happens if VALEN_COLLAPSED is set (Ch20 audience). - Sigrun (Bond ≥ 5): "He beat me with an arm made of glass. Now he can't stand. So it's me." - Otherwise Brann, who fights in tears: "He asked me to. Last thing he asked." - Valen watches from a stretcher, which puts him in position for the Ring later.
  6. After the PC wins: Valen yields, smiling. "Velmora has a new champion."

The Coronation

  1. Valen lifts the Crown from his head and walks to the PC at the arena's heart, inside the Concordance Ring.
  2. The Choir sings, and the crowd is on its feet.
  3. CHOICE :ch21_crown: kneel to receive it / stand / step back. Each is flavor that the Narrative Systems endings may read.
  4. The moment the Crown is raised over the PC's head, the eighteen Plates in the Ring scream.

The Hijack (default hijacker: Voss)

  1. The Meridian Prism Arena wakes. Its rings (the Arbiter Engine, built into the venue) ignite in gold.
  2. The keystone cradle, installed beneath the arena floor, rises in a golden hand.
  3. The Concordance Ring's pylons sprout golden locks, the new Gilded Chain.
  4. The Crown is torn from Valen's hand into the cradle. The PC is pinned by their own shard's pull, half-kneeling at the cradle's palm.
  5. Voss descends from his box onto the arena floor in a Meridian Engine-harness. He's calm, almost apologetic, wearing his gold sunburst pin.

    "You carried my key through eighteen doors. Let me show you what it opens."

Hijacker variants (NS §3.2): - Dr. Quent (Voss discredited: MAGNUS_EXPOSED plus Rook's leak plus the Night of Masques). The board removed Voss in disgrace before Day 7. Quent giggles in the harness, with Voss's Silvally team. Voss watches from the gallery, appalled at his own machine in someone else's hands. - Ruin Route: Theodric, the Gilded Reflection (story/12_RUIN_ROUTE.md, R7).

His hand. Larch has warned the PC that morning: "Don't let him make you a part. He's very good at parts." Voss offers his hand: "Stand up. Stand with me. It doesn't have to hurt." - With VOSS_ACCEPTED (:ch21_hand): - Take it: Concord Dawn begins here, and Arceus is never fought. - Refuse: Voss, genuinely hurt: "You promised." - Without it, taking his hand is Game over GO_HIS_HAND "His Hand". Foreshadowing: Kestrel's K7 speech, and Celestine's "not chained, not bought". Epitaph: "It didn't hurt. That was the cruelest part."

The allies move. Each beat fires only if its condition holds. Show as a fast montage, one line each. These are Larch's eleven lamps (NS §6.2). - Rook (ROOK_CUTS_GRID, which needs :ch16_rook = hold) cuts Lumen's grid from the arena feed: "First time Lumen's been dark in ten years. Kinda pretty." The Engine flickers. - Nyx blacks out Meridian comms. With the bargain (NYX_PRECISE) it's surgical. Without it, it's bloody, and it doesn't count as an Engine break. - Magnus (MAGNUS_KEPT_FAITH) in the arena's machine-gallery disables the safety governor: "No machine I have built will kill a child. Governor's off. Go." - Marchetti (MARCHETTI_TURNED: Ch14 evidence, or saved at the Fault) orders the Directorate to stand down: "Not for this. Never for this." - Larch reads the Engine's live output off her board of eleven lamps. With TELEMETRY_SABOTAGED: "It's miscalibrated! It's reaching for a shard that isn't where it thinks!" - Calla's schematics (:ch20_calla) light one lamp, or two if she went back. If she went back, Larch touches the second lamp and says nothing. - Sigrun and her dragons hold the sky against Meridian's Gilded Hours airships, with Reshiram beside her. With DOMINAR_SWAYED, the Ashkaar legions fly with her. - Marisol's fishing fleet evacuates Lower Crownhaven across the caldera lake. Edda pilots the lead boat. - Tamsin's silk nets catch falling arena debris over the stands. - The Lantern Hollow (LANTERN_HOLDS), led by Rowan, Teo, or Thistle and Wick, guard the Ring's pylons against Directorate techs. - Kaito holds the arena gate.

Tag battle (story). It's a short, desperate double battle on the cracking arena floor that clears the way to the hijacker. - Partner: Kestrel if Redeemed, otherwise Juno. - Foes: two of Marchetti / Quent / Kroll, whichever aren't turned, dead or hijacking (NS: "Ch21 tag foes v21").

Voss (MERIDIAN_VOSS, cap 100) on :PRISM_ARENA

  • Boss battle (multi-bar for his ace): Silvally (Project NULL), whose type follows the active field as the Prism Arena cycles through the eighteen gym fields. The rest of his team is "things men made" (see 03_CAST).
  • Mid-battle lines:
  • "Your mother wilted for ten years because a god dropped its furniture on her."
  • "I'm not your enemy. I'm the only adult in this arena who'll say the true thing out loud: we don't need it."
  • Between bars 2 and 3, if KESTREL_FALLEN: Kestrel is revealed harnessed into the Engine as Voss's conduit, Gauntlet fused to the cradle and burning. CHOICE :ch21_reach_kestrel:
  • Reach (needs Morwen's Ring or Morwen's words from a kept letter): the PC says her line, "Be the kind that's nearest when it matters." He comes back. The Gauntlet goes dark, and Kestrel tears himself loose.
  • Burn: the PC's shard tears the Gauntlet off by force. He lives, scarred across the arm (Ruin −3).
  • Let go: he burns out as the Engine fires (KESTREL_LOST). This is the road to The Empty Lighthouse.
  • On defeat: the Engine doesn't stop. Voss drives his harness-spike into the cradle and becomes the conduit himself. "Then I'll hold it."

Arceus Descends

  1. The sky over Crownhaven opens, white.
  2. Arceus is dragged down through it into the Engine's golden chains, eighteen locks closing on eighteen empty places in its wheel. It is enormous, half-bound and in agony. The Prism Arena turns to :BLEED across the whole caldera.
  3. The hijacker, at the cradle, ecstatic and horrified: "It's — it's beautiful —"
  4. Arceus's Judgment strikes the harness. CHOICE :ch21_spare_voss: - Shield him with the shard (PC_SPARED_VOSS): he lives, crippled. - Let it judge: he becomes light at the arena's heart.
  5. The Bleed surge rolls out toward forty thousand people in the stands.

Valen holds the Ring. 1. Valen puts the cracked Crown back on, from the cradle's palm where the PC tore it loose, and walks into the Concordance Ring. 2. He grips two pylons and holds the surge. His crystal blazes white. 3. If ILSA_RECONCILED, Ilsa is at his back with her hands on his shoulders, pouring her mind into his: "I've got you. Little owl's got you."

"Go. Free it. I'll hold the door — I've had thirty years of practice."

(If Valen collapsed before the Final, he is carried to the Ring on his stretcher and stands up anyway.)

The True Finale: Arceus (cap 100)

Boss battle: Arceus, multi-bar, type- and field-shifting. Variant by state (NS §6.6): Bound (default), Miscalibrated (telemetry sabotaged), War (Resonance ≤ −6), Gilded (Engine calibrated: the Bad ending), Judgment (Ruin Route only). - Six bars, three locks each, in gym order. Bar 1 is Tamsin, Dorian and Marisol, and so on through bar 6: Nyx, Celestine and Sigrun. The Prism Arena shifts to the matching gym field for each lock. - Between bars, those three Wardens kneel at their pylons and release their Plate, letting go of the bond they've carried their whole life, or refuse. Every refusal makes the next lock forced: Arceus gains +1 to every stat stage for that bar. - All 36 lines (18 releases, 18 refusals, each refusal tied to the player's choice that caused it) are in story/11_FINALE_LINES.md. - Celestine sings the Hymn's last verse as her lock breaks. The Gloaming choir in the stands picks it up, then the Prism Choir, then the crowd. - Harmony vs Ruin. At high Harmony, the PC's Pokémon are steadied by the shard (systems: minor buffs). In the War variant, the shard eats Arceus's types instead of freeing them, and the battle is a war, not a liberation. - Gilded variant: the PC fights for Arceus against the chains and loses the struggle anyway. The last bar can't be broken. This is The Gilded Chain.

Valen's death (all main-route paths except Gilded). As the final lock breaks, Valen's crystal burns through, not to glass but to light. He says the PC's name. If reconciled, he says "Little owl." Then he's gone, and the Crown falls empty to the arena floor.

Arceus speaks

  1. Freed, Arceus stands over the arena, whole except for its heart.
  2. The PC's shard is pulled gently out of their chest, painless and luminous, and hangs between them and the god beside the Crown's shard.
  3. The Legend Plate is whole for the first time since AF 0.
  4. Ilsa (if reconciled) gives Arceus's voice to everyone in the arena:

    "Little light. You have carried me far. You may give my heart back, and the rest of me with it. Or you may keep what was borrowed, and wear it, and hold this land quiet in your two hands for a hundred years. I will not take what is not given. I learned that here."

(The last sentence is a Ruin Route hint: Arceus is talking about Judgment.)

The final decision. The PC speaks (authored), CHOICE :ch21_final. Availability per NS §6.1: - Return (always): "Go home. Take them with you." - Keep (always): "I'll keep them. I'll keep everyone safe." - Borrowed (Kestrel Redeemed and devoted, or Sigrun Bond ≥ 6): the PC hesitates, and Kestrel (or Sigrun) steps forward and takes the Crown: "Not you. Let me be nearest." - Concord (VOSS_ACCEPTED, or Magnus's governor never switched off): "Stay. Stay and power the world." - Unmake (Resonance ≤ −6, or Hollow Standing ≥ 4 with Rowan radicalized or Kroll never publicly exposed): "No. No more gods."

THE ENDINGS (scenes; the conditions matrix is NS §6.3)

GOOD: "Homecoming"

  1. Arceus takes the eighteen Plates and its heart, and ascends. Light rains on Crownhaven.
  2. The Bleed recedes across the realm in one long exhale.
  3. The Fixed who still dream wake: - In Verdance, the Bride Tree's blossom falls all at once, and Elin opens her eyes in Rowan's arms with Juno beside them, under the first falling leaves Verdance has ever seen. If Rowan drowned at the Fault, Juno holds her aunt alone and has to tell her. - In the Lowlight, Dex blinks, finishes his wave, and says, "—and that's why my sister says I talk too much." Rook, standing there in the dark city, laughs until he cries. - In Mirefen, Corvin's Quiet Ward sits up. - In Lower Crownhaven, the Glassfall Scar's forty breathe again.
  4. The Fixed who stopped dreaming (Marrowgate's oldest, the long-Fixed of the Quiet Towns) dissolve into soft light. Kaito is there to watch it, at Marrowgate's sealed gate, with his Lucario. He opens the gate.
  5. The PC, alone in the arena center, feels something they've never felt: the ground. The plain land, with no Imprint at all, imprints them. A Meridian scanner left on the floor reads a faint, ordinary color at last.
  6. Voss: if shielded, he's alive, crippled, and asks to see the sunrise. Otherwise he is light at the arena's center, a man who reached for a god.

Epilogue ("One Year Later": Fallmorn, AF 301, the first Festival of Seasons). Slides: - Juno in Verdance's first autumn, raking leaves with Elin, and with Rowan if he lived. - Kestrel, Ghost Warden of Gravenmoor (KESTREL_ACCEPTED_SEAT), or a Gale Post wanderer. - Teo on the Ebbmouth quay, or his photograph on the Guild wall. - Calla's name on the new Spire memorial, if she went back. - Rimeholt rebuilding on the southern shore, with Anja and Eirik and a thaw-fishery. - Cindervault's forges on coal again. Brann has forged Valen's memorial from the empty Crown's setting. - Lumen with fewer lights and more stars, and the Zenith Council reformed with Nadir's first elected seat. Nyx is in it and hates it. - The quarantine: it lifts now (ENVOY_PERSUADED) or a year late, grudgingly. Edda's ferry runs again. - Bastian dies peacefully at home: "the first Warden in three hundred years with nothing in his keeping". - Celestine sees her first full night sky in sixty years and starts to age, gladly. - The PC stands at the Ferrow's Landing rail where they arrived, as the first ferry comes in. Edda: "Don't lean on my rail like that, sprog."

BAD: "The Gilded Chain"

  1. The Engine holds. Arceus is bound in gold above Crownhaven, a second sun.
  2. The PC, the keystone, sleeps in the cradle, adored as "the Sovereign Who Sleeps", visited by schoolchildren.
  3. Zenith blazes. Nadir is cleared. The Bleed stops.
  4. The kingdoms kneel, because Meridian owns the light.
  5. The final image is the chipped linesman's mug on a desk above the world, steaming. Quent variant: the mug is Quent's novelty mug, and Voss stands among the visiting schoolchildren's parents, looking up at the PC in the cradle.
  6. Credits, then the title screen. There is no post-game.

GREY: "The Long Vigil" (Keep)

The PC wears the Crown. The Plates sleep quiet. Arceus departs incomplete, sorrowing. The Bleed halts, the Fixed stay Fixed, and the miracles remain. The epilogue is the PC at the next Tuning, ten years later, with the first crystal on their hand. Juno and Kestrel are beside them, swearing to find another door. Post-game: the Warden's Gauntlet is framed as Sovereign's audiences.

GREY: "Homecoming, Unfinished" (Return, Consent < 12 or Resonance < 2)

The Return happens without enough consent. The unreleased Plates are torn away, and those cities suffer a Last Shatter. The rest is Homecoming, but with funerals. The slides show one image per refusing Warden's city, in gym order, using the lines in 11_FINALE_LINES.md §3. If Resonance was below Harmony, the PC's scar never takes the land's color. It's hard, it's free, and it's ash-grey and hopeful.

GREY: "The Empty Lighthouse" (Return with Consent and Harmony, but KESTREL_LOST)

Everything in Homecoming happens, but Kestrel burned out as the Engine's conduit. - Hesper climbs the lighthouse every night and lights a lamp nobody needs. - Ysolde keeps the Ghost seat. - The PC reads Morwen's letter aloud at Kestrel's grave in Gravenmoor, beside his mother's, for the boy who never opened it. - Last slide: Kestrel's Aegislash, sheathed, on the PC's belt.

GREY: "The Hollow Spring" (Unmake, main route)

The PC uses the shard to break the Plates. The Imprints die in one night of fire and glass. Arceus, with no Plates to receive, departs wounded forever. Velmora is ruined, and free: nobody will ever rule through a Plate again. Rowan plants the first seed in plain soil, or Thistle does if Rowan drowned. Juno doesn't speak to him, or to her. If Teo lived, he's the one who won't. Post-game: partial (Spire and Gauntlet only).

GREY: "Concord Dawn"

  • Arceus is "humanely" half-bound by Magnus's governor and powers the realm.
  • The Bleed ends and prosperity booms. The PC sits on the Zenith Council.
  • Over the last slide, Celestine speaks in fairy-tale cadence: "Once upon a time a god was kept in a box. It was quiet. For a while."
  • If reached through :ch21_hand, the PC never fought Arceus. The last slide is the PC reading the arena's power output on a Council screen.
  • Post-game: partial (Spire only; the Wardens are pensioned off; Quent's Concord Zero arc starts).

GREY: "The Borrowed Crown"

Kestrel (KESTREL_BEARS) or Sigrun takes the Crown in the PC's place. The Plates sleep, and the bearer starts to crystallize. The PC sails home on the first ferry after the quarantine lifts, then comes back every Festival. - With Kestrel, the last slide is his hand on the rail, beginning to turn to glass, and he's smiling. - With Sigrun: "Ten years. Maybe twelve. I was relieved once. Never again."

RUIN: "The Unmaking" / "The One Who Came Back"

Ruin Route only. See story/12_RUIN_ROUTE.md (R7) and NS §5.4.

POST-GAME HOOKS

Scope by ending (NS §6.5): full after Homecoming, Unfinished (refusers are absent from the Gauntlet), Empty Lighthouse, Long Vigil, Borrowed Crown and both Ruin endings. Partial after the Hollow Spring and Concord Dawn. None after the Gilded Chain. - The Concordance Spire (Battle Tower) in Crownhaven, and the Warden's Gauntlet: all eighteen Wardens back-to-back at level 100. In Homecoming their teams no longer use fields, because the Imprints are fading, so it's a "pure" gauntlet. - Zygarde chooses the PC at Valen's memorial (ZYGARDE_CHOOSES: Valen Bond ≥ 3 or Ilsa reconciled), completing the Zygarde Cell quest. - The Distortion Core (Antumbra, 910): Giratina's rematch and capture, and the leak in the Glass. - Legendary rematches and captures: Kyogre (Lake Rime), Groudon (Undersands), Zekrom (Stormfront), Reshiram (Wyrmscar, if not with Sigrun), Pecharunt (Lightless Deep), Ogerpon and the Loyal Three (Bride Grove). - Shardfall Isles (905) and the Hallow Strait (900/901): the outer islands, where the quarantine began. Bleed remnants are fading. - Concord Zero: Dr. Quent escapes with an Engine fragment, the seed of a post-game villain arc. - Cameo superbosses (design/CAMEOS.md), all optional: Lin (with her lines changed by ending), Garret's Eden rumor near Marrowgate (a deliberate dead end, and a kindness), and level-100 rematches with the earlier cameos. - The Ferrow's Landing lighthouse: Hesper Vane, finally, asks the PC to tell her about her daughter's last night.

25 Giratina guards the seam; the Glass touches other worlds Ch12 cliffhanger: the reflection moves late Ch13: six-winged shadows in reflections. 01 lore: AF 140 Glass Opening, AF 230 sealing. Cameo sidequests (travelers slipping through) Ch15 Reverse Depths. Ruin Route R1

30 Shiv dreamed the PC (cameo) Ch18 sidequest "Dreamwalker" — Ruin Route hooks

Act I → Act V seed checklist (★ items)

Each one must exist in the Act I build: - The NULL scan. - Voss's badge case gift, and its hum. - Edda's rail line. - The Glassed Orchard Veil. - Rowan's Phantump and lines. - The Accord plaque. - The counting rhyme. - The chipped-Crown poster vs the chapel mural. - The Loyal Three shrine and Celestine's rhyme. - Dorian's prologue page. - The Mourner's Saltmere speech. - Kestrel's "kid made of light" (Interlude I). - Valen's glove at the Lowlight and "endures". - The Spire's black core. - Dex. - Larch's AF 290 journal line. - Hesper's dropped rag. - "Meridian Prism Arena" on posters.

Red herrings (keep them fair)

  • "The Mourner is Morwen." Seeded by Kestrel. Disproved at Ch9. Morwen's real fate is in Ch15.
  • "Dorian is the Mourner." A theatrical voice, and he's oddly calm at the hijacked pageant. Disproved when he's on stage beside the Mourner.
  • "Larch is working for Meridian." Her past, her Lattice. She's guilty but loyal. It resolves at Ch12, when she burns her career on air.
  • "Valen is the villain." He's the most suspicious figure in Acts III–IV. He's the liar, not the villain. Ch20 recontextualizes.

Story Bible 14: The Ashkaar–Isenmar War and Arceus's Blessing (SPINE Revision 6)

Authoritative for the war arc, the Blessing, and the kingdom delegations. Where 01, 02, 05–08 disagree with this file, this file wins (the act files carry short pointers). Storyboards are in 13_CUTSCENES.md. Keys marked (proposed) are for the Narrative Systems agent to adopt in NARRATIVE_KEYS.

PART A — ARCEUS'S BLESSING (the Festival reframed)

What everyone believes

The Festival of Champions is the one sacred ceremony all seven kingdoms share. Every ten years each kingdom carries its Plates to Crownhaven and sends two delegates to fight before the Plates. The last one standing receives Arceus's Blessing: 1. The Light. The Crown is set on the victor inside the Concordance Ring, and the light of all eighteen Plates passes through them. The Choir teaches that this is Arceus touching one mortal. 2. The Seat. The Blessed holds the Sovereign Seat for the decade. 3. The Boon. The Blessed may speak one decree that every crown must honor until the next Festival: a border, a peace, a tax, a pardon. This is why kings care, and why they send their best. Maren's first Boon founded the Wardguard. Valen's three Boons: the Plate-tax ban (AF 270), the Marrowgate silence (AF 280, sealed), the Morwen verdict (AF 290, sealed).

What is true

  • The Light is the Tuning. "The Plates' light passing through the Blessed" is the bearer's body hushing the Plates. The Blessing shortens every life it touches. Nothing in 01 §5 changes except the name the public uses.
  • The Boon is real law and was Maren's own invention: a reward large enough that the strongest person in the realm would always volunteer for a thing that kills them.
  • Valen has been Blessed three times. He intends the PC to be the fourth name on the list and the last.
  • Voss needs the Blessing moment itself: eighteen Plates converged, the Crown raised, a bearer in the Ring. The Arbiter Engine fires during the Light. He also wants Zenith's delegates to win the Boon as a fallback ("Mandate in perpetuity").
  • The Hymn of Return read correctly: the last Blessed gives the Light back.

Retcons (small)

  • "Sovereign" = "the Blessed". The Coronation in Ch21 is "the Blessing". Arceus's speech gains one line: "They call this my blessing. I have never once been asked."
  • The Good ending adds a Boon beat: with the Plates gone, the PC still gets one decree. CHOICE :ch21_boon (proposed): end the war's reparations / seat Nadir on every council / dissolve the Seat itself.
  • Entrants are delegations, not a free draw (below). The bowl still decides pairings.

Delegations (fifteen delegates plus the PC)

The seat rules are owned by Narrative Systems: 10_CHOICES_ENDINGS.md §3.1 and NARRATIVE_KEYS §6.1 are authoritative. In short: - Two seats per kingdom, plus Valen for the Accord. Every delegate has been met at least twice by Act IV. - Elowen: Juno and Celestine (Juno may give her seat to Rowan, "the Gardener"). - Antumbra: Ysolde, and Kestrel if Gravenmoor restores the name Vane, else Ilsa. - Zenith: Rook or Odessa, and Kestrel under Meridian's sunburst, else Magnus. - Nadir: Kaito, and Nyx or Corvin. - Ossaria: Sahar and Bastian. - Isenmar: Marisol and Anja. Ashkaar: Sigrun and Brann. - The war decides seats. A kingdom that lost the war, or both if the clash broke both armies, forfeits its second seat, and the Accord lends it to a seatless Warden. - Story staging: the delegations camp in the Court of Eight Pavilions. Each ruler presents their delegates to the Hierophant on Day 0, and a forfeited seat is shown as an empty pavilion pole with the banner cut down.

The PC's colors — CHOICE :ch20_colors (proposed to NS; flavor and one Standing only). The PC enters as the Unimprinted challenger, but may wear a kingdom's colors. Three crowns offer the PC theirs on Day 0: Queen Maelis (the PC landed on her soil), the Zenith Council (Voss's Fellowship), and Nyx ("Nadir could use a null"). Larch offers a fourth: no colors at all, entered under the old Accord clause for "the Unsworn". Each choice changes crowd reactions, one R16 line and one Standing; Unsworn is the Harmony option and Celestine's Hymn names it ("one of no color").

Pairings. The bowl avoids same-kingdom pairs in R16. Stage pools from 10_CHOICES_ENDINGS §3 stand, re-read as delegations. War pairings (Ashkaar vs Isenmar) are staged as grudge matches under truce.

PART B — THE ASHKAAR–ISENMAR WAR

Why they fight (and why it isn't a detour)

  • Isenmar's heat is Ashkaar's fire. Meridian's heat-towers in Rimeholt are fed by Prism Cells charged at the Cindervault geothermal tap. Ashkaar's Flame Imprint is being drained to keep Isenmar's children warm. Meridian sold it to both crowns as two unrelated contracts.
  • Old grievance: the River Ebb rises in Ashkaar's highlands as meltwater. Each kingdom claims the headwaters. Isenmar's ice-fleet and Ashkaar's riders have skirmished there for a century.
  • Voss wants the war. Two opposed Imprints colliding at scale is the best calibration data the Engine can get short of the Festival. Meridian arms both sides with Prism weapons and logs every clash. The war also keeps two crowns too busy to vote against the Mandate.
  • The Bleed: every battlefield where Fire and Ice Imprints meet becomes a Bleed zone of steam-glass. The front line is a Bleed line, and only the PC can cross it.
  • The legendaries: Kyogre (woken by the Thaw) and Reshiram (the Light Stone) become the kingdoms' banners. The climax is their clash.

Timeline and inserts per chapter

When Beat (insert into the named chapter)
Ch4 Ebbmouth (seed) Dock gossip: an Ashkaar rider burned an Isenmar ice-barge at the headwaters. The Ebb Guild's strike is partly about war-levy taxes the Thane is raising "just in case". A Meridian recruiter sells heat contracts under a poster of Cindervault's forges.
Ch8 Cindervault (seed) The Dominar's envoy says it aloud: "Our fire warms our enemies' beds." Brann shows the PC the tap's manifest: destination Rimeholt. The closing of the eastern passes is aimed at Isenmar's heat, with Zenith second. (Retcon: the Dominar's threat is to Isenmar first.)
Ch9 Conclave Anja and the Dominar's envoy nearly come to blows in the observers' gallery. Valen separates them.
Ch11 Rimeholt: war breaks out With the passes closed, Rimeholt's towers overdraw the local Lattice to compensate, which causes the Thaw. Kyogre's rain rolls east and floods Ashkaar's lowland forts. The Thane blames the embargo for his drowned town; the Dominar blames "Isenmar's sea-beast". War is declared at the chapter's end, on every screen, with Voss offering Meridian's "neutral logistics" to both.
Ch12 Skyreach Gale Post flies war mail for both sides. Odessa refuses Directorate orders to carry Prism munitions. The Larch Papers include the two heat contracts side by side. Nobody reads that page.
Ch13 Lucent Rowan's tribunal is interrupted twice by couriers from the front. The kings' gallery has two empty chairs.
Ch14 Cragholm / Undersands The Steamline is on every screen: steam-glass along the north-east coast. Refugees from both kingdoms share Bastian's seams and fight over bread. A Meridian convoy passes through carrying identical Prism lances for both armies (the PC can see the manifests; this is evidence for Okoro, not a choice). War 2, :war_convoy is offered here by Gale Post from Marisol.
Ch15 interlude beat News: the Siege of Skaldfast. Ashkaar's dragons ring the fortress-port. Marisol's Ebb Guild runs the blockade through the Rime Narrows by night. If Teo lived, he's organizing boats.
Ch16–17 The Purge happens while both crowns are looking at each other. That is why nobody stops it. Nyx says so.
Ch18 Gloaming shelters deserters from both armies. They can't lie there, so they tell the truth about why they ran.
Ch19 Wyrmrest: the climax See below. This replaces "the Dominar marches on Zenith": his legions are massed against Isenmar's host, which has come up the frozen Ebb with Kyogre before it.
Ch20 The Peace of Plates: a truce for the Festival. Both kingdoms send delegates to win the Boon and end the war on their terms.

The Battle of the Shattered Ice (Ch19)

Setup. The front is on the north-east coast (WORLD.md §8–9): Isenmar's fortress-port Skaldfast is besieged from the Ashkaar Siege Camp, the Cinder Cape Warfort guards Ashkaar's shore, the Rime Narrows between them are choked with the ice-fleet, and the Bleed line of steam-glass where the Imprints meet is the Steamline. Sigrun's trial and oath (08, Ch19) happen first, at Wyrmrest. Then word comes that Kyogre has risen from the Ice Vault and is walking the Narrows like a tide, and that her father has carried the Light Stone to the front. Sigrun flies the PC there overnight. The climax is fought on the Shattered Ice, the broken sea between the two hosts.

The six war choices (keys and effects: NARRATIVE_KEYS §4.3; this is their staging): 1. :war_warning (Ch11, before the Thaw). Climbing Frostfang Pass, the PC sees the Dominar's legions massing before anyone else knows. Warn Isenmar, warn Ashkaar that they've been seen, warn both through Valen, or stay silent. 2. :war_convoy (Ch14). Marisol's blockade-runners ask the Unimprinted to walk a convoy through the Steamline into besieged Skaldfast: relief (food), arms (harpoon-guns), or refuse. 3. :war_prisoner (Ch15–16, by Gale Post and a short trip to the Siege Camp). Ashkaar holds Anja; the Thane holds the Dominar's nephew. Free her by walking her out through the Bleed line, broker an exchange, or leave it. This is Anja's extra pre-Festival scene: in the cage she says, "Tell my brother I'd have signed for the heaters again." 4. :war_legend (Ch17–18). Each side begs the PC to bring its legend to the front: calm Kyogre in the Ice Vault, wake Reshiram early with the oath, or neither. 5. :war_climax (Ch19, MAJOR). On the Shattered Ice: stand between, back Ashkaar or back Isenmar. 6. :war_terms (Ch19 aftermath, only if one side won). At the armistice table in Skaldfast's great hall the PC argues for mercy, reparations or occupation.

The four outcomes: - WAR_STOPPED (stood between, and both armies have seen enough of the PC to trust them: War Trust ≥ 4). Both lines hold fire. The beams meet over the PC and part. - WAR_BOTH_BROKEN (stood between without that trust). Nobody listens. The clash detonates and breaks both armies. Both kingdoms forfeit a Festival seat. - WAR_ASHKAAR_WON / WAR_ISENMAR_WON. The loser forfeits a seat. Occupation terms poison the Good ending: Arceus won't bless a land with a boot on its neck.

The battle. Playable set pieces around the cutscene: a gauntlet through the camp of the side you didn't choose (or both, if you stand between), a partner battle with Sigrun or Marisol, then the boss fight as written for Reshiram in 08, reframed: the PC fights whichever legendary is about to fire on the other army (Reshiram by default; Kyogre if backing Ashkaar). If :war_legend kept both legends away, the clash is between the Wardens' aces instead: Sigrun's Salamence against Anja's Mamoswine or Marisol's Gyarados.

The climax is the pixel-animated cutscene WAR-1 "The Beam Clash on the Shattered Ice" (13_CUTSCENES.md): Reshiram's Blue Flare meets Kyogre's Origin Pulse over the Shattered Ice. Where they meet, the air turns to steam-glass and a Bleed dome swells over both armies. The PC's shard (and the two Wardens who love each side) ends it.

Aftermath. - Meridian observers on the ridge pack up their instruments before the steam clears. Larch, by radio: "That wasn't a battle to them. That was a reading." Field Notes logs the Engine's calibration jumping. - Sigrun and Anja (or Marisol) stand on the glass between the armies and call the truce: the war will be settled by the Boon. The rulers agree because their legendaries have both left: Reshiram to Sigrun, Kyogre back to the sea.

What the war changes later

  • Consent: side-taking adds refusals (Brann or Anja/Marisol). Standing between adds none.
  • Hijack montage: if the truce held without a victor, Ashkaar's riders and Isenmar's ice-fleet hold the sky and the lake together.
  • Endings: Homecoming's Boon can end reparations. The Gilded Chain: Meridian annexes both exhausted kingdoms. Hollow Spring: the war restarts with no Plates and no fire.

Story Bible 10: Choices, the Festival Draw, Game Overs, the Ruin Route and the Endings

Owner: Narrative Systems. Binding: design/SPINE.md Revision 3. Implementation contract (every key, value, condition and ID): design/NARRATIVE_KEYS.md — if this file and that one disagree, NARRATIVE_KEYS wins and this file is the bug. Built on the story lead's act files 04–08 and the FLAGS.md crosswalk; their key names are kept (lowercased). Kingdom names are Revision 3: Elowen, Antumbra, Isenmar, Ashkaar, Ossaria, Zenith, Nadir, and Crownhaven.

0. Design pillars

  1. No good/evil buttons. Every prompt trades something real: a life now against proof later (Teo or Sable's comm-device), the truth against the person who'd tell it (expose Magnus, or trust him), honesty against a friend's dignity (expose Anja, or let her confess).
  2. Delayed and visible. Most choices pay off two or three chapters later, and the game shows the debt coming due (§1.4), never the numbers.
  3. The world keeps score, not a meter. The big outcomes are counts of people: how many Wardens let go of their Plate (Consent), how many cracks the PC and their friends put in Voss's machine (Engine breaks). Both are shown on screen before the finale.
  4. Easy in, dark later. Act I's choices are small and kind-hearted (who to trust, what to say). Act II asks who to save. Acts III–V ask who you'll let die, and who you'll become.

1. Choice architecture

1.1 Bonds (10)

Range −10..+10. Tiers: hostile ≤ −5 · wary −4..−2 · neutral −1..1 · friendly 2..4 · close 5..7 · devoted 8+.

Bond Start Why it's tracked What it changes
Juno +2 the friend you start with Consent (≥ 0); estranged Juno (≤ −5) travels apart in Ch11; Garden Match exhibition; Homecoming slide with the PC
Kestrel −1 the rival who can fall Fallen at ≤ −4 (or ≤ 1 with the pity line, or ≤ 1 while you're Meridian's ally), unless you gave him the ring or answered K6 bluntly; devoted (8+) lets him take the Borrowed Crown; Ghost seat with Ysolde
Rowan 0 the Mourner radicalized (≤ −2 after being condemned) opens the Hollow Spring; his R16 appearance needs mercy, not affection
Voss +1 the first person who was kind to you how warmly he offers, how bitterly he takes refusal; Ruin R4
Valen 0 the father-figure who planned your life his collapse (≤ −2 with a refusal at the audience); his last words; Zygarde chooses the PC post-game at ≥ 3
Ilsa 0 the daughter who reads minds Little Owl (≥ 3 to start, ≥ 4 to reconcile); Consent (≥ 5 or reconciled)
Sigrun 0 the heir who was relieved war trust (≥ 4); she draws into your QF if you've gone Ruin-leaning or broke Ashkaar; Borrowed Crown bearer (≥ 6)
Nyx 0 the broker Consent (≥ 4 or the bargain)
Marisol 0 the sister of the boy you may not save if Teo died: Consent at ≥ 3, otherwise she claims her Right of Grievance in R16
Ysolde 0 Morwen's partner Kestrel–Ysolde reconciliation (≥ 4), Kestrel's Ghost seat, Morwen's Echo exhibition

Other Wardens are not Bond tracks. When an act file says "Tamsin's Bond goes up", the choice key itself carries it, and it surfaces in that Warden's Consent condition (§6.2). That keeps ten relationships deep instead of thirty shallow.

1.2 Standing (Hollow / Meridian / Accord)

Range −10..+10 (Meridian and Accord start at +1, the Hollow at 0). "High" = ≥ 4 (the Ch10 Sanctuary / Black Site branches, the Ch17 Archive branch); "Low" = ≤ −2; "broken" Hollow = below 0. Standing gates content (branch maps, who ambushes you, Voss's warmth, Lantern Hollow at the hijack) far more than endings; the endings read Standing only through the Hollow Spring and Concord options.

1.3 Resonance (Harmony ↔ Ruin)

One integer from −10 (Ruin, none) to +10 (Harmony, every). It moves only when the PC uses the shard or chooses how to carry grief: soothing or silencing the Glass Matriarch (Ch2), venting or forcing the caldera (Ch8), the Ogre (Ch10), "nobody" (Ch10), burning Morwen's letter (Ch12), the Mindscape line (Ch13), the K6 line (Ch15), the oath (Ch19), Calla (Ch20), burning Kestrel's gauntlet off (Ch21). Bands: ruin ≤ −6 · ruin-leaning −5..−2 · balanced · harmony-leaning 2..5 · harmony ≥ 6.

1.4 How the player sees consequences (no numbers)

  • "X will remember that." Only when a Bond changes tier, or on a MAJOR choice. Rare on purpose, so it lands.
  • The Ties page (Trainer Card, page 3): ten portraits with a lantern that is cracked / guttering / lit / bright / blazing / starred, plus three faction sigils with one word each (Enemy … Ally). The openbook password adds numbers.
  • The scar. The PC's star-shaped scar in the Bag portrait shifts from pearl-white through colorless to a black-violet that eats the edges of the icon. Bleed motes drift toward the PC at Harmony and away at Ruin. Wardens comment ("You've gone cold, love.").
  • The Velmora Gazette in every Pokémon Center: two headlines per chapter picked by rule (e.g. "IRONREACH BOMBER STILL AT LARGE" vs "MERIDIAN CONTRACTOR LINKED TO IRONREACH" after Ch13).
  • Tamsin's eighteen banners (Ch20, the opening ceremony): she weaves one per Warden, in white thread if that Warden will let go and grey if not. "Twelve white. I don't know what it means, but it feels like it means something." That is the Consent count, shown.
  • Larch's Engine board (Ch20, Day 5): a hand-built panel of eleven lamps wired to her readings of the arena. Each lit lamp is a crack in the Engine (§6.2). "If that case is still singing to him and Magnus never flinched, three lamps won't be enough. I can't make that sound kinder."
  • The Journal records every named decision in one line of prose ("You told the Glass Court what you saw in the Lowlight.").

2. Choice catalog (48 keys)

Format: key — situation. Options → immediate effect. Long-term. Exact deltas: NARRATIVE_KEYS §4.2. ★ = MAJOR (10).

Prologue and Act I — "The Last Ferry" (warm; nothing here can ruin you, but everything is remembered)

  • :pro_fellowship — Voss offers a Fellowship card on the Dovetail. accept (money, Meridian trust) / decline (Voss likes you more: "Independent. Good.") / silent (he leaves it on the rail). Sets how warmly Meridian treats you in Acts I–II.
  • :ch2_matriarch (new) — The beaten Glass Matriarch thrashes in the silk-moth grove. soothe (stay with it all night; half the grove crystallizes; Harmony) / silence (press your hand to it and the Imprint leaves it like water down a drain; the grove is saved, the Matriarch is left hollow; Ruin −2). The player's first lesson: the efficient choice is the Ruin one.
  • :ch2_tamsin — Tamsin's secret Lattice contract. expose / help_confess / keep_secret. Keeping it means the pylon keeps draining Briarwick, and in Act V she can't afford to let go of her Plate (Consent).
  • :ch3_juno_talk — tell Juno about the NULL scan? told (she keeps it) / deflect / silent.
  • :ch4_kestrel_mourner — is the Mourner his mother? kind / blunt (−2) / silent.
  • :ch4_teo — Marisol is tipping off her brother's cell. report (Meridian trust; Marisol −4; Teo is hunted) / keep_secret / confront (she breaks down; the strongest Marisol bond). Seeds who Marisol is if Teo dies.
  • :ch5_lowlight_testimony — a Directorate officer asks what you saw. "The Mourner broke the Plate" (it's what it looked like) / "Someone else was there" / "I don't know." Telling the official story on record costs Dorian's Consent: "I keep the lie I know until someone shows me a better one."

Act II — "Imprints" (who do you save?)

  • :ch6_drillsite — the freed Pokémon and the drill-site children. hollow / directorate (a Mandate Pass that opens checkpoints later) / sanctuary (Sahar invokes Warden's right). Directorate costs Sahar's Consent.
  • :ch6_kestrel_rumor — K3. encourage (+2 now, −3 at Ironreach when it isn't her) / warn (−1 now, +2 at Ironreach: "I hate that you were right") / silent. The kind answer now is the cruel one later.
  • :ch7_ledger — J2. name_him (Juno −3, then +2 at Ironreach: she already knew) / reassure (+2, then −2) / ask_corvin.
  • :ch8_caldera — the Caldera Crisis. vent with Wick (forges cold; Harmony) / dampers with Quent (the tap keeps running; Ruin −1) / shutdown by force (a harder fight; Accord trust; the Dominar's respect).
  • :ch8_lightstone — hearthguild / wyrmrest (Sigrun +2; the only route to swaying the Dominar) / dominar (Brann won't let go of his Plate for a man who'd give away his guild's relic).
  • ★ :ch9_teo_sable — the Conclave Shatter. Teo is Fixing under the scaffolding; Sable is running for the service lift with the comm-device. Save Teo (he lives with glassed fingers; Marisol +4; Sable escapes clean) / chase Sable (you tear the device from her belt; Teo dies reaching for his sister). Long-term: Teo leads the Lantern Hollow strikers, or his photo is nailed to the strike banner; only the device exposes Kroll publicly at the Ch13 tribunal; Marisol's Consent or her Right of Grievance in R16.
  • :ch9_rowan_words — Rowan, led away. why / look_after (+2) / silent (+1).

Act III — "Fault Lines" (who do you let fall?)

  • :ch10_null — Black Site branch only: take the Type: Null. Voss +2. An unsettling gift: it reacts to you like the badge case.
  • :ch10_ogre — the Loyal Three or the Ogre (three is GO_LOYAL). ogre (Ogerpon fights with you, later joins Juno) / hesitate.
  • :ch10_succession_stance — "Who should own the Plates?" accord (Valen +2) / kingdoms / nobody (the first "nobody"; Harmony).
  • :ch11_anja — her suppressed readings. expose (ANJA_EXPOSED; Meridian blamed loudly) / let_confess (she becomes the Act V swing vote). Exposing her costs her Consent and makes her claim R16: "You threw me to the court. Fine. Ice remembers."
  • :ch11_strike (new) — the Ebbmouth Strike picket when the strikebreakers arrive. stand (the optional tag battle with Marisol; +2, +3 if Teo died) / mediate. The only Act III way back to Marisol after Teo's death.
  • :ch12_letter — Morwen's dead letter to "I.". keep (you can quote her in the hijack) / send (Kestrel +3: "You sent me a letter. Hers.") / burn (Ruin −2; Odessa finds out: "Dead letters are the last thing some people ever said." She won't let go of her Plate.)
  • :ch13_remember — the five-year-old in the Mindscape. "Show me." / "I'm scared too." (Ilsa +2, Harmony +2) / "...Not yet."
  • ★ :ch13_kaito — Kaito asks whether to speak about Marrowgate. Encourage (the Accord's moral authority cracks; Valen −2; Ilsa +2; Kaito's Consent) / spare him the burden (Accord trust; Kaito never lets go). A man who never confessed can't release anything.
  • ★ :ch13_rowan_testimony — your testimony at the Glass Court. Condemn (Pale Gaol; Juno −3; Rowan −4: if he survives, he radicalizes, which opens the Hollow Spring) / Mercy (Rowan +3, Juno +3, Hollow +2; with Hollow trust ≥ 4 he comes to the Festival as "the Gardener" with Juno's blessing) / Refuse (Ilsa, privately: "Coward. Kind of a coward. I understand.").
  • ★ :ch13_fault (new, who lives) — the Fault. The Glass Court's undercroft floods with Rowan chained in the dock, and the orrery's brass ring comes down on Director Marchetti. Wick's people are on the far side of the water. You can reach one. Rowan (he escapes with Wick, as written; Marchetti is dragged out by her troops, maimed and unturned) / Marchetti (Rowan drowns in the dark under the court, still chained; Marchetti owes you her life and stands the Directorate down at the hijack). Long-term: who leads the Lantern Hollow (Rowan, else Teo, else Thistle and Wick); whether Elin wakes into her husband's arms; Juno −4.
  • :sq_doctors_cell — "The Doctor's Cell" (Ch11–13): free Corvin from the holding barge, or fail. Corvin's Consent; the Cragholm hospital.

The Ashkaar–Isenmar War (Revision 6; chapters placed by the story lead, Act III–IV) — six proposed choices

  • :war_warning — you see the Dominar's legions at Frostfang Pass before anyone. Warn Isenmar / warn Ashkaar they've been seen / warn both (the Accord's way; both sides note it) / say nothing.
  • :war_convoy — Marisol asks the one person who can walk the Bleed-front to take a convoy through the Steamline into besieged Skaldfast: relief (food) / arms (harpoon-guns; Marisol +3, Sigrun −2) / refuse.
  • :war_prisoner — Ashkaar holds Anja; the Thane holds the Dominar's nephew. free one in secret / broker an exchange / leave them.
  • :war_legend — both sides beg you to bring their legend: calm Kyogre / wake Reshiram / neither. It decides whose beams meet in the climax.
  • ★ :war_climax — the Shattered Ice, both lines charging. Stand between them / back Ashkaar / back Isenmar. Standing between only stops the war if both armies have seen you act in good faith four times (war trust: warned both, relief, a prisoner helped, no legend used as a weapon, Sigrun ≥ 4, Marisol ≥ 3). Otherwise the beam clash goes off over your head and breaks both armies.
  • :war_terms — if one side won: argue for mercy, reparations or occupation at the armistice table in Skaldfast.
  • :ch20_colors (the story lead's key) — whose colors the challenger wears on Day 0: Elowen's, Zenith's, Nadir's, or none ("the Unsworn", a whisper of Harmony). Crowd reactions, one R16 line, one small delta.
  • :ch21_boon (the story lead's key) — the Blessed's one binding decree: end the war's reparations / seat Nadir on every council / dissolve the Seat. It is asked before the ending is fixed, so ending reparations can undo an occupation you argued for and reopen Homecoming.

Long-term: a beaten kingdom forfeits a Festival seat (§3.1); beaten and unforgiven, its Wardens won't release their Plates (Marisol and Anja, or Brann and Sigrun; both pairs if the clash broke both armies), which can sink Consent below 12 on its own; occupation makes Homecoming impossible (Unfinished at best); the winner's fleets or legions (both, if you stopped it) defend Crownhaven in the hijack; Anja vs Brann in the bracket is decided by it.

Act IV — "The Drowned Crown" (who are you willing to become?)

  • ★ :ch14_telemetry — Larch opens the badge case. Destroy (Voss knows at once; Directorate pressure in Ch16–17; an Engine crack) / Sabotage (keep it and feed it lies: two cracks, a tense "did he notice?" beat at every later badge) / Leave it (the road to the Bad ending, and Bastian's Consent is gone: "You walked a Meridian beacon into my seams.").
  • :ch14_marchetti — after her defeat, show her the Splitter Casing or the comm-device? show_evidence (she goes still; turns at the hijack) / none.
  • :ch15_kestrel — the PC's authored line after K6. sorry ("I'd give it back if I could": +1 now, +3 at the SF if he's Redeemed, but pity is the one thing a weak bond can't carry) / she_chose ("Don't you dare make her choice my crime": −2, and he can never fall) / resolve ("I'm going to make it worth it": +2, Harmony +2).
  • :ch15_look_back — only with a complete Ledger: the Ruin Route door (§5).
  • :ch16_zekrom — outcome of freeing Zekrom (always, if you win). One Engine crack.
  • :ch16_rook — leak_now (Kroll is confirmed realm-wide today; Rook is fired and blacklisted from the Spire) / hold (he leaks on Day 1 of the Festival, and he's still inside on Day 7 to cut Lumen's grid). Truth now, or a weapon later. Rook can only release a Plate he can reach.
  • ★ :ch16_magnus — the Arbiter blueprints. Expose (Magnus arrested; with Rook's leak and the Night of Masques, Meridian's board throws Voss out before Day 7 and Quent hijacks the Engine instead) / Conscience ("Will you let it take a child?": the governor goes off in the finale, an Engine crack, Magnus's Consent). The honest public act removes the man who could have saved you from inside.
  • :ch17_nyx — trade your secret for the arena schematics (her blackout is precise, an Engine crack, her Consent) / refuse (she helps anyway, sloppily, and gets hurt).
  • ★ :ch17_voss_offer — the Sun Court. Refuse / Feign (the hijack opens with Voss's Silvally weakened: "You let me get close.") / Accept (VOSS_ACCEPTED: at the hijack his hand ends the game in Concord Dawn if you take it).

Act V — "Sovereign" (who carries it?)

  • ★ :ch18_kestrel_ring — after K7, Morwen's Ring. Give now (+3: he can never fall) / Keep (a bigger moment at the SF if he's Redeemed, and the one thing that can reach him in the Engine if he isn't). The safest choice and the most dramatic choice are different. Then the prompt that is GO_STAY_OUT.
  • ★ :ch19_oath — at the Draco brazier, aloud. Return / Keep (Valen +3; Kaito draws into your QF: "You'd keep them? Marrowgate's still knocking."; Sigrun won't let go of the Draco Plate) / Open ("I don't know yet") / Ruin (only at Ruin-leaning or lower; Sigrun draws her blade and lets you pass). The hidden "Whatever you want to hear." is GO_LIARS_PYRE.
  • :ch19_dominar — the petition. swayed (needs the Light Stone at Wyrmrest plus proof, or Sigrun ≥ 4) / failed (the legions march on Zenith).
  • :ch20_envoy — Envoy Okoro. proof (needs two of: Larch Papers, Arbiter Blueprints, testimony that the Fixed still dream, from Teo if he lived ("I could hear everything while my hand was glass") or Corvin's ward records if he's free; no Severance) / plea.
  • :ch20_calla (new, who lives) — at the Night of Masques Calla Wren finds you, shaking: she can get the arena's power schematics to Larch. Only offered if someone inside is already moving (Rook held, or Magnus kept faith). shelter her with Edda (partial schematics, one crack) / send_back for the full set (two cracks, and Kroll finds her in the Spire stairwell; she doesn't come back) / turn_away.
  • ★ :ch20_audience — the PC answers Valen's confession. Return / Keep (he weeps, once) / Refuse ("Nobody decides for me."). If the PC refuses with a cold Bond, Ilsa never came, and the telemetry still runs, Voss's pre-ignition drains him: Valen collapses before the Final, and his successor fights you.
  • :sq_little_owl — Ilsa's quest culmination: reconciled / refused.
  • :ch21_crown — kneel / stand / step back (flavor; stepping back is a whisper of Harmony).
  • :ch21_hand — only with VOSS_ACCEPTED: take his hand (Concord Dawn now) / refuse ("You promised.").
  • :ch21_reach_kestrel (new) — only if Kestrel fell: he's harnessed into the Engine as Voss's conduit, between Voss's second and third bars. reach (needs the ring or Morwen's words; he comes back) / burn (the shard tears the gauntlet off; he lives, scarred; Ruin −3) / let_go (he burns out as the Engine fires).
  • :ch21_spare_voss — Judgment strikes the hijacker. shield (he lives, crippled, and asks to see the sunrise) / let_judge.
  • :ch21_final — the final decision (§6.1).

3. The Festival draw (Revisions 5–6: kingdom delegations, story-met characters only)

3.1 What the Festival is now

The Festival of Champions is the rite by which all Velmora chooses the one person who receives Arceus's Blessing. Sixteen stand in the Prism Arena: the Unimprinted challenger and fifteen delegates, two from each of the seven kingdoms and the Accord's Sovereign. Nobody in the bracket is a stranger: every possible opponent has been met at least twice by Act IV. The four Rev-4 outsiders are gone.

Who each kingdom sends (exact rules: NARRATIVE_KEYS §6.1):

Kingdom Seat A Seat B What decides it
Elowen Juno Celestine fixed, but Juno gives her seat to "the Gardener" (Rowan) if you earned it (R16 draw)
Antumbra Ysolde Kestrel if Gravenmoor names him (redeemed, and the ring given or Ysolde ≥ 4), else Ilsa whether the name Vane is restored or he's disowned
Zenith Rook if he held the footage and is still Meridian's face, else Odessa Kestrel under Meridian's sunburst if Gravenmoor didn't claim him; else Magnus unless exposed; else Odessa/Rook Rook's and Magnus's Ch16 choices; Kestrel's state
Nadir Kaito Nyx, or Corvin if you freed him and refused Nyx's bargain who the City Below trusts
Ossaria Sahar Bastian, unless the raid came in on your beacon (telemetry left); then the seat is lent out Ch14
Isenmar Marisol (or Anja, if only one seat is left and she's the one with the open account with you) Anja, or forfeit the war
Ashkaar Sigrun Brann, or forfeit the war
The Accord Valen — —

The war decides seats. A kingdom that lost the war (or both, if the clash broke both armies) forfeits its second seat; the Accord lends it to a Warden with no seat (Dorian first, then Tamsin, Rook, Odessa, Ilsa, Nyx, Corvin). A victor gets terms, never a third delegate.

2 Anja she holds a seat and you exposed her "You threw me to the court. Fine. Ice remembers."
