Functions moved off the retired outsiders: the Return confirmation is Celestine's, and it's an oath because she cannot lie ("Give them back. I've waited forty years to say that to someone who could."). The warning before His Hand is Larch's ("Don't let him make you a part. He's very good at parts."). The Concord Dawn closing line is Celestine's, in fairy-tale cadence: "Once upon a time a god was kept in a box. It was quiet. For a while." "That isn't him fighting" is Kaito's, because his Lucario felt it. The Ruin hint is Ilsa's: "You're thinking very loudly. Twenty-three times."

3.3 Proof: exactly one opponent per stage, never the same person twice

  1. At least one / at most one. Every list ends in an unconditional fallback who is always seated (Juno, Celestine, Kestrel, Valen; Voss), and the resolver stops at the first match.
  2. No duplicates. Persons per stage are disjoint: R16 {Marisol, Anja, Rowan, Juno}; QF {Dask, Sigrun, Kaito, Ysolde, Odessa, Celestine}; SF {Kestrel}; Final {Valen, Brann}; Hijack {Theodric, Quent, Voss}. Core's validator enforces it.
  3. Only seated people are drawn. Candidates whose seat depends on choices (Marisol, Anja, Odessa) carry seated() in their condition; Kaito, Ysolde and Sigrun are always seated; Rowan and Dask enter by replacing the default; Brann's stand-in is live and needs no seat.
  4. Fifteen seats always fill. Each seat ends in an unconditional candidate or a forfeit drawn from a pool of seven for at most three forfeits.
  5. Alive and present. Rowan needs ROWAN_ALIVE; Quent precedes Voss when Voss was removed; Brann only when Valen collapsed.

3.4 Companion matches and highlights

NPC matches resolve by rule, and the player can watch any notable one as a pixel-animated highlight (MATCH_RULES, NARRATIVE_KEYS §6.5: winner rule plus 2–4 key moments each). Order: a drawn path opponent wins until they meet the player; then the pair's rule; then rating. Examples: - Anja vs Brann, the war in miniature: Anja wins if Isenmar won or the war was stopped with the prisoners freed, else Brann. Its centre is a Blizzard–Flamethrower beam clash where, this time, nobody dies. - Kestrel vs Kaito (Kestrel wins): Lucario flinches from something that isn't the blade. - Juno, when a Grievance displaces her: she beats Ysolde at Bond ≥ 5 ("Your aunt is listening"), and loses to Sigrun with her garden at stage five. - Valen vs Sigrun (Valen): "He beat me with an arm made of glass." Valen vs Kaito: Kaito's first Aura Sphere goes a hand's width wide, on purpose.

3.5 Sample runs

Run R16 QF SF Final Hijack Arceus
"The kind one" (Teo saved, mercy, war stopped, five close Bonds) Rowan Odessa Kestrel, for Gravenmoor Valen Voss Miscalibrated
"The honest one" (chased Sable, exposed Anja and Magnus) Marisol Celestine Kestrel Valen Quent Bound
"Isenmar's sword" (backed Isenmar, occupation) Juno Sigrun Kestrel Valen Voss Bound → Unfinished at best
"Meridian's friend" (telemetry left, oath Keep, cold audience) Juno Kaito Kestrel (Fallen), for Meridian Brann Voss Gilded
"The Renegade" Juno Dask Kestrel Valen, Last Tuning Theodric Judgment

3.6 Displaced defaults, exhibitions, teams

  • Displaced: Marisol/Anja swap seats with Juno, and the QF Wardens swap with Celestine; the displaced one plays on as an NPC. Rowan takes Juno's seat (she gave it), and on the Ruin Route Dask takes Celestine's ("I cannot lie, so I won't pretend this is a game.").
  • Exhibitions (story-met only): the Pageant Bout (Dorian and Rook, a double), Juno's Garden Match if she wasn't your R16, the Ebb Guild match with Marisol (Teo saved, close), Morwen's Echo on the Night of Masques, and the Eevee Octave (Vesper is met in Ch3 and Ch13). On the Ruin Route only the Octave plays.
  • Teams: NARRATIVE_KEYS §7. 19 new Festival teams (Marisol 90, Anja 90, Rowan 90, Celestine 92, Sigrun 92, Kaito 92, Ysolde 92, Odessa 92, Dask 92, Kestrel Fallen 90–92, Brann 96, Valen 1, Quent 99, Theodric 0, Dorian 90, Rook 90, Morwen 90) plus 4 Arceus configs. Revision 6 adds none: the other delegates are NPC-only. Retired: Isaura 2, Drey 1, Mireille 2, Thorne 1, Bastian 90, Sigrun 96, and every cameo FESTIVAL_* team.

4. Game overs: the Ledger

23 canonical game overs (the story lead's 22 minus the cut "Encore", plus two new ones) and 2 Ruin-only extras. Titles, epitaphs, hints, triggers, the foreshadowing that must be on screen first, and checkpoint placement are in NARRATIVE_KEYS §9–10, verbatim. Rules every one of them follows: - Fair. Each is a deliberate player choice, warned at least once on screen in the same chapter, and most need a confirm or a repeated prompt. None fires from a cumulative stat on its own (Pull the Nail is offered only at high Hollow trust, but you still have to say yes). - Cheap to retry. Prismfall.checkpoint sits directly before each trigger, so a curious player loses seconds, not an hour. - Recorded. Each death writes its epitaph to the cross-save Ledger, where it stays across new games.

Act # Game over Kind One-line trigger
I 1 Homeward joke (tutorial) go back on the ferry
I 2 Two in the Orchard defy a warning pull Juno through the Veil
I 3 Curtain Call darkly funny join the ghost company "forever"
I 4 Live Wire defy grab the cracked Zap Plate barehanded
II 5 Sinkfield defy step off the bones for the glint
II 6 Sweet Tooth darkly funny take the Sweetrot a third time
II 7 Let Them Listen complicity take the Hammer's detonator
II 8 Pull the Nail betrayal (Sable's) plant her charge where only you can walk
III 9 Loyal choice stand with the Loyal Three
III 10 Weather (new) battle you were told to flee fight Kyogre the moment it wakes instead of running for the towers
III 11 Thin Ice failed escape go back for the data core
III 12 Read the Fine Print interlude (as Kestrel), darkly funny open Voss's drawer
III 13 Looking Back defy look back through the Glass
IV 14 Willing capture let Quent "examine" you
IV 15 Echo defy stay for your mother's echo three times
IV 16 Overtime timed keep copying past the klaxon
IV 17 Into the Line defy step between the Directorate and Nadir
IV 18 The Throne capture, darkly funny sit on the Gilded King's throne
V 19 Fair Bargain darkly funny buy an answer without asking the price
V 20 Somebody Else's Story (new) choice-based agree to stay out of Crownhaven
V 21 Liar's Pyre defy tell the brazier "Whatever you want to hear."
V 22 Early Crown betrayal (Valen's) accept the crown the night before the Final
V 23 His Hand betrayal (Voss's) take his hand at the hijack
Ruin R1 Welcome, Behind Ruin-only accept Archseer Dask's offer to stay behind the Glass
Ruin R2 Long Live the King Ruin-only kneel to Theodric

The two new ones. - Weather (Ch11). At step 4 of the Thaw, "the lake breathes". Anja, already running: "That's not a Pokémon, that's weather. Towers!" Prompt: Fight it now / Get to the towers. Fighting starts an unwinnable lv 80 Kyogre (one Primal-rain Origin Pulse). "You tried to fight the sea. The sea didn't notice. In spring, Rimeholt carved your name on the ice wall, and the thaw took that too." Choosing the towers continues to the partner battle and the real boss, as written. This is the game's one "battle you were told to flee", and it teaches the Ch21 lesson early. - Somebody Else's Story (Ch18). After K7, Kestrel: "Don't come. I mean it." Prompt: Stay out of Crownhaven? → He's already walking away. Let him go? Everyone who matters has told the PC it must be them (Celestine's "walker", Sigrun's summons, Larch's letter). "You did what everyone had told you since the ferry. The Engine took Kestrel instead. He lasted eleven minutes. Velmora lasted a week."

Cut: "Encore" (Ch20). It needed four conditions to fire and duplicated Into the Line's lesson ("don't make them choose"). Dorian's ad-lib stays as flavor; the PC simply doesn't go up.

Hints. After the first ending is seen (or with the ledgerhints password), unfound Ledger slots show a one-line riddle instead of "???". Hesper Vane's lighthouse mirror has one crack per game over found; at 23 the cracks make a door shape (the only diegetic hint that the Ledger means something).

5. The Ruin Route: "Every Type and None"

5.1 Unlock and branch

  • Unlock: all 23 canonical game overs in the Ledger (ruin_unlocked).
  • In-story trigger: Ch15, right after the Giratina boss in the Reverse Depths. A pane of black glass shows Juno on the other side, calling the PC's name: the exact image of Looking Back (GO 13). On a save with a full Ledger, the prompt appears again: Look back? Saying yes no longer kills you. The reflection steps aside, and the PC walks through. (:ch15_look_back; no keeps the main story and the door never reopens on that save.) The branch comes after K6, so Kestrel's state is already set.
  • Why it needs every death: inside the Glass, the PC finds out that every game over happened. Antumbra is the reverse side of the world, where things that almost were are kept, and Giratina kept every one of them. You can't walk into the Reverse until it knows your face, and it learns your face from your graves.

5.2 What the Ruin Route reveals (never stated on the main route)

  1. The Ledger is real. The Reverse Depths' floor is a field of twenty-three graves, each carved with its epitaph. "You have no Imprint in Velmora. In here, you have twenty-three." The PC's Reflection stands up from the last grave: the one who remembers dying every time.
  2. Antumbra is an unfinished Judgment. At AF 0, when the Chain closed, Arceus began to judge Aurelia: to unmake it. The Prismfall was the god choosing to break itself rather than finish. The half-done unmaking is Antumbra. Rivers run uphill there because that's what a world looks like halfway to nothing. Giratina, guardian of reverse places, has held the seam shut for three hundred years so the sentence can't finish.
  3. The verse of none. Maren carved a third verse of the Hymn of Return on the Reverse side of the Concordance, where only the Glass could read it: "And if the walker comes with no color in their heart, the god will come home to finish what it began." A whole Arceus, returned to a land that chained it, could complete the Judgment of AF 0. Celestine knew: "I cannot lie, so I stopped singing it."
  4. What "I learned that here" means. In every main-route ending Arceus says "I will not take what is not given. I learned that here." It's talking about Judgment. It came within one heartbeat of unmaking a kingdom, and chose to break instead. Homecoming works because the Wardens give, and the PC carries the shard in Harmony. The Ruin Route is the PC deciding nobody, god or Crown or company, should hold that power again.
  5. The Gilded King is still alive, behind the glass. Antumbra froze the Sun Court at the instant of the Calling. Theodric's reflection stands in it mid-command, the reflected Chain in his hands. Voss has dreamed of him all his life.

5.3 Chapter outline (replaces the rest of Ch15 through Ch21)

  • R1 "Twenty-Three Graves" (Reverse Depths). The graves; the Reflection, who speaks in the PC's own authored lines, reversed. Mirror battle: the Reflection (your own party, generated at your levels, on :INVERSE). Then Giratina (Origin) tests the Renegade (multi-bar trial on :INVERSE). Giratina gives the PC the none: Resonance locks at ruin (≤ −6), and the Ledger becomes a Key Item, the Black Case. New traversal, Mirrorwalk: marked mirrors in Zenith, Nadir, Elowen and Ashkaar open onto their Reverse maps.
  • R2 "Built to Break" (Cogsworth, Gym 15). The PC now wants the Engine finished: it's the only machine that can hold all eighteen Plates still long enough to unmake them. Zekrom still has to be freed (the core must fail so Voss reroutes everything to Crownhaven). :ch16_magnus reads differently: expose means you ruin him so nobody stops the build; conscience means you persuade him to build it "to break right". Gym 15 (Ruin): Magnus on :FACTORY, which becomes :SCRAPYARD on turn one: "You came here to break it. So did I, once."
  • R3 "The Sun Court Below" (Underveil, Gym 16; Aurelia). Nyx sides with the Renegade; her house kept the drowned truth for three hundred years and she's tired of it. Gym 16 (Ruin): an illusion double battle where :NEW_MOON runs with Inverse matchups. In the Sun Court, Theodric's reflection fills the mirror-wall. Voss walks in, sees him, and falls to his knees: "He's alive. Behind the glass he's still alive." He leaves with the cradle, serving a dead king now.
  • R4 "Midnight in Gloaming" (Gym 17; Pecharunt; K7). Celestine tells the PC the verse of none, and refuses the badge. Gym 17 (Ruin): :FAIRY_TALE turns :INVERSE for her last Pokémon ("Once upon a time, which was the only time"). Xerneas does not wake; the dusk stays. K7 plays as written, but Kestrel knows: "You're going to erase her? Everything she paid for?" :ch18_kestrel_ring is still offered.
  • R5 "The Last Gate" (Wyrmrest, Gym 18). The oath can only be true one way: "I'm going to end it. All of it." Reshiram accepts the truth and flies away without a fight. Gym 18 (Ruin): Sigrun on :DRAGONS_DEN, no Reshiram, every Pokémon a step faster ("Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed. I've weighed you."). The Dominar can't be swayed; his legions march on Zenith during the Festival.
  • R6 "The Unfestival" (Festival week). The Accord bars "the Renegade". Archseer Dask claims the PC for the Veiled Synod and walks them in through the Glass; Dorian, who has never once let the bowl ruin a story, seeds them anyway. R16 Juno ("You'll make sure Verdance never gets an autumn."), QF Archseer Dask (his offer to stay behind the Glass is GO_R_WELCOME_BEHIND), SF Kestrel (he tears the Gauntlet off to fight you as himself), Final Valen, the Last Tuning ("I'll hold them quiet one more time. I'll die doing it. That's still better than you."). No exhibitions. Ilsa, earmuffs off: "You're thinking very loudly. Twenty-three times." At the Night of Masques, the actor playing the Gilded King freezes mid-line, because the mirror behind the stage is showing the real one.
  • R7 "Every Type and None" (the finale). At the Coronation the Engine wakes, but the hand that reaches up through the arena's crystal walls isn't only Voss's: Theodric's reflection steps through wearing the Chain. Voss kneels (kneel with him: GO_R_KNEEL). When Theodric casts him aside, :ruin_voss: spare him or abandon him to the Chain. Hijack: Theodric. Then Arceus descends, not dragged but coming, to finish what it began. True Finale: Arceus (Judgment), seven bars, Giratina at the PC's side (player side of two), the last bar on :INVERSE as Giratina turns the arena inside out. Valen holds the Ring one last time, to keep the crowd alive while god and Renegade fight. Then the Unmaking.

5.4 Final boss, endings and cost

  • Final boss: Arceus (Judgment), ARCEUS_JUDGMENT (NARRATIVE_KEYS §6.6).
  • The Unmaking needs a vessel. The none unmakes the Chain, the Plates, Judgment and finally the Glass, and whoever carries it goes with the Glass. The Reflection offers to go in the PC's place: "I've done this twenty-three times. Let me do it once on purpose." Then :ruin_walk: who walks out of the Glass?
  • :me → END_UNMAKING "The Unmaking". The Reflection is unmade with the Glass. Antumbra and Velmora fold into each other. Every Imprint ends at once: no fields, no Bleed, no miracles; Rimeholt thaws, Cindervault cools, Verdance gets its first autumn (Juno won't look at the PC while it falls). The Fixed wake, all of them, even the long-Fixed who had stopped dreaming, and some of them wake as strangers. Arceus, with nothing left to judge or be chained by, leaves as a light with no color and never comes back. Giratina, the final witness, closes its eyes. Mirrors are just mirrors. The PC is the only person alive who remembers the old world, and twenty-three ways it ended. The scar is still colorless, by choice.
  • :reflection → END_REFLECTION "The One Who Came Back" (secret). The PC is unmade with the Glass, and the Reflection walks out wearing their face, which is GO 13's epitaph come true. The epilogue is the same world as the Unmaking, seen slightly wrong: the Reflection signs its name mirrored once and corrects it; Juno, raking the first leaves: "You never used to hum." Last image: Hesper's lighthouse mirror, with nothing behind it.
  • Post-game: Antumbra's mirrors stay open as ordinary places (the Reverse maps), Giratina is catchable at the Distortion Core, there is no Arceus, and the Festival never runs again. On END_REFLECTION the post-game is played as the Reflection (nobody else notices).

5.5 Why it's worth playing

It answers the three questions the main route leaves open (what Antumbra is, why the Ledger remembers, what Arceus meant by "I learned that here"), and once you know them, Homecoming reads differently: it was one consenting Warden away from the end of the world. It adds four re-fought gyms, a new Festival bracket (Dask, Valen of the Last Tuning, Theodric), the only scenes where the Gilded King speaks, Giratina as an ally, Mirrorwalk maps, two endings, and two more graves for completionists.

6. Endings

6.1 The final decisions

  1. Ch17, the Sun Court (:ch17_voss_offer). Accepting is a promise you keep or break later.
  2. Ch21, the hijack (:ch21_hand, only if you accepted). Taking Voss's hand ends the game in Concord Dawn before Arceus is ever fought. Without the promise, the same gesture is GO_HIS_HAND.
  3. Ch21, the True Finale. If the Engine is fully calibrated, the PC fights for Arceus and loses the struggle anyway: The Gilded Chain.
  4. Ch21, Arceus speaks (:ch21_final). The PC says it aloud: - Return — "Go home. Take them with you." (always) - Keep — "I'll keep them. I'll keep everyone safe." (always) - Borrowed — the PC hesitates, and Kestrel (redeemed and devoted) or Sigrun (Bond ≥ 6) steps forward: "Not you. Let me be nearest." - Concord — "Stay. Stay and power the world." (if you accepted Voss's offer, or Magnus's governor was never switched off) - Unmake — "No. No more gods." (main route: Resonance ≤ −6, or Hollow trust ≥ 4 with Rowan radicalized or Kroll never publicly exposed)
  5. Ruin Route, R7 (:ruin_walk). Who walks out of the Glass.

6.2 What decides the outcome

  • Wardens' Consent = how many of the 18 Wardens release their Plate (NARRATIVE_KEYS §5 WARDEN_CONSENT, one condition each, in gym order). It's on screen twice: Tamsin's white and grey banners (Ch20), and the Arceus fight itself, which is six bars of three locks each. Between bars those three Wardens kneel and let go, or refuse. Every refusal makes the next lock forced (Arceus gains +1 to every stat stage for that bar). Each refusal has a reason the player caused: Tamsin can't afford it (her secret kept), Dorian keeps the lie he knows (you told the official story), Rook can't reach his Plate, Corvin is in a cell, Brann won't for a man who gave the Light Stone away, Kaito never confessed, Anja "drowns without it", Odessa remembers the burned letter, Bastian remembers the beacon, Ysolde lost Kestrel, Magnus "won't hand my city's future to a god", Celestine hears the wrong key, Sigrun meant to wear the Crown.
  • Engine breaks = eleven lamps on Larch's board: telemetry destroyed or sabotaged (sabotage counts twice), Zekrom freed, Magnus's conscience, Rook still inside, Nyx's precise blackout, Calla's schematics (twice if she went back), Marchetti turned, the Lantern Hollow at the pylons, Kestrel not lost. The Engine is calibrated (Bad ending live) only if the telemetry was left running, Magnus wasn't reached, and three or fewer lamps are lit.

6.3 Conditions matrix (first row that matches wins; exact strings: NARRATIVE_KEYS §12)

# Ending Kind Condition
1 The One Who Came Back Ruin (secret) Ruin Route and the Reflection walks out
2 The Unmaking Ruin Ruin Route
3 Concord Dawn Grey took Voss's hand (after accepting in Ch17), or chose Concord
4 The Gilded Chain Bad (canon) Engine calibrated
5 The Hollow Spring Grey chose Unmake (main route)
6 The Borrowed Crown Grey let Kestrel or Sigrun take it
7 The Long Vigil Grey chose Keep
8 Homecoming, Unfinished Grey Return, but Consent < 12 or Resonance < 2
9 The Empty Lighthouse (new) Grey Return with Consent and Harmony, but Kestrel burned out in the Engine
10 Homecoming Good (canon) Return, Consent ≥ 12, Resonance ≥ 2, Kestrel alive

6.4 Epilogue beats

The story lead's scenes in 08_ACT_V stand for Homecoming, the Gilded Chain, the Long Vigil, Unfinished, the Hollow Spring, Concord Dawn and the Borrowed Crown. This spec adds the conditions that vary them, and three new endings. - Homecoming (Good). As written. The variable slides: Elin wakes in Rowan's arms or, if Rowan drowned at the Fault, Juno alone holds her aunt and tells her; Teo on the Ebbmouth quay or his photo on the guild wall; Kestrel as Ghost Warden (KESTREL_ACCEPTED_SEAT) or a Gale Post wanderer; Calla's name on the Spire memorial if she went back; the quarantine lifts now (ENVOY_PERSUADED) or a year late; Voss watches the sunrise (spared) or is light at the arena's heart; Nadir's first elected seat with Nyx in it. - Homecoming, Unfinished (Grey). One slide per refusing Warden's city, in gym order, each a single image of what the Last Shatter took ("Briarwick: the looms went still in one night. Tamsin weaves by hand now."). If Resonance was below Harmony, the PC's scar never takes the land's color. - The Empty Lighthouse (Grey, new). Everything in Homecoming happens, but Kestrel burned out as the Engine's conduit. Hesper climbs the lighthouse every night and lights a lamp nobody needs. Ysolde keeps the Ghost seat. The PC reads Morwen's letter aloud at the grave he never visited, the letter he never opened. Last slide: Kestrel's Aegislash, sheathed, on the PC's belt. - The Long Vigil / The Borrowed Crown (Grey). As written; the bearer is Kestrel if KESTREL_BEARS, else Sigrun. With Sigrun: "Ten years. Maybe twelve. I was relieved once. Never again." Juno and the PC swear to find another door either way. - The Hollow Spring (Grey). As written. If Rowan drowned, Thistle plants the first seed in plain soil; if Teo lived, he's the one who won't speak to her. - Concord Dawn (Grey). As written, plus: if reached through :ch21_hand, the PC never fights Arceus at all, and the last slide is the PC reading the arena's power output on a Council screen with Celestine's line over it: "Once upon a time a god was kept in a box. It was quiet. For a while." - The Gilded Chain (Bad). As written. The PC as "the Sovereign Who Sleeps". If Quent hijacked, the mug on the desk is Quent's, not Voss's, and Voss is in the crowd of schoolchildren's parents, visiting. - The Unmaking / The One Who Came Back (Ruin). §5.4.

6.5 Post-game state

Per NARRATIVE_KEYS §12. In short: full post-game (Concordance Spire, Warden's Gauntlet, Distortion Core, legendary rematches) after Homecoming, Unfinished (refusers are absent from the Gauntlet and their cities keep Bleed scars), Empty Lighthouse, the Long Vigil (the PC is Sovereign; the Gauntlet is framed as Sovereign's audiences), the Borrowed Crown (the bearer is absent) and both Ruin endings (Mirrorwalk maps stay open). Partial after the Hollow Spring (Spire and Gauntlet only; most legendaries are gone with their Plates) and Concord Dawn (Spire only; the Wardens are pensioned off; Quent's Concord Zero arc starts). None after the Gilded Chain: credits, then the title screen. Zygarde chooses the PC at Valen's memorial in any ending with a memorial if Valen's Bond is 3 or more, or Ilsa reconciled with him (ZYGARDE_CHOOSES).

7. Other systems

  1. Quiet Hours (relationship scenes). A bench icon at Pokémon Centers when a Bond first reaches friendly and again at close: 20 short scenes (Juno's seed catalogue, Kestrel teaching the PC to throw stones at the lighthouse, Ysolde lighting a lantern for Ines). Each grants +1 and a keepsake at close (Juno's seed packet = a Grassy Seed; Marisol's union pin; Nyx's crescent earring, which sells for one Poké Dollar and is priceless). Impl.: one common event per scene; condition bond_tier(:X) == :friendly && !made(:qh_x1), register :qh_<bond><n> choice keys with value [:seen].
  2. Interludes. The act files already define five (Rowan, Kestrel, Wenlow, Valen…). Add Interlude VI "The Night Ferry" as Morwen in AF 290, unlocked after any ending: a stealth walk carrying the five-year-old PC to Edda's boat. Its last line is Morwen's echo's line from Ch15. Impl.: pbChangePlayer to the interlude character, stash $player.party in Core state, give a preset party, restore on exit; interlude choices never touch the PC's Bonds (except where the act file says so).
  3. Field Notes. Each field gets an entry on first battle; individual effects fill in only once observed ("Fire moves hit harder here."). :BLEED keeps a log of which fields it has borrowed. Impl.: listen to :prismfall_field_changed; Field Effects marks effect lines as seen when they trigger; Core stores a set per field.
  4. The Ledger viewer and Hall of Reflections. The Ledger also records every Festival opponent variant the player has met. Post-game, the Distortion Core's Hall of Reflections lets them re-fight any variant they've met in any playthrough (Kestrel Fallen, the Gardener, Brann for Valen, Quent…) at lv 100. It reuses every team the balance team built. Impl.: Ledger lines MET:<ENTRANT>; the hall's menu reads them.
  5. The Second Ferry (New Game+). After any ending, the secondferry password starts a new game that keeps the Ledger (always), Field Notes, Pokédex seen flags and one Pokémon of choice from the Hall of Fame as "a passenger". Ines's letter gains a postscript that matches the last ending seen. The Journal shows faint "echo" lines under choices made in the previous run. Impl.: a carryover file written at the ending, read by the password handler.
  6. The Velmora Gazette. Two headlines per chapter per Pokémon Center, chosen by condition strings from a table (§1.4). Impl.: GAZETTE[chapter] = [[condition, headline], …], first match per slot, shown by a newspaper event.
  7. Promises. A Quest Log category for things the PC says they'll do ("I'll come back for a Fizzbolt," to Dex; "I'll look after her," to Rowan). Kept promises pay off in epilogue slides; broken ones get one quiet line. Impl.: choice keys :promise_<name> with [:made, :kept, :broken]; the Quest Log lists :made ones.
  8. Passwords. Core's table plus ledgerhints, nomercy (no checkpoints before game overs), openbook (numbers on the Ties page), colorless (halved Resonance swings) and secondferry (NARRATIVE_KEYS §14).

8. Requests for the story lead (reconciliation list)

Edits the act files need so they match this spec. Keys and conditions are in NARRATIVE_KEYS; nothing here changes a LOCKED SPINE item. 1. Bond mentions for non-Bond characters (Tamsin, Sahar, Brann, Anja, Rook, Corvin): replace "X's Bond goes up/down" with the choice key; their payoff is their Consent condition (NARRATIVE_KEYS §5). Ysolde and Marisol are Bonds now. 3. 04 Ch2: add the Glass Matriarch prompt (:ch2_matriarch: soothe / silence) after the boss. 4. 04 Ch5: add Dorian's refusal line for the Arceus fight if the PC told the Directorate "The Mourner broke the Plate". 5. 06 Ch11: add the Fight it now / Get to the towers prompt at "the lake breathes" (GO_WEATHER); make the strike's optional tag battle the :ch11_strike choice (stand / mediate). 6. 06 Ch13 tribunal: the Splitter Casing alone is circumstantial. Public exposure of Kroll at the tribunal needs the Ironreach comm-device; the casing still lets the PC turn Marchetti in Ch14. 7. 06 Ch13 Fault (new MAJOR): add :ch13_fault. Knock-ons: the Lantern Hollow is led by Teo (if saved) or by Thistle and Wick; "the Gardener" can't enter R16; Homecoming's Bride Tree scene is Juno alone; the Hollow Spring's seed is Thistle's; Rowan's Act IV–V lines go to whoever leads. 8. 07 Ch15: after the Giratina boss, the Ruin trigger (only with a full Ledger), §5.1. 9. 07 Ch16 :ch16_rook: leak_now gets Rook fired and blacklisted from the Spire, so he can't cut the grid in Ch21; hold keeps him inside. Update the hijack montage line to fire on ROOK_CUTS_GRID. 10. 07 Ch17: Nyx's refuse branch is fine as written (her blackout happens, but it doesn't count as an Engine break). 11. 08 Ch18: after K7, add Stay out of Crownhaven? (GO_STAY_OUT). 12. 08 Ch19: Liar's Pyre's hidden option is simply always offered (no lie tracking). Anja (exposed) replaces the story lead's Tamsin variant, which is cut (no Tamsin Bond to read). 15. 08 Ch21: Fallen Kestrel is not a hijack opponent; he's harnessed as Voss's conduit and :ch21_reach_kestrel plays between Voss's second and third bars. The tag partner is Juno when he's Fallen. 16. 08 Ch21 Arceus: six bars of three locks in gym order; write all 18 release lines and all 18 refusal lines (reasons in §6.2). 17. 08 Endings: add The Empty Lighthouse; the Gilded Chain's Quent variant; the two Ruin endings (§5.4) replace the Unmaking sketch, and Archseer Dask is the Ruin QF (his offer is GO_R_WELCOME_BEHIND). 18. Story Bible 12: The Ruin Route — "Every Type and None" (R1–R7)

Mechanics, unlock and endings are specified in 10_CHOICES_ENDINGS.md §5 and NARRATIVE_KEYS §11. This file is the chapter outline at the same depth as the main acts. It replaces the rest of Ch15 through Ch21 on a save with a full Ledger where the PC answers yes to Look back? (:ch15_look_back) after the Giratina boss. Tone: quiet, cold and lucid. The PC isn't a villain. They have seen every way this world ends and have stopped asking it to be saved. Everyone who loved them on the main route still does, which is the horror of it. Revisions 4–6 applied: no sister-game cameos in the Festival (Nova is an R5 sidequest); every opponent is met earlier on this route or the main one; the Festival is the ceremony of the Blessing; the war still happens.

What this route reveals (never stated on the main route): the Ledger is real; Antumbra is an unfinished Judgment; the Hymn has a third verse, "the verse of none"; "I learned that here" means Arceus stopped one heartbeat short of unmaking Aurelia; and the Gilded King is still alive behind the Glass.

R1 — "Twenty-Three Graves" (the Reverse Depths)

  • Location: Giratina's Reverse Depths (806, :INVERSE), then a new floor beneath it.
  • Beats: 1. The PC steps through the black glass. Juno's reflection steps aside, and it isn't Juno. 2. The floor is a field of twenty-three graves, each carved with one of the PC's epitaphs from the Ledger. "You have no Imprint in Velmora. In here, you have twenty-three." 3. The Reflection stands up from the last grave: the PC's own sprite, mirrored, the one who remembers dying every time. It speaks in the PC's authored lines, reversed ("Yet not... too scared I'm... me show"). 4. Mirror battle: the Reflection. The PC's own party at their levels, on :INVERSE. 5. Giratina (Origin) tests the Renegade: a multi-bar trial, not a hunt. It ends when the PC stops attacking for one turn. 6. Giratina gives the PC the none. Resonance locks at Ruin (≤ −6). The Ledger becomes a Key Item, the Black Case, and the PC's badge case turns black to match.
  • New traversal: Mirrorwalk. Marked mirrors in Zenith, Nadir, Elowen and Ashkaar open onto Reverse versions of those maps.
  • Archseer Dask is waiting at the Depths' mouth (third meeting): "Welcome, behind. You looked. I told you never to look. I'm glad one of us disobeys."
  • Cliffhanger: the Reflection falls into step behind the PC like a second shadow. Only Pokémon and Celestine can see it.

R2 — "Built to Break" (Cogsworth, Gym 15)

  • Premise: the PC now wants the Engine finished. It is the only machine that can hold all eighteen Plates still long enough to unmake them.
  • Beats: 1. Cogsworth as in Ch16, seen colder: the PC walks past Rook's canteen table. If the player sits anyway, Rook: "You look like me after the Lowlight." 2. Zekrom must still be freed, so the core fails and Voss reroutes everything to the Temple-Arena. The boss plays as written, but when it's free it doesn't flee. It looks at the PC and recoils. 3. :ch16_magnus reads differently. Expose ruins him so nobody can stop the build. Conscience persuades him to build it "to break right", and he understands exactly what he's being asked.
  • Gym 15 (Ruin): Magnus on :FACTORY, which becomes :SCRAPYARD on turn one. "You came here to break it. So did I, once."
  • War beat: the foundry is casting Prism lances for both armies. The PC can stop it or not, and the game no longer tells them which is kinder.
  • Cliffhanger: the Sump Purge on the screens. The Reflection says its first forward sentence: "We died there once. It didn't help."

R3 — "The Sun Court Below" (Underveil, Gym 16; Aurelia)

  • Beats: 1. Underveil after the Purge. Nyx sides with the Renegade without being asked: her house kept the drowned truth for three hundred years and she's tired of it. "You're going to end the secret-keeping business. I should be upset." 2. Gym 16 (Ruin): the illusion double battle, with :NEW_MOON running Inverse matchups. 3. Aurelia by Mirrorwalk: the Sun Court's mirror-wall is a window. Behind the glass the court stands frozen at the instant of the Calling, and Theodric's reflection is in it mid-command, the reflected Chain in his hands. His eyes move. (First meeting with the route's hijacker.) 4. Voss walks in, sees him, and falls to his knees: "He's alive. Behind the glass he's still alive." He leaves with the cradle, serving a dead king now. The PC watches the most reasonable man in Velmora become a zealot in ten seconds.
  • Game over hook: GO_THE_THRONE is already in the Ledger, so the throne's armrests are closed fists now. Sitting does nothing. The Reflection: "We've done that one."
  • Cliffhanger: Theodric's reflection mouths one word at the PC through the glass: "Finish."

R4 — "Midnight in Gloaming" (Gym 17; Pecharunt; K7)

  • Beats: 1. Pecharunt in the Lightless Deep, as written. The thralls wake and thank the PC, and it lands wrong. 2. Celestine tells the PC the verse of none, which Maren carved on the Reverse side of the Concordance: "And if the walker comes with no color in their heart, the god will come home to finish what it began." "I cannot lie, so I stopped singing it." 3. She refuses the badge, and fights for it anyway. Gym 17 (Ruin): :FAIRY_TALE turns :INVERSE for her last Pokémon ("Once upon a time, which was the only time"). 4. The dusk does not lift. Ogerpon, if it walked with the PC, leaves at the Verge and goes back to the Bride Tree. 5. K7 plays as written, but Kestrel knows: "You're going to erase her? Everything she paid for?" :ch18_kestrel_ring is still offered. Giving it is the last warm thing on the route.
  • Cliffhanger: GO_STAY_OUT's prompt appears and can't be answered yes. The cursor won't move there.

R5 — "The Last Gate" (Wyrmrest, Gym 18; the Shattered Ice; Nova)

  • Beats: 1. The oath can only be true one way: "I'm going to end it. All of it." Reshiram accepts the truth and flies to the front without a fight. 2. Gym 18 (Ruin): Sigrun on :DRAGONS_DEN, no Reshiram, every Pokémon a step faster. "Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed. I've weighed you." 3. The Battle of the Shattered Ice (14_WAR_AND_BLESSING). The PC can't stand between: the option reads "End it." The shard doesn't calm the beam clash. It eats it, and both legendaries' beams go out like candles. WAR-1 plays with shots 10–12 replaced: the clash-ball turns black and vanishes, Kyogre and Reshiram both flee the PC, and both armies stop fighting out of fear. The truce is called anyway. Nobody thanks the PC. 4. The Dominar can't be swayed, and neither ruler will ride with the Renegade.
  • Sidequest: Nova (CAMEOS.md §9), on the Mirrorwalk dream-road out of Gloaming. She offers a gentler unmaking: everyone asleep and happy forever. Accepting is GO_R_SWEET_DREAMS. Refusing is a fight, and the Crescent Charm.
  • Cliffhanger: on the Sovereign's Ascent the Wardguard bars the road. Dask's Synod opens a mirror in the cliff instead.

R6 — "The Unfestival" (Festival week)

  • Beats: 1. The Accord bars "the Renegade" from the Blessing. No kingdom offers its colors. Dask enters the PC as Antumbra's second delegate, "Unsworn, behind", and Dorian, who has never once let the bowl ruin a story, seeds them himself. 2. Opening ceremony: the Plates dim as the PC passes each pylon. The vision of Arceus is the same eye, but it speaks differently: "Little light. You have carried me far. Why are you so cold?" 3. R16: Juno. "You'll make sure Verdance never gets an autumn." She fights to stop the PC, and cries the whole match. 4. QF: Archseer Dask (met Ch13, Ch15, R1). He fights to measure the PC, on :INVERSE: "Behind, welcome. Welcome, behind. The Synod must know what it is letting through." 5. SF: Kestrel. He tears the Gauntlet off to fight the PC as himself. If he has the ring: "She was nearest. I'm nearest now. Stop." 6. Night of Masques: the actor playing the Gilded King freezes mid-line, because the mirror behind the stage is showing the real one. 7. Valen's audience is three lines long. Valen: "I know what you are now. I'll hold them quiet one more time. I'll die doing it. That's still better than you." 8. Final: Valen, the Last Tuning. He fights with the Crown on and the Plates lit behind him.
  • No exhibitions except the Eevee Octave. Vesper won't look at the PC, but Understudy's siblings do.
  • Cliffhanger: the Blessing. Nobody will raise the Crown. The PC picks it up off the arena floor.

R7 — "Every Type and None" (the finale)

  • Beats: 1. The Engine wakes, but the hand that reaches up through the arena's crystal walls isn't only Voss's. Theodric's reflection steps through wearing the Chain, and the Aureate court is visible behind him in every reflective surface in Crownhaven. 2. Voss kneels. Theodric invites the PC to kneel with him: GO_R_KNEEL. 3. Theodric casts Voss aside into the Chain. CHOICE :ruin_voss: spare him (the PC pulls him out; he lives, hollow) or abandon him. 4. Hijack: Theodric, the Gilded Reflection. His team is the Gilders' court: Golurk, Aegislash, Gholdengo-gold reflections of Wardens' aces, on :PRISM_ARENA running Inverse. 5. Arceus descends, not dragged but coming, to finish what it began in AF 0. Valen holds the Ring one last time, to keep the crowd alive while god and Renegade fight. No Warden releases anything; all eighteen locks hold. 6. True Finale: Arceus (Judgment), seven bars, with Giratina at the PC's side. The last bar is on :INVERSE as Giratina turns the arena inside out. 7. The Unmaking needs a vessel. The none unmakes the Chain, the Plates, Judgment and finally the Glass, and whoever carries it goes with the Glass. The Reflection offers: "I've done this twenty-three times. Let me do it once on purpose." 8. CHOICE :ruin_walk: who walks out of the Glass.
  • Endings (10_CHOICES_ENDINGS §5.4):
  • :me → "The Unmaking". The Reflection is unmade with the Glass. Every Imprint ends at once. All the Fixed wake, even the long-Fixed, some of them as strangers. Verdance gets its autumn, and Juno won't look at the PC while it falls. Arceus leaves as a light with no color and never returns. The PC is the only person who remembers the old world and twenty-three ways it ended.
  • :reflection → "The One Who Came Back" (secret). The PC is unmade, and the Reflection walks out wearing their face. The epilogue is the same world seen slightly wrong: a signature mirrored once and corrected, and Juno saying "You never used to hum." Last image: Hesper's cracked mirror, with nothing behind it.
  • Post-game: Mirrorwalk maps stay open as ordinary places, Giratina is catchable, there is no Arceus, and the Blessing is never held again.

Requests to other teams

  • World: four Mirrorwalk Reverse maps (Zenith, Nadir, Elowen, Ashkaar) and the graves floor under 806.
  • Balance: the Reflection (mirror party), four Ruin gym teams, Dask (QF), Valen (Last Tuning), Theodric, Arceus (Judgment), Nova.
  • Cutscenes: the WAR-1 Ruin variant (shots 10–12).

Story Bible 13: Cutscene Storyboards (SPINE Revision 6)

Shot-by-shot storyboards for the Prismfall Cutscenes plugin (DSL spec: design/CUTSCENES.md when it lands). Each shot lists camera, sprites/action, FX, text. Durations are suggestions at 60 FPS feel. Sprites are battle/overworld Pokémon and trainer sprites; no new painted art is assumed except the listed backdrops. Variants are selected by narrative keys (NARRATIVE_KEYS). Proposed keys are marked (proposed).

WAR-1 — "The Beam Clash on the Shattered Ice" (Ch19 climax; ~95 s)

Backdrop: the Shattered Ice (broken sea-ice plates, black water between; Skaldfast's walls left, Cinder Cape Warfort right; dawn). Variants (NARRATIVE_KEYS §4.3): A = WAR_STOPPED, A′ = WAR_BOTH_BROKEN (shots 1–10 as A; then shot 11 is a white-out, shot 12 is cut, and shot 13 shows both lines gone and the two rulers alone on the glass), B = WAR_ASHKAAR_WON, C = WAR_ISENMAR_WON. If :war_legend kept the legendaries away, shots 2–5 use Sigrun's Salamence (Draco Meteor, then Flamethrower) against Anja's Mamoswine or Marisol's Gyarados (Blizzard / Hydro Pump); the clash in shot 6 is unchanged.

# Camera Action FX Text
1 Wide, slow pan left→right across both hosts (4 s) Isenmar: sled-ships, Walrein and Mamoswine ranks, Thane Eirik on the lead prow. Ashkaar: riders on Salamence/Charizard in a sky column, the Dominar on the Warfort wall Snow overlay left, ember overlay right; they meet mid-screen —
2 Cut: low angle on the black water (2 s) The water bulges. Kyogre rises, Primal markings lit Rain starts; screen tint blue; rumble Eirik: "Isenmar! The sea remembers it's a sea!"
3 Cut: the Warfort wall (2 s) The Dominar lifts the Light Stone. It cracks white. Reshiram unfolds above him White flash; heat-haze shader; tint orange on the right half Hrothgar: "Then let it remember fire."
4 Split screen, vertical seam (2 s) Left: Kyogre's mouth gathers a blue sphere. Right: Reshiram's tail-turbine ignites blue-white Charge particles pull inward on both sides; rising whine —
5 Wide (1.5 s) Both fire. Origin Pulse (blue-white beam, left) and Blue Flare (white-blue fire beam, right) cross the ice Beam sprites extend 12 frames each —
6 Center, hard zoom on the meeting point (3 s) BEAM CLASH. The beams lock. The clash point shudders left, then right, then holds Clash-ball sprite, pulsing; screen shake 6 px; white strobe every 8 frames; ice plates crack outward in a ring —
7 Cut: ice surface (2 s) Soldiers on both sides thrown flat. Ice turns to steam-glass in a spreading circle Steam overlay; glass crackle SFX; :BLEED palette shimmer Soldier: "The Steamline! It's coming this way!"
8 Pull back, high wide (3 s) A dome of Bleed light swells from the clash point over both front ranks. Figures at its edge begin to glass Dome sprite scales up; color bleed to prismatic —
9A Track right, ground level (4 s) The PC walks into the dome, alone, toward the clash. Their starter beside them PC's chest glows colorless; dome dims in a circle around them (no text; footsteps only)
9B/9C Track with the PC's mount/boat (4 s) The PC rides Sigrun's Salamence (B) / stands on Marisol's prow (C) straight at the clash Same glow Sigrun (B): "Hold on!" / Marisol (C): "Tides take it, kid, now!"
10 Close on the PC, beams overhead (3 s) The PC raises a hand. The shard's light goes up into the clash-ball Colorless ring expands; both beams desaturate to white —
11 Center (3 s) The clash-ball goes silent and collapses into a single falling mote. Both beams gutter out All FX cut to silence; one soft chime; rain turns to snow —
12 Two-shot (4 s) Kyogre and Reshiram both look down at the PC. Hold. Kyogre sinks. Reshiram banks away Water ripple; wingbeat wind Field Notes pop-up: "It looked at me as if I were very small and very familiar. Again."
13A Wide, the glass between the armies (5 s) Sigrun lands on one side of the PC, Anja walks out on the other. They face their own rulers Snow; no music Sigrun: "Father. Enough." Anja: "Eirik. Enough." Both: "The Boon will settle it."
13B/C Wide (5 s) The losing host breaks. The winner's banners go up over Skaldfast (B) or Cinder Cape (C) Smoke Victor: "It's done." Loser's Warden, to the PC: "I'll remember which side you stood on."
14 Cut: a ridge behind the lines (4 s) Meridian observers in white coats fold a brass tripod and box their dials. One is Dr. Quent, humming Sunburst logo glint Quent: "Lovely reading. Simply lovely."
15 Slow fade (3 s) The steam-glass plain at sunrise, both armies' glassed dead standing in it — Larch (radio): "That wasn't a battle to them. That was a reading."

Skippable: yes, after first viewing. Replay: Dream Mist / theater menu.

All on the Temple-Arena backdrop with the active gym field's tint. Outcomes follow the player's choices.

Renames / adoptions for the World lead

All World names from WORLD.md §8–9 are adopted as given: Pyre Keep (Cindervault), the Palace of Glass (Lucent), Rime Narrows, Skaldfast, the Ashkaar Siege Camp, Cinder Cape Warfort, the Steamline, the Shattered Ice, the Temple-Arena of the Blessing, the Court of Eight Pavilions, the Ice Vault. Story-side names that now map onto them: - "Ember Citadel" (Wyrmrest) and "Mirror Palace" → retired in favor of Pyre Keep and the Palace of Glass. - "Meridian Prism Arena" = the Temple-Arena of the Blessing's Meridian-era rebuild name (both are used in dialogue). - Story still uses: the Holdfast (the Thane's fortress in Rimeholt), Briar Keep (Verdance), Sandglass Palace and Valley of Tombs (Duneholt), Council Spire (Lumen City), Moon Court (Underveil). Please adopt or send replacements. - Requested small maps: the Dominar's command pavilion (Siege Camp interior); the ridge with Meridian's observers (WAR-1 shot 14).

Story Bible 15: Comedy (SPINE Revision 7 — "we need funny moments!")

The game gets darker every act. That is exactly why it needs people being ridiculous at each other. This file is the humor guide, the recurring dynamics, the running gags, one scripted funny scene per chapter (Ch0–Ch21), optional bits per kingdom, and the portrait-art list. Script format: [portrait: NAME, expression] then the line. Expressions: neutral, happy, smug, angry, crying, shocked, deadpan, panic, sad, blush. Emotes in (italics): sweat drop, shock lines, anger vein, "..." bubble, music note, heart, question mark. The PC's replies are choice prompts shown as > option. The PC has no portrait.

1. Humor guide

  • Where the comedy comes from: people who care about each other, losing patience. The PC is a fifteen-year-old who walks into Bleed zones, vaults and wars as a habit. Every adult who loves them reacts the way that Rejuvenation portrait does: crying and yelling.
  • Character-based, never quippy. A line is funny because of who says it. If the line could be handed to anyone, cut it.
  • The silent PC is a comedy engine. Characters argue with the PC's silence, answer questions nobody asked, and read whole speeches into a "...". The three choice options are often two sensible replies and one that is clearly a bad idea, and the bad idea gets the best reaction.
  • Absurdity lives in optional content: signs, NPCs, sidequests, shop names. The main path stays grounded.
  • Never undercut the big beats. No jokes inside: the Lowlight Shatter aftermath, Rowan's unmasking, the Thaw, the Fault choice, the Cathedral echo, Interlude IV, Valen's audience, the finale from the hijack onward. A joke may come before a blow (it makes the blow land) or a full scene after (it lets the player breathe). Never during.
  • Comedy tracks the tone curve. Act I: broad and warm. Act III onward: drier, shorter, gallows. By Act V a single dry line from Corvin is the whole joke.
  • Jokes can carry plot. Several running gags below are foreshadowing wearing a silly hat.

2. Comic dynamics

A. "The Lecture" — Larch (exasperated) and the PC (chaos, silently)

Larch has kept this child alive by post for six months and is discovering they treat every Bleed Veil like a doorway. She starts each scolding as a scientist and ends it as a parent. She cries when she's angriest and denies it ("It's the tea. The tea is very strong."). Brann and Corvin inherit the role when she's absent, in their own registers: Brann booms and then hugs; Corvin lectures in the form of a medical bill.

B. "The Committee" — Juno (earnest chaos), Kestrel (furious straight man), the PC (tie-breaker)

Juno narrates plant facts at dramatic moments and adopts things. Kestrel has rehearsed being cool and is ambushed by Juno's kindness every time. Their bickering is the party banter. Kestrel insists he isn't part of any group; the group has a name (Juno named it; see the gag).

C. "The Wardens' Group Chat" — Odessa (chaos), Ilsa (deadpan), Dorian (ham), Bastian (selectively deaf)

From Act III the Wardens share Gale Post relay messages the PC can read at any Pokémon Center ("Warden Circular"). Odessa posts nonsense. Dorian replies in stage directions. Ilsa answers thoughts nobody typed. Bastian replies to the wrong message a week late. Anja's only contribution, every time: "Toes."

3. Running gags (with payoffs)

# Gag How it runs Payoff
1 Larch's kelp tea Everyone who is handed a cup finds a new way to not drink it: Juno waters a plant with it (the plant dies), Kestrel "saves it for later" for nineteen chapters, Brann quenches a blade in it (the blade improves). Festival, Day 5: Valen, dying, drinks a whole cup and says, "That's rather good." Larch has to sit down. In Homecoming's epilogue the PC finally drinks one. > It's disgusting. Larch: "I know."
2 The Lecture count Each scolding is numbered by the scolder ("This is lecture nine."). Different adults keep separate, incompatible counts. Festival hijack aftermath / epilogue: Larch: "Lecture forty. You fought God. I had it written down as a thing not to do."
3 "Team Compost" Juno names the trio in Ch1. Kestrel refuses it in every act. It ends up on a Gale Post parcel, a pit-fight bracket, and a Directorate arrest warrant. Festival, SF (Redeemed): after he tears off the Gauntlet, the arena board lists his affiliation as he requested that morning: "TEAM COMPOST". He says nothing about it, ever.
4 Rook's chat Rook relays what his stream chat says. Chat is always wrong about battles and always right about feelings. Hijack: Rook cuts the grid; his stream dies. First line offline: "Chat? ...Oh. It's just me." Then, quieter: "Okay. Still charged."
5 Bastian's deafness He can't hear questions about fugitives, taxes or bedtime. He can hear a biscuit tin open through forty meters of rock. Arceus fight, his release line is followed by Pip: "Granddad, did you hear what it said?" Bastian: "Every word. I've heard every word for thirty years. Don't tell Hollis."
6 Understudy never gets the part The gift Eevee is cast in every Saltmere play and cut before curtain (as Tree #3, as "a bush", as the understudy to a prop). Dorian sends apology notes. Night of Masques: Understudy walks on as the infant Princess Maren and gets a standing ovation. If it has evolved, the program credits it as "a much older Princess Maren".
7 Corvin's bills After every reckless thing the PC does in his sight, he hands them an itemized invoice ("Item: one (1) heart attack, mine. Item: gauze."). The total is always ₽0. Epilogue: the last invoice arrives by post. "Item: one (1) world, saved. Paid in full."
8 The mirror that waves late (dark) From Ch12 the PC's reflection lags. Optional mirror interactions are jokes: it's caught yawning, it fixes its hair, it mimes "behind you" when nothing is there. Juno finds it hilarious. Ruin Route, R1: the Reflection: "You laughed at me in Skyreach. I was trying to warn you." No joke after that is safe in a mirror.

4. One scripted scene per chapter

Ch0 (Prologue) — "Do Not Lean"

The ferry rail. A sign: DO NOT LEAN ON RAIL. The PC is leaning on it.

[portrait: EDDA, angry] Oi! Sprog! Can you read? *(anger vein)*
> Yes.
[portrait: EDDA, deadpan] Then you're doing it on purpose. That's worse.
> No.
[portrait: EDDA, shocked] ...Oh. Oh, stars. Right. That's a D. That one's an O. *(sweat drop)*
> ...
[portrait: EDDA, deadpan] Strong silent type. Wonderful. I'll just talk to the rail, shall I? Rail! Tell the sprog to get off you!
[portrait: VOSS, happy] I believe the rail has no objection, Captain.
[portrait: EDDA, angry] Nobody asked the passengers in the nice coats!

Ch1 — "Lecture One" (after the Glassed Orchard)

[portrait: LARCH, neutral] So. You walked through a Bleed Veil.
[portrait: LARCH, neutral] Into a Bleed zone. On your first day. To fetch a Skwovet.
> It was stuck.
[portrait: LARCH, angry] IT LIVES IN A TREE. IT IS ALWAYS STUCK. THAT IS ITS ENTIRE ARRANGEMENT WITH TREES. *(anger vein)*
[portrait: JUNO, happy] Technically Skwovet are scansorial, so—
[portrait: LARCH, angry] Juno.
[portrait: JUNO, panic] Shutting up! *(sweat drop)*
[portrait: LARCH, crying] Your mother wrote me one instruction. One! "Keep them out of trouble, Imelda." It has been NINE HOURS.
[portrait: LARCH, crying] I'm not crying. It's the tea. The tea is very strong.
[portrait: JUNO, happy] We should have a team name! For next time!
[portrait: LARCH, shocked] Next— *(shock lines)*
[portrait: JUNO, happy] Team Compost! Because we break things down and make them better!
[portrait: KESTREL, deadpan] *(from the loft)* I'm not in it.
[portrait: JUNO, happy] He's in it.

Ch19 — "Weighed" (Sigrun, the night before the oath; the only joke in the chapter)

[portrait: SIGRUN, neutral] Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed.
*(Her Salamence sniffs the PC, then sits on them.)*
[portrait: SIGRUN, deadpan] He's weighing you.
> Help.
[portrait: SIGRUN, deadpan] It's a thorough process.
> ...
[portrait: SIGRUN, smug] He says you're light. He says "eat something". He's never said that about anyone. *(a beat)* Neither have I. Eat something.

Ch20 — "The Good Tea" (Day 5, before the audience; running gag 1 pays off)

[portrait: LARCH, neutral] Sovereign. I brought— it's nothing. It's tea.
[portrait: BRANN, panic] *(mouthing)* Don't. *(sweat drop)*
[portrait: VALEN, neutral] *(drinks the whole cup)*
[portrait: VALEN, happy] That's rather good.
[portrait: LARCH, shocked] *(shock lines)* ...
[portrait: LARCH, crying] Thirty years. THIRTY YEARS I have made that tea.
[portrait: BRANN, crying] He's dying, Imelda, his tongue's gone—
[portrait: VALEN, deadpan] My tongue is fine, Brann. It tastes like the sea. I was born by the sea.
[portrait: LARCH, crying] I'm not crying. It's the—
[portrait: VALEN, happy] The tea. Yes. It's very strong.

Ch21 — "Lecture Forty" (epilogue only; nothing from the hijack to the ending)

[portrait: LARCH, neutral] Lecture forty.
[portrait: LARCH, neutral] You fought God.
[portrait: LARCH, angry] I had it WRITTEN DOWN. "Things not to do." It was between "lick a Lattice" and "trust Odessa's rope". *(anger vein)*
[portrait: LARCH, crying] Your mother asked me for one thing—
> You kept me out of trouble.
[portrait: LARCH, shocked] I absolutely did not. *(shock lines)*
> You kept me.
[portrait: LARCH, crying] ...
[portrait: LARCH, crying] Drink your tea.
*(The PC drinks.)*
> It's disgusting.
[portrait: LARCH, happy] I *know*.

5. Optional funny bits per kingdom

  • Elowen:
  • A Briarwick weaver has knitted a jumper for a Caterpie. It has sixteen sleeves. She's on the fourth.
  • Sidequest "Gerald" (Royal Chase): the Queen's named Scatterbug keep escaping. Each has a personality file.
  • Saltmere's rival theater stages "The Pageant of the Pageant", a play about Dorian. Dorian has seen it nine times "to gather evidence".
  • Isenmar:
  • A Rimeholt ice-fisher has been waiting at one hole for eleven years. His Bergmite is now an Avalugg and he hasn't noticed he's sitting on it.
  • Ebbmouth's eel-stall owner keeps a ledger titled "THE BOY (4 EELS, DAILY)".
  • Zenith:
  • A Lumen City vending machine has gained sentience from the free current and only sells Fizzbolt to people who compliment it.
  • Sidequest "Customer Support": a Meridian helpdesk Rotom needs the PC to walk a form through nine offices. The form is to cancel the form.
  • Nadir:
  • Mirefen's noodle cart rates customers out of ten. Nobody has scored above a four. Corvin has a two and comes daily.
  • An Ironreach pit announcer narrates everything the PC does in town ("AND THEY'RE OPENING THE DOOR, FOLKS").
  • Ashkaar:
  • Because lying is a crime, Cindervault shop signs are painfully honest: "ADEQUATE SWORDS", "BREAD (YESTERDAY'S)".
  • A dragon-rider recruit is afraid of heights and has been assigned a Trapinch "until he's ready".
  • Ossaria:
  • Sahar's Dig Lab interns revived a fossil they can't identify and have been calling it "Kevin". Kevin is a rock. Kevin is in the staff photo.
  • A caravan trader will only barter in riddles and doesn't know the answers either.
  • Antumbra:
  • In the Inverse borderlands, an NPC complains his umbrella keeps the rain in.
  • A Lucent student's reflection attends lectures for her. It gets better marks. She's furious.
  • Mirror gag set (running gag 8): three optional mirrors where the PC's reflection misbehaves.
  • Crownhaven:
  • The Court of Eight Pavilions has a lost-property tent. Its only item, every day, is Kestrel's eel receipt.

Story Bible 11: Finale Lines — the Eighteen Locks

For the True Finale (08_ACT_V, Ch21): six bars of three locks, in gym order. Between bars, the three Wardens of that bar release or refuse. Conditions are WARDEN_CONSENT in NARRATIVE_KEYS §5. Every refusal has a reason the player caused. A refusal forces the next lock (Arceus +1 all stats for that bar). Staging: the Warden kneels at their pylon; one line; on release the golden lock opens and the Plate rises into Arceus's wheel; on refusal they grip the pylon and the lock stays shut. §3 gives the Homecoming, Unfinished slide for each refusing Warden's city.

1. Release lines

# Warden (Plate) Release line
1 Tamsin (Insect) "Granny wove a whole village around this. I think she'd want me to finish the pattern. Cut the thread."
2 Dorian (Blank) "Three hundred years of pageants, and this is the only true scene I ever staged. Exit, pursued by nothing. Lights down."
3 Marisol (Splash) "The Ebb ran both ways because of this. It'll run one way now, like any honest river. Go on, tides take you."
4 Rook (Zap) "First time I've ever held it. Kinda thought it'd be heavier. ...Stay charged, wherever you're going."
5 Sahar (Earth) "The desert keeps everything. I'm asking it, just this once, to give something back."
6 Corvin (Toxic) "Dose makes the cure. Three hundred years is rather over the dose. Off you go, dear."
7 Brann (Flame) "Forge it to break right, lad. Forge it to break right." (He's weeping.)
8 Kaito (Fist) "A fist is a promise. So is an open hand." (He opens it.)
9 Juno (Meadow) "Verdance has never had an autumn. I'd like to see one." (She is always the first to let go in her bar.)
10 Anja (Icicle) "We'll move south. We've hauled heavier things than a city. Take it before I count my grandchildren's toes again."
11 Odessa (Sky) "The wind doesn't care who owns it. Never did. Fly home."
12 Ilsa (Mind) "I've heard everyone's thoughts but yours. I'd like the quiet. Go." (If reconciled, she says it with one hand still on her father's shoulder.)
13 Bastian (Stone) "First time in three hundred years a Warden's had nothing in his keeping. Feels like setting down a sack of stone. Go on, then."
14 Ysolde (Spooky) "Go home. All of you. Go home." (The echoes in the stands fade as she says it. If Kestrel is beside her, he puts his hand over hers on the pylon.)
15 Magnus (Iron) "Tolerances exceeded. I'm releasing the part." (A beat.) "That was a joke. I'm told I should make them."
16 Nyx (Dread) "My family kept one secret for three hundred years. I'm keeping none. It's yours, sweetheart. Take the dark with you."
17 Celestine (Pixie) (sung) "Not chained, not broken, not bought, not crowned: given back, as a gift is found."
18 Sigrun (Draco) "Dragons don't let anything through they haven't weighed. I've weighed this. It's too heavy for anyone. Go."

Forced-lock beat: when a refused lock is broken by force, the Warden is thrown back from the pylon and the Plate tears free with a crack of Bleed light. No line. The camera holds on their empty hands.

CAMEOS — Sister-Game Sidequests (Story lead; SPINE Revision 4)

The Reborn / Rejuvenation / Desolation cameos are optional sidequests, the way Rejuvenation features Reborn's Aya and Adrienn: a small quest somewhere in the world, usually ending in one tough optional fight. They are never in the Festival, never required, never in the finale. The Festival uses original entrants (03_CAST "Festival Outsiders"; 10_CHOICES_ENDINGS §3). The old Festival default draw in earlier drafts of this file is obsolete. Trainer type IDs keep the SPINE cameo convention FESTIVAL_<NAME>. Versions: 1 = the sidequest fight, 2 = post-game rematch at level 100 (unless noted). SPINE difficulty offsets (+100 Hard, +200 Casual) apply. Facts marked ✔ were checked against web sources (bottom). The rest is our own invention, in the spirit of the source game. Rulings: Lin's Togekiss keeps Oblivion Wing; Amaria's Lapras is "Deliverer".

Why they're in Velmora (in-universe)

Antumbra, the Kingdom Behind the Glass, is the reverse side of the world. Its Glass faces outward too, onto other worlds that already brush against each other: - ✔ Desolation's Shiv appears in Reborn's post-game. - ✔ Reborn's Adrienn and Aya help rebuild Aevium.

The Festival once invited champions through Antumbra's Veil Gate, but the Synod sealed it in AF 230. Since the Bleed accelerated (AF 289) and above all since the Fault (end of Act III), the Glass has been leaking. Travelers from Reborn, Aevium and Ayrith slip through mirrors, puddles, sewer-grates and dreams, and wake up somewhere in Velmora. Each one needs a way home, and the PC, who can walk through Bleed Veils, is the one who can find the right mirror. Rule of the Glass: a traveler can bring only what is theirs. Anything bound to their home world's gods stays behind.

Placement overview (one or two per act before post-game)

# Cameo (source) Act / Ch Kingdom, area Unlock Fight (lv) ID / version
1 Shelly (Reborn) I / Ch2 Elowen, Gossamer Deepwood after Gym 1 double, ~17 FESTIVAL_SHELLY 1 (2 = PG)
2 Venam (Rejuvenation) II / Ch7 Nadir, Miasma Caverns under Mirefen after Gym 6 and J2 ~38 FESTIVAL_VENAM 1 (2)
3 Garret (Desolation) II / Ch9 Nadir, Ironreach's lower shafts after the Conclave (Gym 8) ~46 FESTIVAL_GARRET 1 (2)
4 Melia (Rejuvenation) III / Ch10 Elowen, the Royal Chase after J3 (Gym 9) ~50 FESTIVAL_MELIA 1 (2)
5 Alexandra (Rejuvenation) III / Ch11 Isenmar, Rimeholt's sunken lower town after the Thaw and Gym 10 ~56 FESTIVAL_ALEXANDRA 1 (2)
6 Amaria + Titania (Reborn) IV / Ch17 Nadir, drowned Aurelia's lower galleries after Gym 16, Dive double, ~78 FESTIVAL_AMARIA 1, FESTIVAL_TITANIA 1 (2)
7 Shiv (Desolation) V / Ch18 Elowen, Gloaming's dusk-chapels after Gym 17 ~84 FESTIVAL_SHIV 1 (2)
8 Lin (Reborn) Post-game Ferrow's Landing lighthouse, then the Antumbra Distortion Core antechamber any ending with a post-game superboss, 100 FESTIVAL_LIN 1
9 Nova (Desolation) Ruin Route only Mirrorwalk dream-road (R5) Ruin Route ~90 FESTIVAL_NOVA 1

1. "Lost in the Gossamer" — Shelly (Reborn)

  • Source ✔: Reborn's pink-haired Bug Gym Leader, who loves throwing bugs at you in double battles. ✔ In Reborn's post-game she becomes Champion, and insists she isn't the region's Professor "until her credentials arrive next year".
  • Where/when: Act I, Ch2, Gossamer Deepwood (:SILKWOOD), after Gym 1. It's the first cameo and teaches players that the Glass leaks.
  • Premise:
  • Silk-moth weavers report "a girl who keeps apologizing to the Bugs" deep in the wood.
  • Shelly fell through a dew-pond's reflection and has spent three days lost, because the Silkwood keeps re-weaving the paths.
  • She's terrified of crowds, so Briarwick's busy looms scare her more than the wood does.
  • The PC finds her, walks her through a small Bleed Veil to the dew-pond she came through, and helps her calm a Bleed-glassed Masquerain guarding it.
  • Before she steps back through, she asks for "one practice battle, so I'm not rusty when I get home".
  • Fight: double battle, lv ~17, on :SILKWOOD. Bug core with a partner, so it's tough for its placement.
  • Reward: Silver Powder and a TM (Struggle Bug). Lore: "Your forest sounds like home used to, before the city got sick."
  • Post-game (v2): she comes back through the same pond, at lv 100 in doubles.

2. "Sewer Royalty" — Venam (Rejuvenation)

  • Source ✔: Veronica "Venam" Vasile, 17, a Punk Girl and Aevium's Poison Gym Leader (Poison Heart Badge).
  • ✔ She leads East Gearen's teen trainers out of the Abandoned Sewers.
  • ✔ Ace: Seviper, and ✔ her signature move is Venam's Kiss (a Poison move super-effective against Steel).
  • ✔ She was petrified and restored by the Spring of Rejuvenation.
  • Where/when: Act II, Ch7, the Miasma Caverns runoff tunnels under Mirefen (:CORROSIVE_MIRE), after Gym 6 and J2.
  • Premise:
  • Wenlow mentions "a loud girl with a snake" helping drag Sweetrot addicts to the Infirmary from the deep tunnels. She fell through a sewer-grate's reflection.
  • Venam loves Nadir within five minutes ("better graffiti than mine, worse air") and hates the Sweet Market vendors on sight.
  • The quest is a tunnel crawl with her as a temporary partner: a partner battle against Mother Gutter's vendors.
  • At the Quiet Ward, she sits with the half-Fixed patients and goes quiet. Then she tells Corvin and the PC about being stone: "I could hear everything. Everyone who sat with me." Corvin asks if he can record it.
  • Fight: the farewell fight at the grate she came through, lv ~38, on :CORROSIVE_MIRE. Ace Seviper with a custom Venam's Kiss (if the move team adds it; otherwise a strong Poison move).
  • Reward: Venam's Testimony (Key Item, Corvin's recording).
  • It can be played for Juno at the Bride Tree (Ch10) or in Ch20.
  • It is one of the three proofs for Envoy Okoro (:ch20_envoy).
  • Also Black Sludge.
  • Post-game (v2): lv 100.

3. "Two Lucarios" — Garret (Desolation)

  • Source ✔: Ayrith's Fighting Gym Leader (Cellia South). Gruff, blunt and loyal, and ✔ searching for his kidnapped sister Eden. ✔ Mega Lucario.
  • Where/when: Act II, Ch9, Ironreach's lower shafts below the Iron Pit, after the Conclave.
  • Premise:
  • During the Ironreach Open's undercard, a stranger with a Lucario asks every pit-fighter about a girl named Eden. He walked out of a puddle in the shafts during the Conclave's Bleed burst.
  • Kaito recognizes something in him and lends him a cot.
  • The PC helps him search the old Aurel shafts, where his Lucario senses people sleeping behind the walls. This is a tiny foreshadow of the Quiet Towns and Marrowgate.
  • She isn't here. He wants to fight the PC "to see if this world's worth searching".
  • Fight: lv ~46 on :COLOSSEUM, with Mega Lucario (if Megas are enabled; otherwise Lucario with an item build). Kaito watches.
  • Reward: Lucarionite if Megas are enabled, otherwise a Riolu egg. Also Black Belt. Lore: "Talk less. Fight more. Then we'll talk."
  • Post-game (v2): lv 100, plus the Marrowgate rumor: a girl matching Eden's description seen near the Quiet Towns. It's a deliberate dead end and a kindness. We never resolve another game's plot.

4. "What Are You For?" — Melia (Rejuvenation)

  • Source ✔: The heart of Rejuvenation. Adoptive daughter of Professor Jenner and a reserve Normal Gym Leader. ✔ Hapi (a Togekiss raised from her treasured Togepi), Zoroark, Mimikyu, Snorlax. ✔ She is Venam's close friend and ex.
  • Where/when: Act III, Ch10, the Royal Chase (Safari-style reserve) outside Verdance, after J3.
  • Premise:
  • A blonde girl with a Togekiss is searching the reserve for "a friend who fell through a sewer". If the Venam quest is done, the PC can tell her Venam got home safely.
  • The Queen's huntsmen think she's a poacher. The PC vouches for her.
  • She watched Juno's confirmation from the crowd and asks the PC the question nobody else does: "Everyone keeps telling you what you're for. Have you decided yet?"
  • Her way home is the reflection in the Chase's royal fishpond.
  • Fight: lv ~50, with Hapi (Togekiss) as ace.
  • Reward: a Togepi egg and a Soothe Bell.
  • Bonus if Venam's quest is done: a Gale Post letter from Venam, routed through the Glass ("u owe me a rematch, null kid").
  • Post-game (v2): lv 100.

5. "Crystal Sleep" — Alexandra (Rejuvenation)

  • Source ✔: Elite 8 member and Acting Champion of Aevium. ✔ Her known Pokémon lean Dragon/Psychic: Latias, Dragonite, Altaria, Starmie, Lapras, Sigilyph, Garchomp. ✔ Her mother Celine, Aevium's Champion, sleeps encased in crystal, recovering from wounds inflicted by Madame X.
  • Where/when: Act III, Ch11, Rimeholt's sunken lower town after the Thaw and Gym 10.
  • Premise:
  • Among the half-Fixed fishers on the broken ice sits a tall woman with a Latias, asking each one whether they can hear her. She came through the Thaw's black water.
  • Velmora's Fixing looks horribly like her mother's crystal. She has questions for Anja (blunt) and Corvin (by Gale Post).
  • The PC helps her move three Fixing fishers to the heat-tower shelters before the next freeze. She is quietly devastated by how many there are.
  • She leaves through a frozen mirror in the Thane's hall: "My mother has been asleep in crystal for a long time. I'd like to know if yours wake up."
  • Fight: lv ~56, on a Dragon/Psychic champion team. Ace: Latias.
  • Reward: a Draco Meteor tutor and Celine's Notes (lore Key Item: an otherworld physician's notes on crystal stasis). Field Notes then adds a line: "the sleepers may be dreaming".
  • Post-game (v2): lv 100.

6. "Deliverer" — Amaria and Titania (Reborn)

  • Source ✔: Amaria, Reborn's late-game Water Gym Leader, famous for her nicknamed team, including Lapras "Deliverer" (ace, per ruling), Starmie "Twinkle", Ludicolo "Indiana" and Kingdra "Atlantica". ✔ Her gym is an underwater maze. Titania, Reborn's Steel Gym Leader: ✔ her battle is on the Fairy Tale field in a sky castle, and her chapter is paired with Amaria's (badges 15–16). Her team concept is knightly (Aegislash, Mawile).
  • Where/when: Act IV, Ch17, drowned Aurelia's lower galleries (Seabed / Aurelia maps, Dive), after Gym 16, before the PC leaves Underveil.
  • Premise:
  • Edda's divers report a woman swimming without a suit in the drowned galleries, singing to a Lapras, and an armored knight pacing the drained shallows above who refuses to leave.
  • Amaria followed "a city under a city" through the Glass. Drowned places call to her, and Aurelia is calling hard.
  • Titania followed Amaria.
  • The PC dives a short underwater maze to reach Amaria, who is sitting in a flooded Gilder hall. She says the drowned city "isn't dead, it's waiting". This is a Ruin Route hint about Theodric's reflection.
  • Titania won't let Amaria go home until "someone who can walk through walls" proves the way back is safe.
  • Fight: double battle vs Amaria and Titania, lv ~78, on :WATER_SURFACE (Amaria's side; Titania's Steel suffers). Aces: Lapras "Deliverer" and Aegislash.
  • Reward: a Liquidation TM, an Assault Vest from Titania ("a knight's spare"), and a Lapras of the PC's own: Deliverer's calf. "She'll carry you out of a flood someday." This echoes Valen's Lapras.
  • Post-game (v2): both at lv 100.

7. "Dreamwalker" — Shiv (Desolation)

  • Source ✔: The Champion of Ayrith. Mysterious and initially shady, ✔ later revealed to be working under Darkrai. ✔ Signature Aipom → Ambipom with absurd HP. ✔ He's a trickster, and ✔ he has already crossed worlds, as "Dreamer Shiv" in Reborn's post-game.
  • Where/when: Act V, Ch18, Gloaming's dusk-chapels, after Gym 17.
  • Premise:
  • Gloaming's townsfolk keep dreaming the same grinning stranger. He walks dreams, and Gloaming's endless dusk makes the dream-road easy.
  • He greets the PC: "Met you already. You were standing on a ferry, glowing like a lighthouse."
  • The quest is three dream-errands for the townsfolk: return a lost name, finish a dead man's sentence, find a child's Pokémon in a dream that isn't hers.
  • He finally admits he's lost: "Dreams leak through your Glass like water through a roof. I followed one too far."
  • Fight: lv ~84. Trickster tech (Fake Out, Trick, Taunt, odd abilities). Ace: Ambipom with absurd HP (custom stats, balance-approved).
  • Reward: a Dusk Stone and Dream Mist (Key Item: re-watch any completed interlude).
  • Ruin hint: only if the Ledger holds any game over: "You've done this before, haven't you. I can smell the other nights on you."
  • Post-game (v2): lv 100.

8. "the meteor champion" — Lin (Reborn) — post-game superboss

  • Source ✔: Lin (Lindsey Paraeah), Reborn's hidden Big Bad. She usurped Team Meteor and is fought as Reborn's Champion.
  • ✔ Signature: a Togekiss with Oblivion Wing, a move it can't legally learn (signature beats legality, per ruling).
  • ✔ She caught Arceus.
  • ✔ Cynical, blunt, mean-spirited, Jekyll-and-Hyde. Her honest lines are written in lowercase, as an homage.
  • Where/when: post-game, any ending with a post-game. Hesper Vane reports "a woman who talks without capital letters" haunting the Ferrow's Landing lighthouse at night. Found there; fought at the Distortion Core antechamber (Antumbra).
  • Premise:
  • She came through the Glass the night Arceus came down, because she felt it from her world. Per the Rule of the Glass, her own Arceus wouldn't follow.
  • Her lines depend on the ending:
    • Homecoming: "you gave it back. good. i couldn't."
    • Long Vigil: "you kept it. i kept mine too. look at us."
    • Concord Dawn: "it's quiet, isn't it. for a while."
  • With any game over in the Ledger: "you've done this before, haven't you."
  • Fight: superboss, lv 100, the hardest optional fight in the game. Ace: Togekiss (Oblivion Wing). The rest are Reborn-endgame heavy hitters, chosen by balance (no Arceus).
  • Reward: the Ledger Lens (Key Item: turns Ledger hints on for this save, the in-game equivalent of the 'ledgerhints' password), plus a lowercase letter left in the lighthouse: "see you in the next one."

9. Nova (Desolation) — Ruin Route only

  • Source ✔: Leader of Team Crescent. An anti-villain who wants a dream world where everyone is happy, manipulative but sincere. ✔ Signature: Gothitelle.
  • Where/when: Ruin Route, R5, on the Mirrorwalk dream-road between Gloaming and Wyrmrest (story/12_RUIN_ROUTE.md).
  • Premise: drawn to a PC whose Resonance has locked at Ruin, she offers a gentler unmaking: everyone asleep and happy forever. Accepting is GO_R_SWEET_DREAMS. Refusing leads to a fight.
  • Fight: lv ~90. Ace: Gothitelle.
  • Reward: the Crescent Charm (Ruin Key Item; Nova's last kindness). In R7 it lets the Reflection speak one extra line.
